# Gymdesk

Multi-tenant Gym Management SaaS. Express backend + Next.js frontend + MySQL 8 (Oracle HeatWave) + Clerk auth.

## Before implementing any feature

For any issue that involves new API surface, DB schema changes, or significant UI work, write a plan first and get approval before coding. A plan should cover: migrations, API endpoints, frontend sections, tests, and doc updates. Keep it concise — bullet points per layer, not prose.

**Issue type `Task` is the marker for work that needs no plan.** A GitHub issue whose type is set to **Task** is a small, self-contained fix or change that needs no new API surface, no migration and no significant UI, so the plan-first rule above does not apply to it. Whoever writes the issue (Oscar, usually) sets the type by hand; that is the whole opt-in. Tasks are consumed only by the `gymdesk-tasks` routine (`skills/routines/gymdesk-tasks.md`); issues of type **Feature** are consumed only by `gymdesk-features` (`skills/routines/gymdesk-features.md`); every other issue (untyped, or any other type such as Bug) is the `gymdesk-issues` routine's, which searches with `-type:Task -type:Feature`. If a Task turns out to need any of those after all, it is not implemented: comment and retype it as a normal issue. Do not mark such work with a label or a `fix:` title prefix — the type is the one signal both routines filter on.

Read these files first — they contain the full context needed to implement correctly:

- `docs/architecture.md` — starts with a TL;DR; full codebase structure, auth, roles, DB conventions below it
- `docs/feature-patterns.md` — step-by-step checklist and code templates for new features
- `docs/roadmap.md` — what each area of the product does today, the open issues and what is left on each, the settled decisions and the shipped phases. Short, by design: it is rewritten, never appended to
- `docs/changelog.md` — one entry per shipped ticket, by month; the record of how the product got here. The only doc a ticket appends to
- `docs/rules.md` — the business rules below stated for a person: one plain entry per rule with its ticket and the module that enforces it. A view of the Hard constraints, kept in step by the Finishing-a-task checklist; read it to learn what the product does, read the constraint below it to implement against it
- `docs/decisions.md` — settled architectural choices (MySQL, Clerk, no ORM, etc.) — don't re-litigate these
- `docs/payments.md` — **required reading for anything that touches money**: the first-payment (CIT) and recurring-charge (MIT) processes end to end, the data model with its CHECK sets, the provider boundary, and the manual test runbook. The constraints below are the rules; that document is how the two processes actually run.
- `docs/mobile-app.md` — **required reading for anything that touches the iOS/Android app** (Capacitor shell, native sign-in, push tokens, universal links): the plan, what the 2026-10-04 spike proved, and the design rules that keep per-gym apps (stage 2) a configuration of the generic app (stage 1) rather than a fork. Planned, not built yet.

Use **Members** as the reference implementation for staff-level CRUD with soft-delete.
Use **Plans** (`api/src/api/membership-plans.ts` + `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/plans/`) as the reference implementation for an admin-only CRUD API and its Inline row CRUD frontend (expandable rows, no modals — see `docs/feature-patterns.md`). For the Modal CRUD frontend shape, see Class Types.

Use `/plan` to generate a structured implementation plan before coding. Use the `db-reviewer` agent to check migration files. Use the `test-writer` agent to generate test files.

## Parallel agents (worktree workflow)

Multiple Claude Code agents may work simultaneously, each in its own worktree and feature branch.

### Ground rules

- **One agent per worktree.** Never modify files in another agent's worktree directory.
- **One feature branch per task.** Always branch from `main`; never branch from another feature branch.
- **No destructive Git commands** (`git reset --hard`, `git clean -fd`, `git checkout -- .`, `git restore .`, `git branch -D`) unless the user explicitly requests them. Never discard changes to make a merge succeed.

### Required sync before feature completion

Before a feature is considered complete, the agent must integrate the latest `origin/main` into its branch:

```bash
git fetch origin
git merge origin/main   # always merge, never rebase
```

If conflicts occur:
- Resolve them carefully — preserve valid changes from **both** sides.
- Never blindly choose "ours" or "theirs".
- Pay particular attention to `docs/changelog.md` and `docs/roadmap.md` (see below).
- Run tests after resolving conflicts.
- Confirm the working tree is clean before marking the feature done.

Use `/finish-feature` to run through the full sync + test + status checklist automatically.

### Special handling of docs/changelog.md and docs/roadmap.md

Both are shared files modified by multiple parallel agents, and they answer two different questions (split on 2026-10-09, after the application-status review found the roadmap's Status section a month stale).

`docs/changelog.md` is the **record**: one `- **Done**: #N (…)` entry per shipped ticket (one per stage for a staged ticket), under the month heading of the day it merges, newest month first. A ticket appends its entry once and never edits it afterwards; an entry is never written as *In progress* — the PR that merges the work is the one that adds it, so by the time it exists the work is done.

- Add your entry at the **top** of the current month's section; create the month heading if it is the first entry of the month.
- Do not reformat, reorder or remove another agent's entries.
- When resolving merge conflicts, keep both sides' entries — merge the section manually rather than picking one side.
- Always sync with `origin/main` before the final commit if it was modified.

`docs/roadmap.md` is the **state**: a Status table with one row per area and an *Open work* list naming every open issue with what has merged and what is left. It is rewritten in place, never appended to, and a ticket touches it only when it changes the state of an area: closing an open issue removes that issue's bullet and updates the row (and the open-issue count in the header); opening a new stage of an open issue updates the bullet; a ticket that closes nothing open leaves the file alone. Do not add a per-ticket paragraph to it — that is the changelog's — and do not leave an item reading *in progress* after its issue closes.

## Hard constraints

- No microservices, no event sourcing, no AI/LLM integrations.
- One database: MySQL 8 (HeatWave when deployed). No additional stores without a concrete reason.
- Every domain table must have `gym_id`. Every query must filter by it. A **shared platform catalogue** — `nutrition_library_items`, `exercises`, `themes`, and since #947 `personal_goals` and `nutrition_goals` — is the one other shape: `gym_id` is nullable with the FK still `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `NULL` means a System row owned by the platform and administered from Cordel, non-NULL means that gym's own, and a gym-facing read takes `(gym_id IS NULL OR gym_id = ?)` while every write still checks the row's own `gym_id`. The global lookup tables (`benefit_types`, `charge_types`, `action_types`, `muscles`, `result_types`) have no `gym_id` at all.
- A `weekday` column means **0=Sunday … 6=Saturday** (`gym_operating_hours`, `activity_type_schedule_rules`, `trainer_availability`, `training_plans`). A column storing luxon's ISO numbering (1=Monday … 7=Sunday) must be named `iso_weekday` instead, so the two bases can never be joined by accident — see `member_recurring_slots` (migration 169).
- A new `member_notifications.type` goes in **two** places: the `NotificationType` union in `api/src/infra/notifications.ts` **and** the `chk_member_notifications_type` CHECK (current definition: migration 226). Adding only the first makes every insert of that type fail invisibly — `sendNotification()` is fire-and-forget and only logs. The CHECK may be a **superset** of the union, never a subset: migration 087's `booking_reminder_24h` and `booking_reminder_1h` have never been written by anything and stay that way, retired by disuse rather than deleted.
- **A member's own booking raises no alert, and the one booking alert the platform raises on its own is the 2-hour training reminder** (#1113, migration 226). §4's distinction is *who acted*, decided where the notification is **generated** and never by hiding a row in the Alerts page: `POST /me/bookings` raises nothing at all (the response is the member's confirmation, so a persistent `booking_confirmed` / `waitlist_joined` told them what they had just done), and `DELETE /me/bookings/:id` already behaved that way — it alerts only the member it **promotes** off the waiting list, which is not that member's own action. Every staff-side alert stays, because somebody else acting *is* news: `POST /bookings` with `waitlist: true`, the staff `DELETE`'s `waitlist_removed`, #979's `event_reactivated` and #980's `waitlist_closed` are untouched. The reminder is `booking_reminder_2h`, a **third** reminder value rather than a reuse of the two above (their copy says *tomorrow* and *starting soon*, so writing a two-hour reminder under either would word the alert as something else), and `api/src/domain/bookingReminders.ts` is the one place that decides what a reminder is — the type, the lead time, who is owed one, what the payload says — with `api/src/api/booking-reminders.ts` as the I/O half. Four of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Nothing stores that a reminder was sent**: the dedupe is the `member_notifications` row itself (#647 stage 4's device, and why the insert is `recordNotifications()` rather than fire-and-forget), so §5's at-most-once and §6's "the live booking, not the replaced one" both fall out of the query — a `reminder_sent_at` column would be a second record of one fact, and a pass that wrote one but lost the other would either silence a member for good or alert them on every pass. **The window is "within the lead time", not "exactly at it"** (`starts_at > UTC_TIMESTAMP()` and `<= UTC_TIMESTAMP() + INTERVAL 120 MINUTE`, compared in SQL so no DATETIME crosses a timezone conversion), because a scheduled runner fires late and asking for 115–120 minutes would silently drop every member whose class fell between two passes: a late pass must cost punctuality, never the alert. **The lead time is a declared constant rather than configuration**, since the member reads *in 2 hours* in their own language — the copy and the number change together, and "all config via environment variables" is about deployment facts, not a figure the product says out loud. And **the run claims no run-log slot**: #780's guard is one *completed* run per UTC date and this is the one scheduled run that is not nightly (`.github/workflows/booking-reminder-run.yml`, every 15 minutes, with `concurrency` so two passes cannot overlap), so a date-keyed guard would silence every pass but the first of the day; a repeated pass is safe because the written row is what removes a booking from the candidate set. It carries its own `BOOKING_REMINDERS_INTERNAL_SECRET` (a job with a workflow of its own gets a secret with it), records no audit row and checks no feature flag — a reminder is about a booking that already exists, not a capability a gym opts into. A waitlisted member is owed nothing, because a place in a queue is not a training. Do not add a stored `reminder_sent_at` or a second record of what was reminded, a second place that decides who is owed a reminder, a run-log claim or a nightly schedule for it, a lead time read from the environment, an alert raised by a member's own action, or a copy of the reminder's sentence outside the Members App's locale keys.
- **A member cancels their own booking while the event is 24 hours away or the booking is under 2 hours old, and one place decides it** (#1162). `api/src/domain/bookingCancellation.ts` is that place (`decideMemberCancellation()`, `CANCELLATION_NOTICE_HOURS = 24`, `CANCELLATION_GRACE_HOURS = 2`) and `api/src/api/booking-cancellation.ts` the SQL half: the two inputs are **seconds**, `TIMESTAMPDIFF` against `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` from `calendar_events.starts_at` and `calendar_event_bookings.booked_at`, so no DATETIME crosses a timezone and the numbers the reads report `can_cancel` from are the numbers `DELETE /me/bookings/:id` refuses on (`409 cancellation_window_closed`, carrying `professional_service`; an event already started stays the `400`). Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **`booked_at` is the one booking-creation timestamp** (the ticket's `booked_on`): written by the INSERT of a `booked` row and by a waitlist promotion — the moment the member gained a booking — never updated otherwise, reported as `booked_on` by `GET /me/schedule`, `GET /me/bookings` and the staff `GET /bookings`, and shown as *Booked on* in both apps from that one value; no second column, no derived creation time. **A waiting-list place is not a booking**: leaving one is never inside the window, because a `waitlisted` row is nothing the gym plans around (and carries no `booked_at`); the one limit it shares is the event starting. **A legacy row with no `booked_at` gets no grace period** rather than an invented one. And **the two durations are declared constants, not configuration**, since the member reads *24 hours* in their own language. There was no 24-hour rule before this ticket — a member could cancel until the event started — so the notice window is this ticket's, with the grace period beside it, exactly as its §2 states the rule. On the Members App `apps/member/src/lib/bookingCancellation.ts` decides nothing about eligibility — it picks the locale **key** for the explanation (`cancellationNoticeKey()`: the Professional-Service wording when the API's flag says the occurrence delivers or requires one, #647/#973/#980) and the three screens that offer Cancel (My Bookings, the Calendar sheet, the dashboard card) draw it **where the action was** (§5), never a disabled button with no explanation. Do not add a second place that decides cancellability, a client-side date comparison, a stored `booked_on` beside `booked_at`, a grace period for a waiting-list place, or a lead time read from the environment.
- A **push** is a courtesy copy of a `member_notifications` row and never a condition of it (#1072, mobile app WP1, migration 221). All three writers (`sendNotification`, `sendBulkNotification`, `recordNotifications`) hand the alert to `deliverPushNotifications()` (`api/src/infra/push.ts`, over the pure `api/src/domain/pushDelivery.ts`) **after** the insert and never await it, because the row is what the Members App reads: a deployment with no credentials (which does not even query the table), an unreachable Firebase or a member with no device each cost the push and nothing else. A new `member_device_tokens.platform` therefore goes in **two** places — `DEVICE_PLATFORMS` in `api/src/domain/deviceTokens.ts` and `chk_mdt_platform` — and `parseDeviceRegistration()` is the one place a `POST /me/devices` body is judged (an unknown platform, a blank token or an over-wide value is a 400, never a coercion). Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. `UNIQUE (platform, token)` is **global, not per gym**, and the `POST` is an upsert that re-points `gym_id`/`member_id`: an FCM token identifies an app *installation*, so a shared handset belongs to whoever signed in last — a second row would push one member's alerts to a phone that is now someone else's, and the cross-tenant consequence of that choice is recorded in migration 221's header rather than mitigated by a narrower key. `app_id` exists from the first migration because credentials are resolved per app (`FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS`, one JSON object keyed by app id, raw or base64 — a stage-2 per-gym app is a new key, never a code change), and a token whose app has no entry is **skipped rather than failed**. A failed send deletes the token **only** where FCM says it can never work again (`UNREGISTERED`, `SENDER_ID_MISMATCH`): `INVALID_ARGUMENT` is kept, because FCM answers it for a malformed *message* too and deleting on it would empty the table on the first bug in the message builder, and a 429 or 5xx is an unknown outcome rather than evidence against the token (#785's `provider_error` judgment). And the message carries the payload's own `title` plus a `data` block for routing and **no sentence** — the per-type copy a member reads is the Members App's locale keys, so composing it server-side would be a second copy of it in a module that must pick a language. Do not add a second push path, an SDK (the whole protocol is a `node:crypto`-signed service-account JWT and one `fetch` per token; `firebase-admin` is deliberately not a dependency and the legacy server-key API is decommissioned), a notification type for push, or a stored copy of what was delivered.
- **The native shell declares no app identity, and an app profile is the one place one may be spelled** (#1074, mobile app WP3, `docs/mobile-app.md` design rule 1). `apps/mobile` is a Capacitor 8 workspace holding `ios/` and `android/` and loading the **deployed** Members App through `server.url` — there is no second front end and no web build step there, so `www/` is one local page. What a build is *for* is `apps/mobile/profiles/<id>.json` (stage 1 has exactly one, `cordel-fitness`), with each field overridden by its own `MOBILE_*` environment variable: `resolveAppProfile()` in `src/appProfile.ts` is the one place that `env ?? profile` resolution lives and it is **pure**, `src/loadAppProfile.ts` is the I/O half that finds the file, and both `capacitor.config.ts` and the scripts come through the second — the same two-module split WP2 used, for the same reason. Five of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **A missing identity is an error, never a default**: `appId`, `appName` and `serverUrl` have no fallback in code, because a shell with no Bundle ID installs over another app's identity and one with no URL opens a blank WebView — and the custom URL scheme defaults to the **app id**, never to a literal. **`npm run profile:apply` is the only writer of a native identity**: `cap add` seeds the Xcode project, `Info.plist`, Gradle and `strings.xml` once and `cap sync` then copies only web assets and plugins, so a profile changed without that step would ship the previous app's identity; the transforms are pure (`src/nativeProjectFiles.ts`, assertable with no macOS machine) and each **throws rather than reporting success** when its anchor is gone. **Google's URL scheme is derived, not configured** — the reversed client id — and a build with no client id registers none, which is WP2's "a native control that cannot work is absent" one layer down; Android's `namespace` is likewise **read** from the project rather than written, since it is the package the checked-in `MainActivity` lives under while `applicationId` is what identifies the app. **A URL opened on a running app reaches the *scene* delegate**, not the app delegate, because `Info.plist` declares a scene manifest — so `NativeSignInUrl.handle` is one rule asked from both, and anything a sign-in SDK does not consume goes to Capacitor, which is how a link becomes `appUrlOpen`. And **the token iOS registers must be the FCM token**, since delivery is FCM HTTP v1 (#1072) and FCM never delivers to an APNs token: the `AppDelegate` hands the APNs token to `Messaging` and posts Firebase's own token as Capacitor's `registration` value, behind `#if canImport(FirebaseMessaging)` so the project compiles before the one Xcode package step that needs a Mac — until that step is taken, iOS push cannot be delivered, which `docs/mobile-runbook.md` §2 and `docs/go-to-production.md` §6 both carry. The gate is `api/src/test/mobile-shell-profile.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only): no identity literal outside `profiles/` and the generated native projects, the committed projects matching the stage-1 profile, the shell's app id equal to the API's `DEFAULT_APP_ID` (a registration naming no `app_id` resolves FCM credentials under it), and every `@capacitor/*` / `@capgo/*` version equal to the Members App's — a web half and a native half of one plugin. Do not hard-code an app identity in the config, a script or a page, add a second profile resolution, write a native identity outside the apply script, add a plugin to one app and not the other, or fork the workspace for a per-gym app: stage 2 is a second profile file plus its own store plumbing and no code change.
- **An invitation link opens the app, and the grant is two halves that one declaration keeps in step** (#1076, mobile app WP4, `docs/mobile-app.md` WP4). A universal link works only when the domain publishes a file naming the app **and** the app declares the domain, so each half is one place. The web half is two route handlers in `apps/member` — `app/api/well-known/apple-app-site-association/route.ts` and `…/well-known/assetlinks/route.ts`, both `force-dynamic` because the documents are built from the environment per request — over `src/lib/appAssociations.ts` (**pure**: what the files say, assertable with no server, no device and no store account) and `src/lib/wellKnownResponse.ts` (how they are served). The canonical `/.well-known/…` paths reach them through a `next.config.js` **rewrite and never a redirect**, which Apple refuses, and the middleware never runs on either — its matcher excludes anything containing a dot, which both paths do, and that is load-bearing rather than incidental: Apple and Google fetch these with no session and no `Accept-Language`, so Clerk's protection and the locale redirect must not see them. Five of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **What a build is published under is configuration in one variable keyed by app id**: `MOBILE_APP_ASSOCIATIONS` is a JSON object (or that JSON base64-encoded, for the quadlet `Environment=` lines) naming each app's `apple_team_id` and `android_sha256_cert_fingerprints`, which is `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS`' shape and its reason — a stage-2 per-gym app is a new key, never a code change and never a new variable name — and it is environment rather than a profile field because the Members App's image copies `apps/member` and `shared` only and cannot see `apps/mobile/profiles/`, and because a Team ID and a release certificate belong to the store account. Either half may be omitted, since an iOS-only release is a real state. **A malformed entry is dropped, reported and never published**, and one app's typo costs that app alone: these files are read by Apple's and Google's CDNs and cached for a day, so publishing a wrong `appID` or a SHA-1 where a SHA-256 belongs claims an association that silently cannot work — worse than claiming none — which is also why `normalizeCertFingerprint()` checks the length rather than trusting the separators, and why the parser is pure and the **route** is what logs. **A file with nothing to say is a `404`**, not a `200` carrying an empty `details` array: that reads as "configured, associates nothing" and is indistinguishable from a working file in a log, where a `404` is diagnosable — WP2's "a native control that cannot work is absent, never broken" one layer down. **The native half is written only by `npm run profile:apply`** (#1074's rule, unchanged): the `associated-domains` entitlement on iOS and `@string/app_link_host` for an `autoVerify` App Links `intent-filter` on Android, both on a host that is **derived** — `new URL(serverUrl).hostname`, because that deployment is what serves the association files, so a profile field beside it could only disagree with the domain the two platforms actually verify, and ticking *Associated Domains* in Xcode would carry one gym's host into every other profile's build. And **which paths open the app is one declaration spelled in two syntaxes**: `APP_LINK_PATH_SEGMENT` gives Apple's component (`/*/link`) and Android's `pathPattern` (`/.*/link`), the app claims the invitation path (`/{locale}/link?gym_id=…&__clerk_ticket=…`) and **not** the whole domain — a filter over `/*` would swallow the OAuth and Clerk redirects a sign-in bounces through, and a link the app takes at the wrong moment has no way back to the browser. `apps/mobile` cannot import that module (another workspace, and the manifest is XML), so `api/src/test/mobile-app-links.unit.test.ts` is the gate (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only) and fails the build when the committed manifest or entitlement stops matching it — a drift is otherwise invisible until one platform opens the app and the other the browser. There is **no second link rule**: WP2's `appUrlOpenPath()` already reads an `https` URL, which is what a universal link arrives as, and the custom URL scheme stays registered beside the new filter for a link a universal link misses (an in-app browser that does not trigger one, a build whose domain is not yet verified). With the variable unset both files 404, verification fails and a tapped invitation opens in the browser and completes there, which is the pre-ticket behaviour exactly. A Clerk invitation email links to **Clerk's own domain**, so universal links never fire for it; `/link` therefore offers *Open in the app* (the custom scheme, `NEXT_PUBLIC_MOBILE_APP_SCHEME`, decided in `lib/openInApp.ts`) **before** redeeming the single-use ticket. Do not serve either file from `public/`, add a redirect in front of one, hard-code a Team ID, a fingerprint or a host, add a second place that decides which paths open the app, widen the filter to the whole domain, or add a second link rule beside `appUrlOpenPath()`.
- **Test builds of the mobile apps are published by CI and listed in Cordel, and the bucket is the record** (#1077). `.github/workflows/mobile-build.yml` builds the Android debug APK and an ad-hoc signed iOS simulator app (`CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY=-`, never `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`, which drops the keychain entitlement and breaks Google Sign-In in the simulator) per environment (`dev` → the `cordel-fitness-dev` profile, `com.cordel.fitness.dev`; `pro` → `cordel-fitness`; the simulator build is a CI check and is **never published**) and, on every run but a pull request's, `.github/scripts/publish-mobile-build.sh` uploads the **Android** build: `cordel/mobile-builds/<app id>/<platform>/<file>` plus a `<file>.json` sidecar and prunes to the newest 20 per app and platform; **Cordel → Mobile builds** (`GET /platform/mobile-builds`, `…/:id/download`, superadmin-only, read-only) lists and serves them. `api/src/domain/mobileBuilds.ts` is the one place that decides what a build is, which sidecar fields are required and which build id a download may name (a key outside `<app>/<platform>/<file>`, a dot-segment or a sidecar is a 404), and `api/src/test/mobile-builds.unit.test.ts` fails the build if the script and the parser disagree. A build is numbered from `apps/mobile/package.json`'s version and the workflow's run number (`versionCode` / `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION`), never a literal. An iPhone gets a **link**, not a build: the page's *Open in TestFlight* button comes from `MOBILE_TESTFLIGHT_URL` (forwarded by `deploy.yml`, validated to a `https://testflight.apple.com/…` URL, a disabled button until set). Do not add a table for builds, an upload or delete route, a second writer, a public bucket link for a build, or a build published from a pull request. Do not publish an iOS build to that page or put a download on an iOS row.
- **Everything native in the Members App sits behind two modules, and a plain browser never executes native code** (#1073, mobile app WP2, `docs/mobile-app.md` design rule 3). `apps/member/src/lib/native.ts` is the **decision** half — pure, no React, no plugin import, no `t()` — and is the one place that answers `isNative()`, which platform the shell reports, what `POST /me/devices` is called with, which in-app path an `appUrlOpen` URL becomes, how Google is configured and where the ID token is in the plugin's result; `apps/member/src/lib/nativePlugins.ts` is the **access** half and is the only file in either app that may import `@capacitor/*` or `@capgo/*` (`api/src/test/members-app-native.unit.test.ts` fails the build if another one does — in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only), with every import **dynamic**, because a plugin evaluated at module scope runs during SSR and in every browser while `await import()` still ships the package — which it must, since a page loaded from a remote URL gets no `registerPlugin` from the injected bridge. Five of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Detection and the registered platform are one answer**: `isNative()` is true for exactly the `DEVICE_PLATFORMS` the API's `chk_mdt_platform` accepts, read from the bridge's own platform string, so a future Capacitor target reads as *not* native rather than registering a value the API would refuse on a call whose failure nobody sees. **A component that renders differently resolves it after mount** (`useIsNative()`, `false` on the server and on the first client render) — asking during render is a hydration mismatch, and React resolves those by discarding the client tree; a component that merely *acts* natively calls `isNative()` inside its own effect. **Safe areas are not behind `isNative()`**: `env(safe-area-inset-*)` is `0px` wherever there is no inset, so `safeArea`/`withSafeArea()` in `lib/memberChrome.ts` (#983's one place a visual value is spelled) are correct in both builds, the top inset is `TopBar`'s **own padding** so the strip under the status bar carries the header's themed background rather than the page behind it, and the bottom inset is the layout's, once, for every route. **A native control that cannot work is absent, never broken** (Sign in with Apple, #1075, follows the same rule: iOS only and off unless `NEXT_PUBLIC_APPLE_SIGN_IN` is set, its entitlement written by `profile:apply` only when the profile enables it, its Clerk exchange the one unverified function `signInWithAppleToken()`): no bridge, or no `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_*` client ids in the build, hides Clerk's own Google button and leaves the ordinary email-and-password form; where the native sheet *can* work (#1077) Clerk's own button **stays** — same logo, label and place as on the web — and `useNativeGoogleSignIn()` swaps what a tap on it does through a capture-phase handler on the sign-in page, so the app and the web look the same and the web screen is untouched — the ids are build args (design rule 1: no app identity is hard-coded) and iOS is initialised with the iOS client **and** the web client, because the ID token's `aud` must be the client Clerk holds. **Handing Clerk that token is three calls, not one** (#1285): the token is requested with a fresh random `nonce` (`googleSignInNonce()` — Clerk refused an Android token without one, and its own Android SDK always sets one), the Google library's own session is cleared with `logout({ provider: 'google' })` before every `login()` (after a Clerk sign-out it replayed the previous token, refused as `authorization_invalid`), and the resource `authenticateWithGoogleOneTap()` returns is handed to `handleGoogleOneTapCallback()`, which is what activates the session and navigates — without it the app sat on the login with no error. The refusal is shown under the notice (`signInErrorDetail()`), since those three failures were indistinguishable until it was; `api/src/test/native-google-complete.unit.test.ts` is the gate. Do not drop the nonce, the pre-login clear or the callback, and do not register the app under Clerk's *Native applications* expecting it to fix a refused token — it does not. And **the push token is removed before the session that authenticates the removal ends**: `DELETE /me/devices/:token` is the member's own, so a sign-out calls `unregisterPushToken()` first (`lib/nativePush.ts`, which is why the token is remembered locally at all), while registering is idempotent by the API's upsert, is simply re-run on every sign-in and names the app's own id (`appIdFromInfo(App.getInfo())`, #1077 — a dev app `com.cordel.fitness.dev` files its tokens under that id, not under the API's default). A tapped notification opens `/notifications` and **no per-type route** — the push `data` block carries the type and entity for a later ticket, and guessing a destination answers the wrong screen. Do not add a second native-detection rule, a plugin import outside that one module, a `isNative()` branch in render, a safe-area literal in a page, a user-agent sniff, or a native control that renders in a build that cannot use it.
- **The Members App's avatar is a menu, and Log out is one confirmed sequence** (#1282). `components/MemberUserMenu.tsx` draws it (the member's email, **Profile**, **Log out** — presentation only, every colour from `lib/memberChrome.ts`, #983) and is rendered by `TopBar` on **every** page, Profile included, since Log out must stay reachable from the one screen that used to have no avatar. `lib/memberUserMenu.ts` is the pure decision (`userMenuItems()`, `signInPath()`) and `lib/memberLogout.ts` the one sequence: `unregisterPushToken()` **before** `signOut()` (#1073's rule — the delete is authenticated as the member whose device it is), then a full navigation to `/{locale}/sign-in`. Log out asks first, through `components/LogoutConfirmDialog.tsx` (the app's one dialog, `MemberDialog`), and nothing is signed out before the confirm. It is offered to a superadmin too: in the Support bar (`AdminBar`, which has no avatar because a superadmin who is not impersonating is linked to no member) and in the avatar menu **while impersonating**, where Log out *leaves the impersonated member and lands on Support mode*: the dialog audits and clears the impersonation (`reportImpersonationStopped()`, `stopImpersonation()`) and navigates home, and the superadmin's own session stays signed in (the confirmation is the member's own, word for word). Only the Support bar's Log out, with no impersonation, signs the superadmin out. Gate: `api/src/test/member-user-menu.unit.test.ts`. Do not add a second logout path, a Log out that skips the push-token removal or the confirmation, or a colour in the menu.
- **No query binds `LIMIT` as a parameter.** `db.query` / `tx.query` are mysql2's `execute()` — a server-side prepared statement — and MySQL refuses a bound `LIMIT ?` there, which nothing catches at build time and which makes the route answer a bare `500` on every call (#1113's reminder run reached CI that way, every pass 500ing with "no reminders were created" as the only visible symptom). A `LIMIT` whose value varies is **validated as an integer and written into the statement**, which is what every paginated route here already does and what `training-plan-templates.ts` and `gym-training-plans.ts` have said in a comment all along; the validation is the safety argument, so interpolating an unvalidated value is not what this permits. `api/src/test/bound-limit-placeholder.unit.test.ts` is the gate over every production source under `api/src` (comments stripped, the test directory excluded, and `LIMIT ?` matched case-sensitively and not followed by a second `?`, since `req.query.limit ?? 50` is how those twelve routes read their own limit).
- A new `recordAudit({ entityType })` value also needs an `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` entry in `api/src/infra/audit-registry.ts`. Without one the rows write but carry no `entity_name`, and the type never reaches the Audit Log's entity-type filter (`GET /audit-logs/meta`).
- A Member holds **zero or one Membership Plan**, never two (#956, migration 213). This reverses #634 §6/§14's "several plans in parallel, but only one of each type" (migration 172): the UNIQUE index on `user_memberships` is `user_memberships_one_active (active_member_key)` again, and migration 213's one-time sweep is what made that creatable (per owning Member, the `active` row beats the `paused` one, then the latest `starts_at`, then the latest `id`; the rest were cancelled and dated like a replacement, with a `status_changed` ledger row each — `npm run memberships:multi-active` is the read-only report that identifies them, and it is also the only thing that reports the overlap the sweep deliberately leaves). `api/src/domain/oneActivePlan.ts` is the one place the rule is decided and `api/src/api/one-active-plan.ts` the one place it is enforced in SQL. Since #1108 stage 1 the one caller is the `draft → active` commit, `POST /user-memberships/:id/activate`, which reads and locks the member's live plan **inside the transaction that flips the status** — the three insert paths (`POST /user-memberships`, `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan`, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`) create a Draft and check nothing, because a Draft is not the member's plan and a replacement has to be configurable beside the plan it replaces. Three of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **`paused` counts as the one plan** and so does a future-dated row (`LIVE_ASSIGNMENT_STATUSES`): the first because pausing would otherwise be a way around the rule, the second because `pending` is a derived `lifecycle_status` and the row is stored `active` — the index cannot express either (its generated column is NULL unless the row is `active`), so the database guarantees at most one `active` row per **owning** Member and the application guarantees the rest. **"Already has a plan" is read through `user_membership_members`**, never `user_memberships.member_id` alone, so a covered member of a family plan somebody else owns already has one (#956 Q4) — that is the overlap `active_member_key` is blind to, which is why it is checked under the `FOR UPDATE` lock and why migration 213 does not sweep it. And **replacing is immediate and explicit**: an unconfirmed caller gets `409 { error: 'active_plan_exists', message, current_plan, conflicts }` carrying both plan names and the current plan's dates (so the confirmation dialog needs no second read of what it is about to cancel — that dialog is one place too: `apps/admin/src/lib/activePlanConflict.ts` recognises the 409 **by shape**, the status *and* the code *and* a usable `current_plan`, so the router's other 409s still read as an error line, and `apps/admin/src/components/ReplacePlanDialog.tsx` draws it over the app's existing `ConfirmDialog`, raised with one set of `common.replace_plan_*` keys because the rule is one rule — by the Assigned Plan card's `⋮ → Activate` since #1108 stage 1 moved the 409 onto the commit, with the four assignment entry points' own wiring left in place for stage 2's Save & Pay; the first attempt never sends `confirm`, which is also why a click handler is never passed by reference — the `MouseEvent` would land in the `confirmReplacement` argument and confirm it — and Cancel clears the conflict and nothing else, no request, no close, no reload. Do not add a second replacement dialog, a per-page wording of it, or a client-side "does this Member have a plan" check), and `confirm: true` makes the superseded row `cancelled` with `closed_at` stamped now and `ends_at` set to the new plan's `starts_at`, through `supersedeLiveAssignments()` and nowhere else — the two plans meet at one date, they never overlap as live rows, the old row is never deleted and its snapshot, promotions, billing events and `next_billing_date` are left exactly as they are, because they are the record of what it billed. A `starts_at` earlier than the plan being replaced is a 400, since the superseded row's `ends_at` would land before its own `starts_at`. `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan` needs no `confirm` (replacement is its whole contract) but does for a *second* live assignment the request never named; the Plans page's Assign is all-or-nothing across the selected Members, because a partial run would leave a gym with cancellations it did not get to weigh. **Covering** a Member is the fourth way they come to hold a Plan, so `POST /user-memberships/:id/members` enforces the rule too — and it is the one path that offers **no** `confirm`: a replacement ends the old plan on the new one's `starts_at`, and coverage added to a plan that started months ago has no such date, so the staff action stays the explicit one (close their plan, then add them) and the 409 says so. Do not add a second status list, a second supersede, a scheduled transition (it would need a nightly sweep to flip the status on the day), a stored "current plan" column, or a path that activates an assignment outside that one commit. Note what this did *not* change: the Member-level Billing Simulation stays consolidated (#629 Q4) and simply has one assignment to consolidate, and `POST /me/payment-requests` lost #634's `409 multiple_active_memberships` with the state that produced it — `user_membership_id` is still accepted and still narrows the read.
- **A newly assigned Membership Plan is a `draft`, and one explicit commit makes it the member's** (#1108 stage 1, migration 227). The three insert paths write `ASSIGNMENT_CREATION_STATUS` (`api/src/api/user-memberships.ts`) and `POST /user-memberships/:id/activate` is the one `draft → active` transition — a route of its own rather than a `status` flip on `PUT /:id`, which refuses it and names it, the way a cancellation is refused and routed to `DELETE`. It is the schema widening migration 198 (#786) said a payment gate before activation would need; `awaiting_payment` stays retired; #1108's second pre-activation state is *Pending Payment*, `pending_payment` since stage 2 (migration 234, the bullet below), written only by Save & Pay. Five of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **A Draft is not the member's Membership Plan**: it is deliberately outside `LIVE_ASSIGNMENT_STATUSES` (#1108 Q2), so one Active plan and one Draft replacement is a legal state — which is what a Draft is *for* — and #956's check, its `confirm` and its supersede all moved onto the commit; migration 213's UNIQUE index needs no change, because `active_member_key` is `IF(status = 'active', member_id, NULL)`, and it still backstops the commit, since the `draft → active` UPDATE populates that column. **A Draft bills nothing and books nothing**: the nightly run and `api/src/api/activity-eligibility.ts` keep reading `um.status = 'active'`, so a Draft is not bookable, which *is* a behaviour change — an unpaid assignment was bookable from creation under #786. **A Draft is fully editable and fully projected** (§2–§5): `draft` is in `SNAPSHOT_EDITABLE_STATUSES`, `ATTACHABLE_STATUSES`, `SIMULATED_STATUSES` and the Member card's own `LIVE_STATUSES`, so the Assigned Plan card's existing **Billing Event Forecast** answers what committing it would bill — Q3's answer is to read it there rather than grow a fourth name beside *Membership Fee Simulation*, *Billing Event Forecast* and the **Billing Events** ledger. **A Draft is invisible wherever "does this member have a plan" is asked of something other than its own configuration**: `latestEnrollmentStatusSql()` excludes it (it reads the *latest* row, so a Draft would overwrite a currently Active member's enrollment status on the Members list and drop them out of the Nutrition Dashboard's count), `countsAsRecentMembership()` answers `false` for it in both directions (a Draft's `starts_at` is by definition inside #927's window), the Financials dashboard's assigned-plan count excludes it, and the member's own reads exclude it through `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_FILTER` beside `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_ORDER` — both halves, in both callers, because `FIELD()` answers 0 for a status it does not list and would sort a Draft *first*. And **a Draft nobody commits does not expire** (Q1a): staff discard it through `POST /:id/close`, which is in `CLOSEABLE_FROM` for that reason and is warning-free because a Draft has never had a `next_billing_date`. The gate is `api/src/test/draft-assignment-status.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only), which also asserts that every `INSERT INTO user_memberships` names the `status` column — it still DEFAULTs to `'active'`, so a fourth insert path that omitted it would create an Active plan with no error. Do not add a pre-activation status beyond `pending_payment` (the bullet below), a second place that commits a Draft, an expiry sweep for one, a Draft that is live, billable or bookable, or a surface that reports a Draft as the member's membership.
- **Save & Pay is the point of no return, and `pending_payment` is the state it leaves a Draft in** (#1108 stage 2, migration 234). `POST /user-memberships/:id/save-and-pay` asks #956's one-plan rule (`409 active_plan_exists` unless `confirm`) and moves the row `draft → pending_payment` — locked, since it is in none of `SNAPSHOT_EDITABLE_STATUSES`, `ATTACHABLE_STATUSES` or the Promotion apply paths, excluded wherever a Draft is (`MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_FILTER`, `latestEnrollmentStatusSql()`, `countsAsRecentMembership()`, the Financials count) and included wherever a Draft is projected (`SIMULATED_STATUSES`, the Member card's `LIVE_STATUSES`, `CLOSEABLE_FROM`) — or straight to `active` when the first cycle owes nothing. `api/src/api/assignment-commit.ts` is the one place either move lives: `submitForPayment()` for Save & Pay and `commitAssignment()` for every `→ active` — `POST /:id/activate` (API-only now, for a free plan or a staff shortcut), the payment webhook's `completed` branch, `POST /:id/record-payment` (a cash first payment: one `payment_recorded` event plus the commit, one transaction) and a `payment_recorded` appended through `POST /payments` against a pending row. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The rule is asked at Save & Pay and performed at activation**: nothing is superseded while the payment is outstanding, and the activation supersedes whatever is still live with the confirmation taken as given, because a paid-up member must not be left planless. **The forecast is never pre-persisted as Billing Events**: the Billing Event Forecast is the engine the nightly run prices from, so the real events match the simulation by construction and the ledger stays what was charged. And **a cash first payment stamps no `next_billing_date` and stores no card**. The card offers *Save & Pay* on a Draft and *Record cash payment* / *Send payment link* on a pending row; `POST /me/payment-requests` accepts a pending row ahead of an active one so the member's own *Pay now* reaches it. Do not add a second pending status, a second place that commits a row, a supersede at Save & Pay time, a pre-persisted forecast, or a `PUT` that flips into or out of `pending_payment`.
- **A Membership is one contract, seen as `primary` by its owner and `linked` by every other covered Member** (#1191). A Duo is one `user_memberships` row (owner = `member_id`) plus a `user_membership_members` row per covered Member, and `api/src/domain/assignmentRelationship.ts` is the one place that decides the relationship (`assignmentRelationship()`) and the one SQL fragment for "the Memberships of Member X" (`MEMBER_MEMBERSHIP_SQL`: owner column OR covered row, as an `EXISTS` so a contract is never duplicated). `GET /user-memberships/member/:id/configuration` and `GET /user-memberships?member_id=` read through it and report the **derived** `assignment_relationship`; the global list stays one row per contract. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Nothing is stored**: no `linked` column, no second `user_memberships` row, no Billing Events for a Linked Member. **A Linked context is read-only and enforced by the server**: a caller names the viewing Member with `?as_member_id=`, and a `linked` one gets `403 linked_member_read_only` on every write under `/user-memberships/:id/*` and on `GET /:id/billing-events`, while `GET /:id` answers `billing_events.available = false`; an `as_member_id` the Membership does not cover is a 404. The Members App's own `/me/*` reads filter on the owner column, so a Linked Member never reaches the contract's Billing Events or payment actions there. And **the Admin hides what the server refuses**: a *Relationship* column (Primary / Linked) on the Member-scoped `AssignedPlansTable`, no `Assign New Plan` / `Cancel Plan` on a Linked row, no Billing Events section, and a Linked plan stays in the Member card's Active list because it is that Member's plan. Do not add a stored linked flag, a second Membership-of-Member query, or a Linked-context write path.
- **Cancel on a Draft is a hard delete** (#1240 PR 1). `DELETE /user-memberships/:id/draft` removes a `draft` row (locked, tenant-scoped, audited) and its snapshot children, which all cascade; any other status answers `409 not_draft` and keeps going through `/close` or `DELETE /:id`, which preserve the record of what was billed. A Draft in Edit mode closes with **Cancel | Save | Save & Pay** in place of *Done Editing* (Cancel confirms first; Save only leaves the mode, since each section persists itself and the row stays `draft`; Save & Pay is #1108 stage 2's route unchanged). The "Negotiated Discount %" is the existing Personal Membership Fee Benefit (`PUT /:id/fee-benefit`), not a new column. Do not add a second discard path or a Draft `cancelled` row.
- Any new code path that inserts a `user_memberships` row must call `snapshotAssignedPlan()` (`api/src/api/assigned-plan-snapshot.ts`) inside the same transaction. An Assigned Plan owns the commercial configuration it was assigned with (#635 §11–§17) — an assignment created without one reads back as `snapshot_captured: false` and silently falls back to the live catalogue, so a Plan or Product edited later would move what it bills.
- Any new code path that *edits* an existing assignment's snapshot must call `materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot()` first, in the same transaction (#635 stage 6). The live fallback is all-or-nothing: the moment one section of an uncaptured assignment is written, the assignment counts as captured and every section the edit did not mention reads back as empty. An edit also stays on that assignment — never write through to the Membership Plan, another assignment or the Product — and a `cancelled`/`expired` assignment is not editable at all.
- Billing reads the **Assigned Plan snapshot**, never the live catalogue, for an assignment that already exists (#635 §13–§17). A query that projects, charges or advances an assignment's billing takes its cadence from `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` in `api/src/api/assigned-plan-snapshot.ts` (`COALESCE(um.recurring_billing_*, bp.recurring_billing_*)`) and therefore **LEFT** JOINs `billing_policies` — an INNER JOIN drops every assignment whose Plan lost its policy. Since #635 stage 13 (migration 189) `billing_policies` holds that pair and `auto_renew` and nothing else: Initial Billing, Initial Service and Recurring Service are gone, **Billing & Duration is the Plan's only billing section** (the surviving pair is its *Billing frequency* — Free/Paid/Bonus say how long a contract runs, never how often it charges), and no second cadence, service-period concept or per-plan billing column may be reintroduced. Since #820 a Plan's *Billing frequency* is also a **choice of two** — Month (`1 month`) or 4 Weeks (`4 week`) — named once in `api/src/domain/planBillingFrequency.ts` and enforced by `PUT /membership-plans/:id/billing-policy`, never by the dropdown alone: the stored shape is still that `(interval, unit)` pair, so a new pair is added in **two** places (the declaration and, mirrored, `PLAN_BILLING_FREQUENCY_OPTIONS` in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/plans/planProfile.ts`) and never as a symbolic column. A cadence outside the two is refused on write but still read, billed and displayed wherever it is already stored — `planBillingFrequencyOf()` answers `null` for it, and a surface that coerced that `null` to Month would tell a gym it bills monthly while the run charges every two months. Prices and benefits come from `user_memberships.membership_fee_price`, the `user_membership_{session,oneoff,periodical}` rows, the `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` rows and `user_membership_services`' own price columns; the live catalogue is only the fallback for an assignment that captured no snapshot. The same holds for a path that merely *displays* an assignment's benefits or cadence, on a staff screen or a member-facing one (`GET /me/membership`, #635 stage 10): it reads the assignment's rows through `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation()` / `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`, so what the Member is shown cannot drift from what the nightly run charges them. The Plan-keyed `membership_plan_benefits` (migration 006) is gone — migration 184 — and nothing replaces it.
- **The entity is called a Product** (#949). *Sellable Item* was its name until this rename and is retired as a term: no user-facing copy, no identifier, no route, no column and no prose in these docs says it again. All three stages have shipped — stage 1 the copy a gym owner reads, stage 2 the code identifiers (`Product*`, `api/src/domain/product*.ts`, `components/ProductBenefits.tsx`), the file names, the locale keys (the `products` namespace, `col_product`, `applies_to_product`, `impact_products`) and the admin route, and stage 3 the wire and the schema (migration 214): the API root is **`/products`** with no alias, the table is **`products`**, its FK column **`product_id`** in the thirteen tables that key to it, the three promotion snapshots' **`product_name`**, the join table **`product_professional_services`**, the eight **`products_*`** CHECKs (plus `chk_products_enrollment_status`), every index and FK constraint renamed with them, and `charge_types.is_gym_charge` → **`is_product`**. The response fields are `products` (on an enriched Plan and on a tax rate's delete impact), `product_id`, `product_name`, `product_status`, `product_deleted_at` and `product_retired`; the stored `promotions.applies_to` value and the Billing Simulation line `kind` are **`'product'`**; the feature flag is **`financials.products`** and the audit entity type **`product`**, whose `audit_logs.entity_type` rows were backfilled because that column is the key `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` and the Audit Log's filter are built from — the audited values themselves (`previous_values`/`new_values`) were not touched, because they are the record of what changed. Three things keep an older spelling on purpose and are **not** drift: the two legacy admin routes `financials/sellable-items` and `financials/gym-charges`, each a `permanentRedirect` to `financials/products` so a link written before either rename still lands; and the old migrations, which keep their own file names and SQL, because they are the history of a schema that really did carry those names (a migration is never edited, and `require()`ing one by another name would simply fail). Two gates hold the line, both in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only: `api/src/test/product-terminology.unit.test.ts` fails the build if a locale **value or key** in either app says otherwise, and `api/src/test/product-identifiers.unit.test.ts` fails it if any retired **shape** — `SellableItem` / `sellableItem` / `SELLABLE_ITEM`, anything spelling `sellable` at all, `gym_charge`/`gym_charges`, `gymCharge`/`GymCharge`, or the English prose — comes back anywhere under `api/src` or either app's `src`. It bans shapes rather than a list of names, which is why it still needs no allowlist: its only carve-outs are the two gates' own fixtures and, by rule rather than by file, the migration **file name** inside a line that `require()`s one. A **Product Type** is `products.type` and nothing else: the global `charge_types` lookup (no `gym_id`, per the constraint above) is a different concept that merely shares a word and keeps the name **Charge Type** — which is also why `is_product` lives on it and says which Charge Types seed a per-gym Product. New copy says Product / Products, *Producto* / *Productos* and *Producte* / *Productes*.
- A **Product's Billing Frequency** is a separate question from a Plan's cadence, and since #945 it is a choice of **four**: Once, 4 Weeks, Month, Year, named once in `api/src/domain/productFrequency.ts` and mirrored for the form in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/financials/products/productFrequency.ts`. `week` (#821) and `per_session` (#945) are **retired, not deleted**: `products_billing_frequency_check` (migration 123, renamed with its table in 214) still permits all six values and there is no backfill either time, because an item a gym configured weekly is a real price whose period is neither a month nor 28 days, and because what a gym meant by *Per Session* — a package billed once, or the usage-based model the product never had — is not knowable from the row, so #945 §3's "flag the value for correction rather than guessing" applies rather than the coercion it also invites. (`per_session` billing identically to `once` — `cadenceForProduct()` gives neither a schedule — is not a licence to rewrite it.) So "what may be stored" and "what may be configured" are two different answers, and `productFrequencyWriteError(next, current)` is the one place that decides the second — `POST /products` refuses a retired value outright, `PUT /products/:id` accepts one only when it is what the row already holds, so editing another field of a legacy item neither 400s nor silently rewrites its frequency, and nothing may be moved *onto* it (nor from one retired value to the other). `isRecurringFrequency()` (`domain/productClassification.ts`) still counts `week` as recurring and `per_session` as not, so a legacy item keeps classifying into the same benefit section and keeps billing, and correcting a `per_session` item to Once moves it between no sections. The editor renders the retired value as a **disabled** option only while the form holds it — never as a selectable one, and never coerced to another frequency — with the notice beside it **naming** that value (`frequency_legacy_notice` interpolates the label, resolved before `t()` is called, since there is more than one retired value), and `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it verbatim, because Duplicate is a copy, not the dropdown. Every other surface only ever *displays* a stored frequency, so the offered list has exactly one consumer and a legacy row still reads *Per Session* where it is stored; `week` meanwhile stays **offered** by a Session Benefit's own Frequency (#918), which is a different set answering a different question. A new frequency therefore goes in **three** places (the declaration, its mirror, and the CHECK beside them); do not add a second accepted-set list in a route or a page.
- **How a billing frequency reads is one answer, in one namespace** (#1128). What may be *stored*, what may be *configured* and what a value is *called* are three different questions: the first two stay the API's (`api/src/domain/productFrequency.ts` for a Product, `api/src/domain/planBillingFrequency.ts` for a Plan's cadence) and the third is `apps/admin/src/lib/billingFrequency.ts`' alone, resolved from the one `billing_frequency` locale namespace — `once` → **Once**, `four_weeks` → **Every 4 weeks**, `month` → **Monthly**, `year` → **Yearly**. It had been spelled in five namespaces, so the same monthly Product read *Month* on the Products page, *Month(s)* in a Membership Plan's benefit row and in a Promotion's and *Monthly* on an Assigned Plan card. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. It is **not an accepted set**: `week` (#821) and `per_session` (#945) are retired from the Product dropdown and still stored, billed and displayed, so they keep a label here — one label — and `billingFrequencyLabel()` answering `null` for an absent or unknown value is what makes a cell read `—` rather than print the key (next-intl prints a missing key verbatim). A **Membership Plan has no frequency terminology of its own**, so the Plan's Billing frequency dropdown, its read-only row, its Details modal and an Assigned Plan's frozen cadence all read that same table through `cadenceFrequencyLabel()` — never the stored pair out loud (`1 / month`, `1 × Month`) — which is why the cadence pair, `planBillingFrequencyOf()` and `legacyCadenceText()` live in that module and `plans/planProfile.ts` **re-exports** them: a card under `components/assignedPlan/` cannot import a page module. A **Session Benefit's Frequency is a different question** (#918 — how often an allowance renews, its own set, `Weekly` among them, its own `plans.session_frequency_*` keys) and this module must never be pointed at it; so is the **period noun** a duration is counted in, which keeps `plans.period_unit_*` because `2 × 4 Weeks` (#892 §9) must not become `2 × Every 4 weeks`. And the **Members App says the same words** from its own `membership.frequency` map, because the two apps share no frontend module. The gate is `api/src/test/billing-frequency-labels.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only): no second spelling in any namespace, no surface interpolating a frequency into a key of its own, and the two apps in step. Do not add a label for a frequency to a page's namespace, resolve one from a page's `t`, print a cadence pair, or offer a new or retired value — that is the API's question, above.
- **A Periodic Promotion's number is a Duration, and it is the number already stored** (#1135). `promotion_periodical.quantity` is what the billing engine counts *periods* with — `buildItemStream()` covers an occurrence while `occurrence < firstCovered + grant.quantity`, and `collectBillableItems()` fixes such a grant's **billed** quantity to 1 ("the grant covers periods, it does not say how many lockers") — so the ticket added no column, no second field and no backfill: what it decided is that the column is **named for what it counts**. `BenefitQuantityColumn` in `apps/admin/src/components/ProductBenefits.tsx` is the one place that says which (`'quantity'`, the default for every section, and `'duration'`, the Promotion card's Periodical section alone, declared on `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_SECTIONS` rather than in the JSX so both halves of the card ask one answer), and `benefitQuantityLabelKey()` is the one place that turns it into a label — it is still one column of the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` grid (#916), because a per-section meaning is a label and a caption, never a column of its own. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. A **Membership Plan's** periodical benefit quantity is *units billed each occurrence*, so that section keeps saying Quantity and nothing else may be relabelled: the two numbers are different questions that happen to share a column. **The derived sentence is the frequency module's**, not a page's: `billingFrequencyDurationLabel()` (`apps/admin/src/lib/billingFrequency.ts`, keys `duration_*` in the one `billing_frequency` namespace) answers `3 months` from the item's own frequency, with the count pluralised **by the message** and a 4-week period reading `3 × 4 weeks` because `2 × 4 Weeks` (#892 §9) is the form this app already counts them in — it is a different sentence from the frequency label beside it (*Monthly*) and from `plans.period_unit_*`, which is untouched. A frequency that **names no period** (`once`, a legacy `per_session`) gets no key and no caption, and `null` is what makes the cell fall back to the stored number instead of printing the key. And the Duration **ends the promotion, never the Product** (§15): the period after the last covered one is `buildItemStream()`'s existing `pay_regular` fall-through, so nothing was added to make a Product continue at its regular price. §13 is the one API change — `PUT /promotions/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` reads the number with `Number()` rather than `parseInt()`, so `3.5` is a 400 instead of a silently stored `3`. The gate is `api/src/test/promotion-periodic-duration.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not add a duration column beside that quantity, a second place that decides what the column counts, a per-page spelling of `3 months`, or a Duration on a Session or One-off grant, whose number is units.
- A **Product's Mandatory flag** (#832, migration 200) is `products.mandatory`, and it is one column for both kinds of row that table holds: the per-gym System items seeded from `charge_types` (`is_system = 1` — the ticket's "Base Products", the Insurance Fee among them) and the gym's own (`is_system = 0`, "Custom"). Do not add a second flag, a platform-level product catalogue or a per-kind editor for it. It is **editable on a System item**, so `PUT /products/:id` writes it outside the `IF(? = 0, …)` `is_system` guard that still freezes a System row's name/type/units, and it is written as `mandatory = IF(?, ?, mandatory)` keyed on whether the request supplied the field — a `COALESCE` on the value itself would make unchecking the box unpersistable, which is the one way a boolean column of this shape goes wrong. A body value that is neither a boolean nor 0/1 is a 400, never coerced (the string `"false"` is truthy). There is no CHECK on it (the router coerces to `? 1 : 0`, and `ADD CONSTRAINT` rebuilds `products` under `ALGORITHM=COPY`). Exactly **one** thing reads it, since #893: a mandatory item is part of every **Membership Plan** benefit section, and `api/src/domain/mandatoryPlanBenefits.ts` is the only place that decides what that means. Its candidate set is the gym's **active, non-deleted** mandatory items — an inactive or deleted one is ignored, because forcing it in would fail every Plan save on a catalogue change nobody asked for — and which section each belongs to is `classifyProduct()` (#550) and never a second mapping. `mergeMandatoryBenefits()` is what a read reports (`GET /membership-plans/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` and `enrichPlan`'s three embedded sections), adding a missing mandatory item as `implicit: true` at quantity 1; `withMandatoryBenefits()` is what the replace-all `PUT` writes, so a client that drops one has it **preserved rather than 400d** — that is what makes an existing Plan pick up a newly mandatory item the first time any of its sections is saved. A submitted mandatory item passes through untouched, quantity included: Mandatory says the item must exist, never what its quantity is, and it is not a reason to change one. That is the **Plan's** quantity; on an **Assigned Plan** the quantity of a catalogue-mandatory line is the number of Members the Membership covers (#1187): `resolveAssignedPlanBenefitQuantity()` (`api/src/domain/assignedBenefitQuantity.ts`) replaces — never multiplies — the configured quantity, `syncMandatoryProductQuantities()` (`assigned-plan-snapshot.ts`) applies it in SQL from `snapshotAssignedPlan()`, `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` and `POST`/`DELETE /user-memberships/:id/members…` (non-closed assignments only), the count is `user_membership_members` rows owner included (never below 1), the quantity is read-only for staff and members, the Plan's own row and the Billing Events already written are never touched, and the Plan-level `membership_plan_*.mandatory` Yes/No (#1184) plays no part in it. Do not add a second place that decides it, a hard-coded Product, or a recalculation of historical billing. The enforcement is the server's — the shared `ProductBenefits.tsx` takes an explicit `enforceMandatory` opt-in (the Plans page only, never Promotions) and merely renders the row without a `✕` and without a picker that could swap the item away, so a client bypassing it changes nothing. Everything else about the flag is still unread and still out of scope: automatic inclusion in a **Billing** Plan, refusing its removal from one, Billing Plan pricing and any Insurance Fee special case, so a path that acts on the flag beyond the above is deciding that behaviour and needs a ticket. In particular #893 changes no billing: a mandatory item reaches an assignment's Assigned Plan snapshot the ordinary way, as a stored Plan benefit row, and nothing back-fills the Plans that were never saved again.
- A **Product of Type `Sessions` prices the whole package**, not one session: `products.amount` is the total for the `units` sessions it contains, so `Units: 5` beside `Price: €50.00` means €50.00 for the five and never €250.00 (#942). Nothing multiplies a price by a unit count, and no per-session price is stored, computed or displayed anywhere. What the ticket added is the sentence that says so, and `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/financials/products/productPriceNotes.ts` is the one place that decides it — `sessionPackageNote()` answers the *Total price for N sessions* wording (ICU-pluralised off the item's own `units`, with a package wording for a session item whose nullable `units` is unset) and `null` for every other type, which is what keeps fee-based items untouched; `taxNoteKey()` answers which `(tax …)` suffix a displayed figure takes, and `null` for an item with no `applied_tax_rate` rather than claiming a tax that is not configured. Every surface showing such a price asks that module rather than testing `type === 'sessions'` beside a price for itself, and the two form hints ask `sessionPackageNoteForForm()` with the draft's live Type and Units so the sentence tracks what is being typed rather than the saved row. Do not add a per-session price column, a per-session figure derived from `amount / units`, or a second place that decides this wording.
- **The Products a gym is created with are a declaration, and seeding one is never a correction** (#1149). Gym creation has written one `products` row per `is_product` charge type since #543 and the Personal Training package since #371, with a name and a type and nothing else; what each of those seven rows should *be configured as* — the price, the Billing Frequency, the two statuses, `units` and `mandatory` — is `api/src/domain/defaultGymProducts.ts` alone, with `api/src/api/gym-default-products.ts` the one writer both creation paths (`POST /gyms`, `POST /gyms/:id/duplicate`) call. There is **no migration and no backfill**: the ticket's §8 is that an existing gym is untouched, so this lives beside the creation paths rather than in a migration. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. Every statement is an **INSERT and never an UPDATE** — idempotency is the database's (`INSERT IGNORE` under `products_gym_id_charge_type_unique` for a charge-type row, #371's `WHERE NOT EXISTS` on `(gym_id, is_system, name)` for the package, which has no `charge_type_id` to be keyed on) — so re-running a gym's initialization adds nothing, a Product the gym has since repriced or deactivated keeps what it holds (§7), and a soft-deleted one stays deleted rather than being resurrected. A System Product's **name and type still come from `charge_types`** (#543's rule, unchanged): the declaration's `name` is the catalogue name it is *expected* to match — a test fixture, never what is written — and a charge type the declaration says nothing about **still seeds**, with the column defaults, because a gym must have a Product for every `is_product` row the catalogue holds. The gym's `Standard VAT` 21% rate is inserted **before** the Products, since every declared price is stored `tax_behavior = 'inclusive'` of it, and a gym that somehow has no system rate gets `tax_rate_id = NULL` rather than a failed creation — the figure is still the price the gym charges. And a **declared frequency is one a Product may still be configured with** (#945): the defaults are created by the product rather than kept from a legacy row, so `week` or `per_session` here would be a typo rather than a state to handle. Adding or changing a default goes in **one** place, that declaration, and changes what the *next* gym is created with and nothing else. The gate is `api/src/test/default-gym-products.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only), which also fails the build if either creation path grows an `INSERT INTO products` of its own or seeds its tax rate after its Products. Do not add a second place that decides what a new gym's Products are, a per-gym default catalogue table, a seed that updates an existing row, or a backfill onto existing gyms.
- The **nightly billing run** (`POST /billing/run`) prices the cycle `next_billing_date` names, never "today": `priceMembershipFeeOn()` (`api/src/api/membership-fee-pricing.ts`) asks the Billing Simulation's own `resolveMembershipFee()` what that date costs and whether it is waived, so what is charged cannot drift from what the staff screens and the Member's My Membership page show (#635 stages 11–12). A waived cycle calls no payment provider and writes no `payment_requests` row — it records a `waived_billing` billing event and advances `next_billing_date`, leaving `last_billed_at` untouched. A new `billing_events.event_type` goes in **two** places: the writer and the `billing_events_event_type_check` CHECK (current definition: migration 185) — adding only the first makes the INSERT fail and takes the run's transaction with it.
- A **`next_billing_date` written onto an assignment is never today or earlier** (#790). The payment webhook stamps the first one as the first `starts_at`-anchored cycle boundary *strictly after* UTC today, and any transition back to `active` (Reactivate, a `PUT` status flip) walks a past one forward the same way along its own schedule — both through `api/src/domain/nextBillingDateStamp.ts`, which reads the cadence via `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` (LEFT JOIN) and every date, today included, as a `YYYY-MM-DD` string from SQL, over the one pure rule `firstBillingDateAfter()` (`api/src/domain/billingDate.ts`, stepping with the run's own `advanceBillingDate()`). A back-dated `starts_at` stays allowed: the cycles that elapsed before the first payment, and the ones missed while paused, are **written off** — no catch-up charge, no `adjustment` row — because the first payment is priced on *today* (`currentCycleDate()`), so stamping `starts_at + cadence` charged one elapsed cycle per night and then the paid cycle a second time. A new writer that puts an assignment (back) on the run's schedule calls one of those two helpers rather than computing a date itself; the run's own `advanceBillingDate(next_billing_date)` step and the staff Retry/Manual payment's one-cycle advance are unchanged.
- A rejected recurring charge **escalates, it does not repeat** (#785, migration 194). The nightly run counts consecutive rejections of the cycle `next_billing_date` names in `user_memberships.failed_attempts` (+ `last_failed_at`) and the second one pauses the assignment (`recordStatusChange`, `active → paused`, `source = 'system'`) — which is the whole mechanism, since a `paused` row is outside the run's `WHERE status = 'active'`. The count advances per run **day** (`DATE(last_failed_at) = UTC_DATE()`, compared in SQL so no DATETIME crosses a timezone conversion): a rejection on a date the assignment was already rejected on is recorded but does not escalate, because #781's second daily attempt becomes the day's real run whenever the first crashed. The retry is therefore the **next run day**, never a second attempt in the same loop: a card declined seconds ago declines again, and `next_billing_date` not moving is what schedules it. Only a provider **rejection** counts: `provider_error` (the provider threw or never answered — the charge's outcome is unknown, so a night the provider is unreachable would otherwise pause a gym's whole book) and `no_payment_method` (nothing was attempted) leave the counter exactly as it was, in both directions. Anything that settles or skips the cycle clears it — the run's own success and waived branches, the payment webhook's `completed` branch (a member paying their checkout link settles the same cycle), both staff actions through `clearDunningState()` (`api/src/domain/billingEventPayments.ts`), and any transition back to `active`, because reactivating means "bill this again" and a resumed assignment gets the documented two attempts rather than one. `domain/billingDunning.ts` is the one place that decides all of this (`countsTowardPause`, `registerRejection`); a new failure branch in the run asks it rather than incrementing the column itself, and a new code path that settles a cycle clears the pair. The decision is taken from the row **under the run's `FOR UPDATE` lock**, never from the row the due query read before the provider round trip: a staff payment landing in that window clears the pair, and deciding from the stale count would write it back and pause a member who has just paid. The columns are **not** part of the Assigned Plan snapshot (`has_billing_snapshot`) for migration 192's reason, there is no CHECK on them (`ADD CONSTRAINT` rebuilds `user_memberships` under `ALGORITHM=COPY`), the escalation is not a new status, and reactivation stays explicit — clearing the count never flips `paused → active`.
- A **system-wide nightly run** (`POST /billing/run`, `POST /recurring-bookings/run`) claims its slot through `claimRun()` and closes it through `finishRun()` (`api/src/infra/run-log.ts`), from the success path **and** the `catch` — never by stamping a `last_run_at`. The rule is **one *completed* run per UTC date** (#780, migration 193): both logs are histories now, one row per run, and a run is refused only when a run for today's UTC date already reached `completed`. Do not reintroduce an "N hours since the last start" window — a cron GitHub fires late then skips a day, and a stamp written before the first row is touched locks the day when the run crashes. Overlap is the `in_progress` half of the guard (a row younger than `STALE_RUN_MINUTES`, `api/src/domain/runGuard.ts`, answers `429`); a stale one is taken over. "Today's run already completed" answers **`200 { skipped_reason: 'already_completed_today', run_date, …zeroed counters }`**, never `429` — the second daily attempt is a safety net, not an error, and the counters are what `.github/workflows/*-run.yml` parses (#778). The field is `skipped_reason`, never `skipped`, which is already a numeric counter of the recurring booking run. A new run status goes in **two** places: `RunLogStatus` in `domain/runGuard.ts` and the `chk_<table>_status` CHECK (current definition: migration 193).
- There is exactly **one** implementation of "what does the Membership Fee cost on this date": `resolveMembershipFee()` (`api/src/domain/billingSimulation.ts`), and every path calls it — the Billing Simulation, `GET /me/membership` (its `membership_fee` and `upcoming_payments`), the Promotion apply/revoke adjustment (`currentMembershipFeeInTx()` in `membership-promotions.ts`) and, through `priceMembershipFeeOn()`, the nightly run and every staff surface (#635 stage 12). A second copy of the arithmetic is what stage 12 existed to remove: the Member was shown one price and charged another for the same cycle, in both directions. An applied Promotion's **Membership Fee Benefit lives inside the Promotion's own Free/Paid/Bonus timeline and ends with it** (the thread's stage 12 answer (a)) — a Promotion configured with no months discounts nothing at all, and a benefit whose own `duration_months` outlasts the Promotion is capped at it (`effectiveBenefitDurationMonths`, #625).
