A **stored** agreed price is therefore never the answer for any cycle, and since #635 stage 15 (migration 191) there is none: `user_memberships.final_price`, the `billing.date_aware_membership_fee` flag that gated the corrected pricing and the `payments.membership_fee_drift` report that quantified it are all gone, date-aware pricing is unconditional, and **no stored, promotion-discounted price column may be reintroduced** — price the cycle. The shared entry point is `api/src/api/membership-fee-pricing.ts`: `priceMembershipFeeOn(row, date)` for a given cycle, `currentMembershipFee()` / `currentMembershipFees()` for "what does this member pay now" (the cycle `currentCycleDate()` names — never one already past), and `FEE_ASSIGNMENT_COLUMNS` for the columns any of them needs. Any surface that shows or charges an assignment's fee — the Assigned Plans list and card, the Payments dashboard's projected rows, a staff- or member-raised payment request, My Membership — goes through it and reports the result as a computed `membership_fee`, never as a column. The number every path discounts *from* is `regularMembershipFee()` (`api/src/api/user-memberships.ts`) — the assignment's frozen `membership_fee_price`, then its Plan's price window, then a non-zero `base_price`; `base_price` alone is a constant 0 since migration 058. `membership_fee_price` is also where a **negotiated** fee lives (there is no second column for it), so a path that writes one is editing the snapshot and must materialise it first; `discount_reason` marks it as negotiated (which is what keeps `POST /membership-plans/:id/pricing/apply-to-assigned-plans` off it), and a past `discount_expires_at` is the one case that reads the Plan's price window instead (`regularMembershipFee`'s `ignoreFrozenFee`).
- A **Membership Plan's catalogue price** is quoted gross-and-net, and the rate that split is taken at is decided in exactly one place: `selectPlanTaxRates()` (`api/src/domain/planTaxRate.ts`, #817). `membership_plans.tax_rate_id` is nullable and the Pricing editor offers that as **Default**, which means the gym's system rate (`tax_rates.is_system = 1`, non-deleted — the row `seedSystemPtPackage()` already picks), so the helper answers two different questions: `own` is what a screen *displays* as the Plan's Tax rate (`null` ⇒ "Default", unchanged since #413) and `effective` is `own ?? system`, which is what `computePriceFields()` splits at and what `applied_tax_rate` reports. Resolving that fallback in the frontend is the defect the ticket removed — a Plan on Default had no rate, so `enrichPlan` returned a null split and the Current price rendered `—` for a Plan that has a price. Any surface showing a Plan's price reports the server's `amount_incl_tax`/`amount_excl_tax` and does no arithmetic of its own (`formatPlanCurrentPrice()` in `plans/planProfile.ts`), a soft-deleted rate is still shown as a Plan's own but is never chosen as the gym default, and no second "effective tax rate" resolution — in a query, a router or a page — may be added beside it.

- A code path that prices an assignment's **Membership Fee** on a given date applies the assignment's own Billing & Duration through `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()` (`api/src/domain/planDuration.ts`): the Free Period, the Pre-paid Duration and the Bonus Duration charge no fee, counted from the assignment's `starts_at` — never snapped to the first of its month, which is a Promotion preview's anchor, not a real contract's (#635 §7, stage 8). **An applied Promotion outranks it**: where a Promotion governs the date (inside its own timeline, or applying its Membership Fee Benefit) it decides the fee alone, per the #635 thread's Q2 answer — stacking the two also lets the Plan's free month override a Promotion's paid one, which inverts that answer. The durations come from `user_memberships.free_periods`/`paid_periods`/`bonus_periods`/`pay_beforehand_periods` and fall back to the Plan's live columns only for an assignment that captured no snapshot at all; a per-column `COALESCE` is wrong here, because the columns are nullable and a Free Period added to the Plan later would reach assignments that already exist (§13). Each of those four numbers is a **count of the assignment's own Billing Frequency periods**, never of calendar months (#892, migration 201 — the rename is what the columns say now): `PlanDuration` carries the cadence it is counted in, so `toPlanDuration()` takes it as a required argument (resolved through `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`, `1 month` when there is none) and no pricing path can silently count a 4-weekly contract's Free Period in months. Changing a Plan's Billing frequency therefore changes the unit and never the stored numbers, and a **Promotion's** own `free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` stay calendar months — `promotionTimeline.ts` is untouched by that rule. Only the Membership Fee is waived — a Plan's Period Benefits are still charged in a free period. The **Pre-paid Duration** (`pay_beforehand_periods`, #635 stage 13, migration 189) is the Promotion's own column on a Plan: the first N periods of the Paid Duration, already paid up front, so they charge nothing and read as `prepaid_plan` — a pre-paid cycle, never a waived one — and it is bounded by the Paid Duration (0..`paid_periods`) wherever it is written, `toPlanDuration()` clamping what it reads. Since #946 "already paid up front" is also *where the money comes from*, and `prepaidPeriodsDueOn()` (same module) is the one place that decides it: the whole Pre-paid Duration is owed **once, on the first of its periods** (`regular x pay_beforehand_periods` — €70/month with 3 pre-paid is a €210 charge), and only the periods that charge pays for bill nothing further. It is keyed on the *period*, never on the exact billing date, so a first payment raised a day late still owes it; it is a **real payment at the regular price**, so the line carries no `waive` benefit but a `prepaid_periods` count (and `quantity: 0` suppresses the fee event of a covered period, `walkStream()`'s existing mechanism); it stays `promotional` in the horizon's sense, so a projection runs on to the first ordinary charge instead of stopping at the lump; and a governing Promotion still decides the fee alone while the Personal Membership Fee Benefit still discounts every period it covers. Because it lives inside `resolveMembershipFee()`, the nightly run, a staff- or member-raised payment request, the Payments dashboard, My Membership, the Example Timeline and the Billing Event Simulation cannot disagree about that cycle — do not add a per-period prepaid charge, a second place that multiplies the fee, or a stored prepaid total. Since #1130 (migration 230) that whole stretch is a **cycle rather than a one-shot timeline**, and whether it starts again is `PlanDuration.repeats` — a **required** field, for the reason `cadence` is one: a pricing path that could forget it would bill a renewing contract its regular fee from the second cycle while the surface beside it showed the cycle restarting. `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()` is still the one place that decides it, now classifying a date inside whichever iteration of Free -> Pre-paid -> Paid -> Bonus it falls in (`planDurationCycleLength()` is Free + Paid + Bonus — never plus Pre-paid, which is the first slice *of* Paid), so a renewing contract never reaches `pay_regular` at all and `prepaidPeriodsDueOn()` collects the whole lump again at the start of every iteration (the thread's answer B). Five of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The flag is the assignment's own, never the Plan's live one**: `user_memberships.auto_renew`, frozen from `billing_policies.auto_renew` by `snapshotAssignedPlan()` at creation and read through `ASSIGNMENT_AUTO_RENEW` with deliberately **no** `COALESCE` onto `bp` — that column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT true`, so a live fallback would make every assignment written before the migration start renewing, which is the retroactive billing change answer A forbids (migration 230's `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` is the backfill that makes every existing contract non-repeating). **`materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot()` does not capture it** (`captureAutoRenew: false`, its one caller): that helper writes down what an uncaptured assignment resolves *today*, which for this column is the stored 0, so capturing the Plan's current flag there would start a second cycle on a contract already past its first one the moment staff edited an unrelated benefit section. For the same reason it is **outside `has_billing_snapshot` / `snapshot_captured`** — #772's rule for a NOT NULL defaulted column — and the all-or-nothing duration fallback reads it in both branches. **`auto_renew` off is the pre-ticket engine exactly** (answer C): one cycle, then the regular price, and the membership does *not* expire — "one cycle only" is about the benefit cycle, never about billing stopping, so no sweep, no `active -> expired` transition and no second stopping rule. And **a Plan's own previews read the Plan's live flag**, because a catalogue preview is about the Plan as it stands now; the two legitimately differ for a member enrolled before the Plan's Auto Renew last changed. Do not add a second place that decides whether the cycle repeats, a `COALESCE` onto the live policy, a per-cycle duration column, an expiry for a non-renewing contract, or a display-only repetition — a preview that repeats a cycle the run does not is the drift #635 stage 12 exists to prevent.
- A **simulation** of what a Plan will bill is a projection over that classifier, never a second copy of it (#818). The Membership Plan card's **Example Timeline** — the section *reads* **Membership Fee Simulation** since #962, on the Plan card, the Promotion card and the Assigned Plan card alike (`section_fee_simulation` in all three namespaces; the projection's own names — this module, the `example_timeline` field and `components/ExampleTimeline.tsx` — are deliberately unchanged, and a heading is not a reason to rename one) — `api/src/domain/planExampleTimeline.ts`, embedded by `enrichPlan` as `example_timeline` and served by `GET /membership-plans/:id/example-timeline` — steps one row per billing period with `advanceBillingDate()` over the Plan's stored `billing_policies` pair (a pre-#820 cadence included, stepped exactly as stored) and takes each row's Status from `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()` at that row's own start date. Since #892 the classifier counts those durations in the very cadence these rows step by, so a row's status is the whole row's status: a 4-weekly Plan with Free Period = 2 shows exactly **two** free rows. (It showed three until #892, because the third 4-week period still started inside two free calendar months; the ticket reverses that in the nightly run as well as in the preview, so the table still cannot advertise a charge the run does not make.) It replaces #485's Billing Events Forecast, which read price × cadence and never looked at Billing & Duration — `domain/billingForecast.ts` is gone; do not reintroduce a Plan projection that ignores the durations, a second place that decides which period is Free/Pre-paid/Pay/Bonus, or any tax arithmetic in the page (the row carries the server's VAT-inclusive `amount` plus a `waived` flag, and a Plan with no price reads `—`, never €0.00) — since #946 the **first** Pre-paid row is the one row of a configured duration that charges: it carries `price × pre-paid periods` with `waived: false` and the `prepaidPeriods` count, because the table must not read `No charge` for the very date the Billing Event Simulation beside it bills. Nothing is persisted and nothing is charged. The table itself is the shared `apps/admin/src/components/ExampleTimeline.tsx`, rendered by the Plan card, the Promotion card and — since #924 stage 3 — the Assigned Plan card, and holding no entity knowledge — a second simulation design for one of them is what the ticket forbade; each page keeps its own status labels (the Plan's read *(benefit)*, never *(promotion)*) and its own Billing cell. Since #1130 that walk has a **second** stopping rule beside the trailing-regular one, because a repeating cycle never reaches a regular period and would otherwise run every renewing Plan to the row cap: `timelineCycleFor()` answers the cycle to bound by (off `PlanDuration.repeats` alone, never re-derived) and such a table stops after `REPEATED_CYCLE_ITERATIONS = 2` complete iterations with its last row **closed**, since "Bonus, from X onwards" is the same lie the walk already refuses when the budget cuts a duration short — what says the cycle carries on is the marker beside the table, not an open-ended period. Since #1130 stage 2 that cycle is also **reported** (`ExampleTimelineResult.cycle`), and `timelineCycleFor()` answers for a non-repeating one too (`repeats: false`, `iterations: 1`) while still bounding the walk only where it repeats, so the stopping rule above is unchanged and the table can group its rows and say whether the cycle starts again. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The grouping lives in the admin's one JSX-free module (`apps/admin/src/lib/exampleTimeline.ts`): `exampleTimelineRowCycle()` says which iteration a row sits in and where a line segment ends, `exampleTimelineCycleNote()` says which marker goes under the table (`repeats` | `once` | `null`), and a card that counted its own iterations is the thing to refuse — it could group rows the nightly run bills differently, which nothing at runtime notices because a tidy second cycle renders perfectly well. The number is the **real** iteration rather than a relabelled `1`/`2`, since an assignment's rows start at the period containing today and a contract on its third cycle must not claim to be at its beginning. A cycle that does **not** repeat is grouped too — `1` beside the configured stretch and `✓ One cycle only — no renewal` under it, the owner's own wording for `Auto Renew = No` — with its trailing regular periods deliberately **outside** the grouping: they are the contract's regular price, not a second iteration, so they carry neither a number nor a line. And the marker's sentence, its `↻` / `✓` glyph included, is each card's own locale key (`timeline_cycle_repeats` / `timeline_cycle_once` in `plans` and `assigned_plans_page`), because the shared table is handed a rendered note and may not choose between the two — the same split that keeps it free of entity knowledge. The look is `ExampleTimeline.tsx`' alone: the `Cycle` column left of Period, `TIMELINE_CYCLE_LINE`/`TIMELINE_CYCLE_TEXT` exported beside the three tones so the Billing Event Simulation's own grouping reuses them rather than spelling a second hex, a 1px line per iteration with a gap at the foot of its last row (the two never touch) and **no** horizontal divider. Rows carrying no cycle — a Promotion's timeline — render the four columns exactly as before. Do not add a second place that decides which iteration a row belongs to, a page-local cycle number, a horizontal divider or a boxed container between iterations, a marker the table chooses for itself, or a displayed iteration the engine did not report. The gate is `api/src/test/plan-cycle-simulation.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Since #924 stage 3 the **projection** is shared the same way: the walk (period stepping, the trailing-regular horizon, the cycle horizon, the row cap, the open-ended final row) is `api/src/domain/exampleTimeline.ts` and each entity owns only an adapter — `planExampleTimeline.ts` for a Plan's single price, `assignmentExampleTimeline.ts` for an **Assigned Plan's Membership Fee Simulation** (`example_timeline` on `GET /user-memberships/:id`, and `GET /user-memberships/:id/example-timeline`), which prices every row through `resolveMembershipFee()` over the very `MembershipFeeContext` `priceMembershipFeeOn()` builds (`membershipFeeContextFor()`), so an applied Promotion, the frozen Billing & Duration and the Personal Membership Fee Benefit are all in the numbers and the table cannot disagree with the nightly run. A row's Status is **reported by the resolver** (`ResolvedCharge.periodStatus`), never re-classified by a caller: the precedence between a governing Promotion and the Plan's own durations is `resolveMembershipFee()`'s decision alone (#635's Q2 answer), and a surface that asked `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()` itself would label a cycle `free_plan` that the Promotion is charging the regular price for. An assignment's rows start at the period containing **today** while their numbers count from `starts_at`, and an assignment with no cadence reads `available: false` exactly as a Plan with no billing policy does. A third caller adds an adapter, never a second walk, a second status decider or a second Billing-cell formatter (`apps/admin/src/lib/exampleTimeline.ts`). Since #915 the Plan card carries a **second** projection beside it, and the two answer different questions on purpose: the Example Timeline is one row per billing *period* and is about the Membership Fee alone, while the **Billing Event Simulation** is one group per billing *date*, listing every line that falls on it — the fee plus each Product the Plan carries. Neither replaces the other and neither may grow into the other. `api/src/domain/planBillingEventSimulation.ts` is that second projection, and it is a **thin adapter over the engine, never a second one**: it expresses the Plan as the hypothetical assignment a member enrolling today would hold and hands it to `computeBillingSimulation()`, then does exactly two things of its own — supply the horizon floor and re-group the engine's cadence sections by date. A path that computes a date, an amount or a period status itself instead of reading the engine's is the thing the ticket forbade. The hypothetical assignment carries no Promotions, no Additional Periodic Services and no Personal Membership Fee Benefit (none is a property of a Plan, and inventing one advertises a price no member is promised) and it is open-ended, because a Paid Duration says how long the contract runs and never when billing stops. The horizon is `minimumCycles` on `BillingSimulationInput` — at least `stream.start + N x cadence` for *every* recurring stream, `SIMULATED_CYCLES = 2` — so the length is derived from the frequencies present and never from a month count, and it only ever *raises* the horizon, leaving #629 §6's first-regular-charge rule intact. Do not convert two frequencies to a common cadence to make them share a group (each item is its own stream; a shared *date* is what a shared group means), do not drop a `waive`d line because it costs 0 (it is a billing event, shown at €0 beside its regular price), and do not put tax arithmetic in the projection or the page: the whole thing runs in **VAT-inclusive euros**, grossed up once by `enrichPlan` through `computePriceFields()` with the stored amount as the fallback for an item that has no rate. That conversion is exact only because a Plan may configure `no_benefit`/`waive` and nothing else (#896 §16, narrowed to the two by #997) and because the `percentage_discount` a pre-#997 line may still carry is proportional too, so a Plan-side action that takes an *amount* would have to be priced net — which is another reason §16 is the CHECK's to enforce and not the dropdown's. Since #922 that projection is **shared**: its result shape, its `SIMULATED_CYCLES` horizon floor and its group-by-date re-grouping live in `api/src/domain/billingEventSimulation.ts`, and each entity owns only the adapter that expresses itself as a hypothetical assignment — `planBillingEventSimulation.ts` and `promotionBillingEventSimulation.ts`, rendered by one frontend component (`apps/admin/src/components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` over the JSX-free `apps/admin/src/lib/billingEventSimulation.ts`), with the labels resolved in each page's own namespace. A **Promotion's** Billing Event Simulation (`GET /promotions/:id/billing-event-simulation`) is therefore the same projection over a different context: the Products it grants, each at the price its grant's own pair gives the units or periods that grant covers and at the regular price after them, open-ended because a Promotion reaching its End Date never revokes a standing application (#900), and anchored on its own `starts_at` when that is still in the future. It carries **no Membership Fee line** — a Promotion has no fee of its own, the fee belongs to whichever Plan it is applied alongside, and inventing one advertises a price no member is promised; what a Promotion does to a fee is the Example Timeline beside it. Since #924 stage 4 that third surface exists and is an **Assigned Plan's Billing Event Forecast** (`billing_event_simulation` on `GET /user-memberships/:id`, and `GET /user-memberships/:id/billing-event-simulation`): `api/src/domain/assignmentBillingEventSimulation.ts`, the one adapter that does **not** invent its input — it is handed the real `SimulationAssignment` the Member-level Billing Simulation is built from (`loadSimulationAssignments()` in `api/src/api/billing-simulation.ts`, scoped to one assignment), so the snapshot rules, the standing Promotion applications, the Additional Periodic Services and the Personal Membership Fee Benefit are all in it by construction and a second read of that configuration is what would let the card disagree with the nightly run. It adds exactly two things, both about *where the projection runs* and neither about what anything costs: the horizon is anchored on **today** (`horizonFrom` on `BillingSimulationInput`, counted from each stream's first occurrence at or after it — for a hypothetical assignment that is the date its streams already start on, so the Plan and Promotion previews are unaffected), because a floor and a 36-month cap measured from a three-year-old `starts_at` would answer with an empty forecast; and the groups already behind us are dropped (`from` on `groupBillingEventsByDate()`), because those are the dates the assignment has been charged on and the **Billing Events** ledger on the same card is the record of them. The engine still walks the contract from its own `starts_at` — that is what decides which period a future date falls in — so its amounts are the ones actually billed: the frozen fee `priceMembershipFeeOn()` prices each cycle from and each line's frozen `unit_price`, the very figures the Membership Fee Simulation above it quotes, never a second gross-up. No line of an assignment is flagged **Mandatory** (#832/#893 is a Membership Plan's question, and a frozen line is not the live catalogue's to label). A fourth surface adds an adapter and a caller, never a second engine, a second grouping or a second component. Since #955 that one component renders the section as **one collapsible card per billing date** — the header carries the date, how many lines fall on it and that date's total (so there is no `Total` row under the lines any more), the lines appear underneath when the card is open, and a single *Expand all* / *Collapse all* control sits above them, rendered only when there is more than one period. The detail table is the **Example Timeline's own** language rather than a second one (#955: "do not introduce a separate visual language", "do not introduce new colors specifically for this component"): `ExampleTimeline.tsx` exports its cells and its three tones (`timelineThStyle`/`timelineTdStyle`, `TIMELINE_TONE_BACKGROUND`/`TIMELINE_TONE_TEXT`) and this component imports them, so a colour changed there reaches both tables and neither page declares a hex of its own. Which tone a line reads in is `simulationLineTone()` in the shared lib, off the treatment the server reported and never off the amount — €0.00 is also what an item with no price costs — and which cards start open is `initialExpandedPeriods()`: the **first** period, every other closed. Expanding is presentation and nothing else: it fetches nothing, recomputes nothing and the per-date totals stay the server's. A fourth consumer therefore gets the cards for free and must not grow a layout, a palette or an expand state of its own. Since #1130 stage 3 those cards are **grouped by the cycle** the Membership Fee Simulation above them groups its rows by, and the grouping decides nothing: each `BillingEventDate` carries the iteration it falls in (`planDurationCycleIteration()`, `api/src/domain/planDuration.ts` — the one place that answers it for a *date*, as the row rule answers it for a period) and the projection carries the cycle itself, the very `timelineCycleFor()` value the fee table reports, so #1130 §3's "the two simulations should tell exactly the same story" is structural rather than a convention. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **A repeating cycle is the projection's horizon**: such a contract never reaches a regular charge (stage 1), so #629 §6's first-regular rule had nothing to find and every renewing Plan ran to the engine's `MAX_SIMULATION_MONTHS = 36` and reported itself `truncated` — `horizonUntil` on `BillingSimulationInput` is that span instead, `timelineCycleHorizon()` over the **same** `timelineCyclePeriods()` count the fee table bounds its rows by (so a cycle longer than 36 months is shown rather than cut, the safety cap being raised to contain it, and both projections clamp at the one `MAX_TIMELINE_PERIODS` budget), and it **bounds** #915's per-stream `minimumCycles` floor rather than being bounded by it, because a yearly Product must not add a card from a third iteration the rows beside it do not list. A contract whose cycle does **not** repeat passes no `horizonUntil` at all, so every horizon rule that existed before the ticket is untouched. **The gutter is a presentation layer over the existing cards** (§2): no table, no bordered container, no horizontal divider and no redesigned card — one `1`/`2` marker and one 1px line per iteration, from `TIMELINE_CYCLE_LINE`/`TIMELINE_CYCLE_TEXT` imported from `ExampleTimeline.tsx` rather than a second hex, with a gap at the foot of an iteration's last card so the two lines never touch. **Where a run ends is all the frontend works out**: `billingEventCycleCells()` (`apps/admin/src/lib/billingEventSimulation.ts`) reads each group's server-given iteration and answers only which card carries the number and which one closes the line — the cell shape is the fee table's own `ExampleTimelineRowCycle`, because it is one grouping language over two layouts — and a card the engine put in no iteration (the regular dates a non-repeating contract settles into) carries neither. And the marker under the cards is the **page's** rendered note, the same `timeline_cycle_repeats` / `timeline_cycle_once` key the table beside it resolves, so one card says one thing twice and the component chooses nothing; a **Promotion** has no Billing & Duration, so it reports `cycle: null`, passes no note and renders exactly as it did before. The gate is `api/src/test/plan-cycle-billing-events.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not add a second place that decides which iteration a billing date falls in, a page-local cycle number, a table or a bordered container around the cards, a divider between iterations, a marker the component chooses for itself, or a month-count horizon for a cycle that repeats.
- **The member's own view of what they will be charged is that same projection, scoped to them** (#1123). `GET /me/billing-event-forecast` is `assignedPlanBillingForecast()` over the caller's own assignment — the Assigned Plan card's Billing Event Forecast and nothing new — so the snapshot rules (#635 §13–§17), each application's own grant snapshot (§16), the Additional Periodic Services and the Personal Membership Fee Benefit (#772) are in the member's numbers by construction, and the Payments card cannot quote a total the nightly run will not charge. `api/src/api/me-billing-forecast.ts` is the whole of the API half, and the member is **never named by a request** (`resolveMemberId()`, #1036's rule). Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Which `user_memberships` row is "the member's" is one place**: `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_ORDER`, read by `GET /me/membership` and by the forecast, because a second spelling of that ordering is how the card comes to name one plan while the forecast underneath projects another. **Next Payment is the forecast's first group** and *Forecast Billing Events* is the rest of it (`nextPaymentDate()` / `forecastDatesAfterNext()`), so the next charge is listed once and the two subcards cannot disagree; the horizon stays the engine's `SIMULATED_CYCLES = 2` per recurring stream, never a month count of the page's own. **A past event's status is the event's, never the date's**: `GET /me/billing-events` reports `status` from `deriveBillingEventStatus()` (#640/#787, the one implementation the staff ledger reads), and the card shows it per line, because one date can carry a settled charge and a rejected one and a card-level verdict would have to call one of them the other. And the Members App half is the two-module split `memberChrome.ts` already implies: `apps/member/src/lib/memberPayments.ts` **decides** (the grouping, the locale **keys**, the formatting — it resolves no `t()` and computes no money) and `components/MemberPaymentsCard.tsx` **draws** (one Billing Event card for the past and the forecast alike, since §4/§5 ask for one structure; expanding fetches nothing, #955; no colour outside `memberChrome.ts`, #983 — so a forecast card is distinguished by the *absence* of the past card's status pill plus an `info`-toned badge, never by a hue invented for a projection). The page's own *Upcoming payments* and *Payment history* sections are gone, absorbed by that card; `upcoming_payments` stays on `GET /me/membership` for any other caller. The title is **not** this ticket's: *My Products and Services* is #1121 §1's, one rename in one place — and the **route is nobody's**, since §1 asks for the section's name and a label never renames an identifier (#1026/#970), so the page is still `/membership`. The gate is `api/src/test/member-payments.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not add a second member-facing projection, a page-local total or amount, a second place that decides which assignment is the member's, a card-level payment status, or a Payments surface that reads the ledger or the forecast for itself.
- **Which Products a member may be shown is the gym's own two columns, and one place says so** (#1121 stage 1). `GET /me/products` is the **Additional Products and Services** subsection of the Members App's *My Products and Services* page, and it is a read of `products` — §7's "do not create a separate product catalogue for Members" taken literally, so there is no member-facing table, no `member_purchasable` flag and no migration. `api/src/domain/memberProductCatalogue.ts` is the one place the rule lives (`status = 'active'` **and** `enrollment_status = 'public'`, the thread's `Q1`) and `api/src/api/me-products.ts` the I/O half, the same two-module split #1123 used. Five of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The predicate is a **SQL fragment**, not a whole query, because stage 2 must validate a purchase against the very rule that decided what was offered — a member must not be able to buy what they cannot be shown, and two spellings of that is how it happens. `is_system` and `mandatory` are **not** filtered on: the first says a Product was seeded from `charge_types` and not that it is internal (a Locker Rental is a System row and a public one), the second is a Membership Plan question (#832/#893), and filtering on either would overrule what the gym configured. The **price is one figure**, `price_incl_tax` from `computePriceFields()` with the stored amount as the fallback for a gym with no tax rate (`grossBenefitUnitPrice()`'s own rule, #817: the arithmetic is the server's), beside a `tax_included` boolean that is `false` rather than a claim where no rate is configured — and an unpriced item is `null`, which reads `—` and is not €0.00. A Sessions package carries its **`units` to be said, never to divide by** (#942 — `amount` is the price of the whole package), so the member reads *Total price for 10 sessions* and no surface derives a per-session figure. And a catalogue the page could **not read is absent, not empty**: the route is behind `member_web.my_membership` **and** `financials.products` (#1036's reason — a gym that switched Products off did not mean "and offer them to members anyway"), and a 403 renders no subsection rather than claiming the gym offers nothing (#1073's "a control that cannot work is absent, never broken"). The Members App half is the same split one layer on: `apps/member/src/lib/memberProducts.ts` **decides** (which `membership.frequency` key a frequency reads under — the one map, #1128 — the package sentence, the tax note; it resolves no `t()` and computes no money) and `components/MemberProductsSection.tsx` **draws** (no `t()`, no colour outside `memberChrome.ts`, #983). A frequency that names no period — `once`, a legacy `per_session` — gets **no suffix**, because `€50 / Once` describes nothing (#1135's rule for a duration caption). The gate is `api/src/test/member-products.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not add a second place that decides which Products a member may see, a member-facing product table or flag, a per-session price, or a second frequency spelling in the Members App.
- **A purchase is a snapshot beside a link, and the payment is what grants it** (#1121 stage 2, migration 228). `member_products` is the row a member's bought Product lands in — the thread's `Q2` — and it carries what they were shown and charged (`product_name`, `product_type`, `billing_frequency`, `units`, `amount` **VAT-inclusive**, `currency`, `tax_rate_percent`) beside `product_id`, the link to the live row: #635 §16's rule one table over, so a Product renamed, repriced or retired afterwards moves nothing about a purchase already made. `api/src/domain/memberProductPurchase.ts` is the one place that decides what buying means and `api/src/api/me-products.ts` the I/O half, stage 1's split unchanged. Five of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The purchase is read through the catalogue's own predicate** (`memberProductCatalogueSql()`), which is why stage 1 made it a fragment: a member must not be able to buy what they cannot be shown, and a `staff_only`, inactive or deleted Product is a 404 on `POST /me/products/:id/purchase` exactly as it is absent from `GET /me/products`. **Stage 2 buys a one-off**: `Q3`'s recurring half — "create or update the billing event plan for such member" — is a second recurring schedule beside `user_memberships.next_billing_date` and therefore a change to what the nightly run charges, so a recurring Product (`isRecurringFrequency()`, #550, never a second list) answers `recurring_not_supported` and the Members App renders **no Buy action at all** for it (#1073: a control that cannot work is absent, never broken). **"Available" is the absence of a row**: §6's three states are the two the column holds plus none, `cancelled` is where a purchase whose payment failed or expired goes, and only a **pending** purchase blocks a second one — `mprod_pending_purchase_key` (migration 183's generated-column device) is UNIQUE over `(member_id, product_id)` while the row is `pending_payment`, so the route's 409 and the database agree, while an `active` purchase does not block, because nothing in `products` says an item may be bought once and a second session package months later is a real purchase. **The row is written before the member pays** and the webhook completes it: one transaction writes the `payment_requests` row (`source = 'product_purchase'`, the fifth widening of that CHECK, with `user_membership_id` now **nullable** because a purchase belongs to the member and under #956 they may hold no plan) and the purchase, so a closed tab loses nothing and a retried webhook completes one row (`UNIQUE (payment_request_id)` plus the `UPDATE`'s own `status = 'pending_payment'`, which is #1118 §10's idempotency). And **a purchase is not a membership cycle**: its webhook branch writes a `payment_recorded` Billing Event with no `user_membership_id` (money arrived, so it belongs in the ledger) and deliberately stores **no card** (a one-off authorises one charge; #788 is where a card comes from), stamps **no** `next_billing_date` and clears **none** of #785's dunning counters — a rejected fee is still owed after a member buys a locker — while `GET /members`' `payment_status` and the member's own Pay-now prompt exclude the source, because that column and that prompt are about the **fee**. The hosted page is told which it is (`purpose: 'product_purchase'` from the source, never from the amount) so its consent sentence does not promise a recurring charge, and `POST /billing/cleanup` cancels a purchase whose payment is over (`cancelAbandonedPurchases()`), since the UNIQUE pending key would otherwise lock that member out of that Product for ever. The gate is `api/src/test/member-product-purchase.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, for the same reason). Do not add a second payment mechanism for a purchase, a second place that decides whether a Product may be bought, a card stored or a billing date stamped from one, a purchase granted before the provider confirms, or a recurring purchase before a ticket decides what its schedule is — the Promotions on a purchase are #1118's and the Admin-side view of them #1118 §12's.
- **A Member's Payment Status is the worst status across every billable concept** (#1235). `GET /members`' `payment_status` is no longer the latest `payment_requests` row of the member: `api/src/domain/memberPaymentStatus.ts` is the one place that decides it — each concept (an Assigned Plan, or a Product bought on its own) contributes its **latest** request, and the worst wins by `PAYMENT_STATUS_SEVERITY` (`failed` > `expired` > `pending` > `completed`), so a failed locker purchase reads `failed` even when the plan is paid. Only a `card_update` verification is excluded (#788: no money in it); a `product_purchase` is counted, reversing #1121 stage 2's exclusion for this column — the Pay-now prompt is unchanged and still about the fee alone. It is independent of Clerk, enrollment and entitlement. Do not add a second aggregation in a screen or a second severity list.
- **`created_by` / `modified_by` / `deleted_by` are plain-text snapshots, resolved in one place** (#1182 stage 1). `api/src/domain/auditActor.ts` is that place (pure): `Pedro` when Pedro acted, `Oscar (impersonating Pedro)` when the superadmin Oscar acted as him, `System` for a nightly run or webhook, `NULL` when nobody can be named (never invented). There is **no actor type and no foreign key** on these columns — not #799's `_by_name`/`_by_type` pair, which stage 2 converts — and the helpers (`resolveRequestActor()`, `resolvePlatformActor()`, `createdAuditValues()`, `modifiedAuditSet()`, `deletedAuditSet()`, `restoredAuditSet()`) are what every mutation path of an in-scope table calls, the timestamps coming from the database clock. A table joins `AUDITED_TABLES` in the stage that migrates it, and `audit-actor.unit.test.ts` then fails the build for any `INSERT INTO` it that omits `created_by`. Scope rule (#1182 Q2): append-only/ledger tables carry `created_at` + `created_by` only, global lookups are out, and a child table is excluded only when it has no business lifecycle of its own (`user_membership_promotions`, `member_personal_goals`, `member_training_plans` stay in). Stage 1 migrates no table. Do not add an actor-type column, an actor FK, or a second place that composes the text.
- **A Promotion on a member's purchase is the Promotion's own grant row, frozen onto the purchase** (#1118, migration 229). The thread's `Q4` refuses a second target relation outright — *"the Product selected inside the Promotion is already the source of truth for applicability"* — so there is no `promotion_products` table and none may be added: an offer **is** the grant the Promotion already carries for that Product, in the section `classifyProduct()` (#550) puts it in, with the `(action, value)` pair on it (#896) as the benefit. `api/src/domain/memberProductPromotion.ts` is the one place all of that is decided and `api/src/api/member-product-promotions.ts` the I/O half, the split #1121 stage 1 and #1123 use. Five of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **`promotions.applies_to` gets its first reader** (#926 — configuration only since migration 204): a Promotion about a Membership Plan is not an offer on a Product even when it grants one, and the window is the one every apply path already enforces (`lifecycle_status = 'active'`, `starts_at <= now <= ends_at`, compared in SQL so no DATETIME crosses a timezone conversion), with `only_applicable_for_new_members` read through `isNewMemberStatus()` — #927's Member-level answer, because a purchase configures no assignment to exclude. **An offer is only ever made where it prices the Product lower than its own price and above nothing**: a `no_benefit` grant changes nothing (and is the default every grant row carries unless somebody chose otherwise, so offering one would put an *Apply promotion* button under a price that cannot move), a grant that prices it to **nothing** has no payment to make — this flow buys a Product by paying for it (§9/§16), there is no free-grant path here and a `waive` grant belongs to an Assigned Plan's own application — and a `fixed_price` *above* the Product's price is a configuration the vocabulary permits and a promotion it is not. `chk_mprodp_amounts` backstops the last of the three, and is deliberately a **superset** of that rule (as is `chk_mprodp_action`): the narrow rule lives in the loader where it can change without an `ALTER`, and tightening either would foreclose a later free-grant path or break migration 203's mirror. **The price is `applyLineBenefit()`' at quantity 1** over the VAT-inclusive figure `computePriceFields()` produced — the one place a pair becomes an amount, the very function the billing engine's charge builders and the Promotion card's own *Final Price* call — so a member and a gym owner cannot be quoted two numbers, nothing here does tax arithmetic (#817) and a Sessions package is never divided by its `units` (#942). **The snapshot is written in the purchase's own transaction, and tapping *Apply promotion* persists nothing**: §7 binds it to "the resulting purchase", so there is nothing for it to hang off before one exists and every member who changed their mind would leave an orphan row — `POST /me/products/:id/purchase` takes a `promotion_id` and **only** that id, re-reads it through the very loader that produced the offer, prices the charge from the answer and writes both rows together, so a Promotion that lapsed in between answers `409 promotion_not_applicable` rather than charging a price the gym no longer offers. `member_products.amount` therefore stays what it has always been — the figure actually charged — and the regular price it was discounted from is the application's own `regular_amount`, which is also what makes §14 true by construction: the webhook's `payment_recorded` event carries `payment_requests.amount`. And **`duration_cycles` is a Periodic grant's own quantity** (#1135: that column *is* a Duration), `null` for a session or one-off grant, because a one-off Product has no billing cycles to express one in (`Q5`) — and under stage 2 every purchasable Product is non-recurring, so a caption is shown only where there is one rather than inventing *1 billing cycle*. The Admin half is §11–§13: the Member card's section was renamed **Products & Services** (the `members` namespace only — the Assigned Plan card's own section keeps *Additional Products*), and #1185 renamed that section and the tab beside it to **Products** / *Productos* / *Productes* — a label change only, the `products_services` tab id and the `section_additional_services` key untouched, `GET /members/:id/products` joins neither `products` nor `promotions` because §13 forbids resolving the current Promotion configuration to describe a historical purchase, and the treatment reads through `benefitTreatmentLabel()` in the `promotions` namespace so it is worded in the Promotion's own voice (§3) with no second vocabulary in `members`. The gate is `api/src/test/member-product-promotions.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not add a Promotion→Product target relation, a second place that decides which Promotions a member is offered, a stored discounted price or a "was it discounted" flag beside the purchase, a second application per purchase, a Promotion applied before a purchase exists, a price named by the client, a free or price-raising offer, or a surface that describes an applied Promotion from the live row.
- **A member's finished Membership Plans are the tail of the one ordering that decides their current one** (#1122 §7/§8). `GET /me/membership` answers `past_memberships` beside `membership`, and `api/src/domain/memberPlanHistory.ts` is the one place that splits them — a pure `splitMemberPlanHistory()` over the rows that query already reads with `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_ORDER` **and** `MEMBER_CURRENT_ASSIGNMENT_FILTER` (#1123/#1108, both halves, one statement). Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. It adds **no second "which plan is the member's"**: a `WHERE status = 'cancelled'` of its own would list the very assignment the card above names on the day a member's only plan is cancelled, and it would have to re-decide what a Draft is — as the tail of that one query, a Draft reaches neither half by construction. The history is **re-ordered by date, newest first**, which that ordering deliberately does not give (it groups by status before date, which is right for picking a current plan and wrong for reading a history). A past plan **carries no money and no action** (§8 — the section is historical visibility): a row is a name, a status and the day it ended (`ends_at`, else `closed_at`; `null` rather than a fall-back to its `starts_at`, which would tell a member their plan ended on the day it began), so no fee is resolved per row and the status is a muted line rather than the current plan's pill. And the card is **the Members App's one collapsible chrome**: `apps/member/src/components/MemberCollapsibleCard.tsx`, promoted out of #1123's Payments card so both wear it, with `lib/memberPlans.ts` deciding (the keys, the formatting — no `t()`, no money) and `components/PastMembershipPlansCard.tsx` drawing (no `t()`, no colour outside `memberChrome.ts`, #983); a member with no history gets **no card at all**, because a collapsed card that opens onto nothing is vertical space spent saying nothing, and expanding fetches nothing (#955). The gate is `api/src/test/member-past-plans.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not add a second place that decides which assignment is the member's, a client-side filter that picks the past plans out of a list, a price, a billing date or an action on a past plan, or a second collapsible card or expand affordance in that app.
- **Save & Pay from the Members App writes the first Billing Event before the money moves, and a member never replaces a plan there** (#1288 stage 1). `POST /me/membership-plans/:id/assign` answers `409 plan_change_via_gym` for a member who holds a live plan (and `409 plan_pending_payment` for one awaiting payment) before creating anything — enforced in the API, not by hiding *Add Plan*; `confirm` is no longer read and the replacement dialog is gone from this path (staff replace through the Admin workflows). After `submitForPayment()` it calls `createPlanCheckout()` (`api/src/api/plan-checkout.ts`): one `payment_recorded` Billing Event (a pending transaction makes its derived status `pending`) plus the `payment_requests` row linked to it, returning `checkout_url`. The webhook's fee branch settles `payment_requests.billing_event_id` when it is set instead of inserting a second event, and `POST /me/payment-requests` (Pay now) on a `pending_payment` row links its request to the open event (`findOpenInitialEvent()`), so a retry never leaves a second pending one. Only the first charge is written; later ones stay the nightly run's. Do not persist the forecast, add a server-side draft, or let a client name an amount.
- **A member adds a Membership Plan through the staff assignment made self-service, and invents no rule of its own** (#1122 §1–§6). `GET /me/membership-plans` lists the active, public plans with the Promotions compatible with each — about a Membership Plan (`promotions.applies_to`), active, inside their window, targeting the plan through `promotion_membership_plans`, and offered only where their Membership Fee Benefit says something (`isOfferableFeeBenefit()` in `api/src/domain/memberPlanCatalogue.ts`: `waive` **is** offered here, unlike for a Product purchase, since a free first stretch is a real plan offer), with a new-members-only one hidden through `isNewMemberStatus()`. `POST /me/membership-plans/:id/assign` takes `promotion_ids` and `confirm` and nothing else — the member is never named by a request (#1036) and never names a price — and runs the staff path's own steps in the staff path's own places: `planAssignabilityError()`, `validatePromotionSelection()`, the Draft INSERT with `snapshotAssignedPlan()` and `recordStatusChange()` (`created_by_type = 'member'`), each Promotion through `applyPromotionToMembership()` (its own snapshot, §6), then #1108 stage 2's `submitForPayment()` / `commitAssignment()`, so the member lands in the same **Pending Payment** state a staff Save & Pay produces and pays it through the same *Pay now* (`POST /me/payment-requests` picks a pending row first; `GET /me/membership` reports it as `pending_membership`). Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The one-plan rule is the member's too**: a live plan answers `409 active_plan_exists` and `confirm: true` replaces it at activation, exactly as for staff, and a Draft the commit refused is cancelled rather than left behind. **The preview price is the first cycle under the benefit alone** (`firstCycleFinalPrice()`, over `applyPeriodBenefit()`); the authoritative fee is the assignment's own once it exists. And **the duration reads in months**, the Membership Fee Benefit's unit, never relabelled as cycles. The Members App half is the two-module split: `apps/member/src/lib/memberPlanCatalogue.ts` decides (keys, formatting, which Promotion is applied is page state until the assignment writes its snapshot) and `components/MemberPlanCatalogue.tsx` draws (no `t()`, no colour outside `memberChrome.ts`). Do not add a member-side assignment that bypasses the staff path's validations, a price named by the client, a Promotion applied before the assignment exists, or a second place that decides which plans or Promotions a member may be offered.
- The **Personal Membership Fee Benefit** (#772, migration 192) is the one fee benefit bounded by nothing. It lives on the assignment — `user_memberships.personal_fee_benefit_action` (`no_benefit` | `percentage_discount`) + `personal_fee_benefit_value` — and `resolveMembershipFee()` applies it **last, to every cycle, for the whole life of the assignment**: it *stacks* with an applied Promotion's Membership Fee Benefit rather than being outranked by it, and it survives that Promotion ending, which is the entire point of the ticket. `personalFeeBenefit` is therefore a **required** field of `MembershipFeeContext`, so a new pricing path cannot forget it; read it through `toPersonalFeeBenefit()` (`api/src/domain/personalFeeBenefit.ts`), which clamps to 0..100 because `user_memberships` carries no CHECK (migrations 174 and 189 declined one on that table — `ADD CONSTRAINT` rebuilds it under `ALGORITHM=COPY`). It is **not part of the Assigned Plan snapshot**: not in `has_billing_snapshot`, not in `snapshot_captured`, and `PUT /user-memberships/:id/fee-benefit` deliberately does *not* call `materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot()` — the columns are NOT NULL with a default, so folding them in would answer "captured" for every row in the table and freeze the duration fallback for assignments that captured nothing. It is also **not a negotiated price** (`membership_fee_price` is still the regular fee it discounts *from*) and it is **not carried onto a superseding assignment** — `POST /:id/assign-new-plan` creates a different Assigned Plan at a renegotiated price. Do not add a second personal-benefit action to the *product* surface without deciding it in a ticket, a `discount_expires_at` beside it, or a stored discounted price.
- A Product configured **inside** a Promotion or a Membership Plan carries an `(action, value)` pair beside its quantity (#896, migration 203), and the pair belongs to that relationship alone — never to `products`, which is what lets the same item be waived by one Plan and discounted by a Promotion. The vocabulary is the existing `PromotionBenefitAction`, and `applyPeriodBenefit()` stays the one place a pair becomes an amount: `applyLineBenefit()` (`api/src/domain/productBenefitActions.ts`) only decides *what* amount to hand it — the **line** (`unit × quantity`), never the unit, per the ticket thread. The two option sets live in that module, are mirrored by the `chk_<table>_action` CHECK beside each of the twelve tables, and since stage 4 are mirrored again for the browser in `apps/admin/src/lib/productBenefitActions.ts`, so a new action goes in **three** places: a Promotion may configure all five (`no_benefit`, `waive`, `percentage_discount`, `fixed_discount`, `fixed_price`), a Membership Plan only the first **two** (§16 — a Plan cannot configure *Fixed discount* or *Fixed Price*, and the CHECK is what enforces that rather than the dropdown — and #997 retired *% Discount* from it as well, leaving a Plan benefit charged at the Product's own price or waived outright). `benefitConfigError()` is the one validation, mirrored in SQL by `chk_<table>_value`: an action that takes no value must carry none, a percentage is 0..100, an amount is non-negative. The default is `no_benefit` — a writer that does not name the column charges the normal price and can never invent a discount — and the six assignment-side snapshot tables carry the pair for the Assigned Plan rule, because a configuration that reached only the catalogue is one no existing assignment could have been agreed with: a path that **copies** a benefit row (`snapshotPromotionGrants()`, `snapshotAssignedPlan()`, `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()`, both `duplicate` routes) copies the pair with it, since billing reads the snapshot and never the live row. On the way in, `parseProductBenefitInput()` is the only place a request's pair is judged, and its third answer is the load-bearing one: a line the request did not *mention* keeps the pair it is stored with rather than resetting to the default, because the six section `PUT`s are replace-all and a client that sends only `product_id` + `quantity` would otherwise wipe migration 203's `waive` backfill and start charging for what a Promotion gives away — clearing a treatment is `action: 'no_benefit'`, explicitly. A read reports the pair through `shapeProductBenefitRow()` (`value` a number, not mysql2's `DECIMAL` string; an action the context may not **store** read back as the default — and `percentage_discount` on a Plan row read back as itself, per the retirement rule below). Since #896 stage 3 the pair is also what **prices** a Product line, in the Billing Simulation's two charge builders (`domain/billingSimulation.ts`) and nowhere else: the item's own treatment applies to the whole line and each covering Promotion grant's is folded on top for the periods or units that grant covers, so the pre-ticket "a Plan benefit is charged, a Promotion grant is free" is now just the `no_benefit`/`waive` case of one rule and a grant reports the action it actually applies (`waive`) rather than the retired `included`. Two properties are the rule: an item's **own** treatment never sets `promotional` — it never ends, so it *is* this contract's regular charge, and flagging it would run every discounted item to the projection's safety cap (#629 §6), exactly as #772 reasons about the Personal Membership Fee Benefit — and a grant configured `no_benefit` covers its periods without changing the price or the horizon. A one-off or session line allocates its units grant by grant in order, capped at the quantity billed, and prices whatever is left over with the item's own treatment. A loader that feeds the engine reads the pair through `toProductBenefit()` **in its own context**, and the field is required on `SimulationGrant` / `SimulationPlanBenefit` for the reason `personalFeeBenefit` is required: a path that forgot it would silently charge for what a Promotion gives away. Since stage 4 the pair is **configured** in exactly one editor, the shared `ProductBenefits.tsx`, which takes the context as a prop: `benefitContext="promotion"` offers all five options, `benefitContext="plan"` the two, and **omitting it renders no column at all** — which is what keeps the Assigned Plan snapshot sections as they were, since their `PUT` takes quantity alone. The value input is rendered only for an action that takes one and is named after that action, and a typed number is deliberately *kept* while the action changes, so `toBenefitItems()` is what submits only the value the selected action takes — and a draft line carrying no action at all still sends a quantity-only payload, for the "keeps what it is stored with" reason above. The **labels** are per context and are locale keys, never anything this module knows: the Promotions UI says *Promotion* / *No promotion* and must not contain the word "Benefit" anywhere (§3 — `product-benefit-actions-ui.test.ts` fails if a `promotions` label says it), while the Membership Plans UI says *Benefit* / *No benefit* for the very same stored `no_benefit`. Do not add a second action list, a second editor for the pair or a pricing field on the Product itself. A **per-benefit frequency** was forbidden here too until #918 decided one for **Session Benefits alone** (the bullet below): it is the renewal period of the sessions a Plan includes, never a second billing cadence for the pair and never a frequency on another section. Since #916 that pair is also what a Membership Plan's Benefit sections **display**: each row reports an `original_price_incl_tax` / `final_price_incl_tax` pair (plus the two `*_line_*` totals) from `api/src/domain/planBenefitPrices.ts`, which is `applyLineBenefit()` at quantity 1 and at the row's quantity and decides nothing else — the gross-up is `grossBenefitUnitPrice()` over `computePriceFields()`, shared with #915's loader, so the sections and the Billing Event Simulation beside them cannot quote one line two ways and no page does tax arithmetic (#817). Since #919/#920 that wiring is `api/src/api/product-benefit-pricing.ts` (`grossBenefitUnitPrice()` + `withProductBenefitPrices(context, …)`, over the single-row `productBenefitPrices()` both end at) and a **Promotion's** three sections are its second caller, reporting the same four fields as *Regular Price* / *Final Price* over the five actions a Promotion may configure — so a new surface showing what a configured line costs adds a caller with its own `context`, never a second gross-up or a second amount decider. Since #924 stage 1 an **Assigned Plan's** three snapshot sections are the third, and they are what that rule looks like for frozen rows: they render the very same grid through `ProductBenefitView` (`benefitContext="plan"`, `showPrices`, *Agreed Price* / *Final Price* in their own namespace) and hand the pricing module the line's **frozen** `unit_price` and its frozen pair rather than a `products.amount` join, so a Product repriced or a Plan re-configured since moves nothing (#635 §17) — the only live column such a loader reads is the item's tax treatment (`tax_behavior` + `tax_rates.rate_percent`, LEFT JOINed), because the snapshot never captured a statutory rate and that is the only way to answer "tax included" at all. The **editor** there stays quantity-only: a read-only card may report a column its editor does not configure, but it must never render a control the section's `PUT` cannot carry. Since #924 stage 2 an applied **Promotion's** three grant sections on that same card are the fourth, and the one thing that differs is the context: each grant is read in the **Promotion**'s option set (`benefitContext="promotion"`, all five actions — read as a Plan's two, a `fixed_discount` grant would normalize to `no_benefit` and the card would quote the full price for a line the member was promised at another one), priced from that application's own frozen `unit_price` and frozen pair (#635 §16 — never a live join against `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}`, and the live tables stay the fallback for an application that snapshotted nothing), and the Frequency column stays the item's own, because #918's renewal Frequency is a Membership Plan Session Benefit's and a Promotion grant has none. Both halves of that card share one locale namespace, so the **key** carries §3's voice rather than the namespace: the Plan sections keep `item_action_*` (*Benefit*) and the grant sections resolve `promo_item_action_*` (*Promotion* / *No promotion*) through the page's own translate wrapper — a shared key whose words differ per section is remapped there, never by a second grid or a second column declaration. That is a catalogue-side preview of what the Promotion grants and changes nothing about an **applied** Promotion, which is still priced against the assignment it is applied to, from that application's own snapshot (#635 §16). An item carrying no price reports `null` and reads as `—`, never €0.00. The three sections render from **one** column declaration, `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` in `apps/admin/src/components/ProductBenefits.tsx` (Product, Quantity, Frequency, Benefit, Original Price, Final Price — Benefit between Frequency and the prices, never after them), whose flags are the page's and not the section's: a column a section has no value for keeps its cell with a `—` rather than disappearing and shifting the columns after it, which is why `showFrequency` is true for all three Plan sections — and, since #919/#920, for all three Promotion sections, whose grid is that same declaration — and why `table-layout: fixed` is not optional there. The labels stay the page's: the shared `col_original_price` reads *Regular Price* in the `promotions` namespace and *Original price* in `plans`, exactly as `col_item_action` already reads *Promotion* and *Benefit*. Do not give a section its own grid, its own price formatter or a stored price column.
- A Membership Plan Product benefit's treatment is **`No benefit`, `Waive` or `% Discount`** (#1184 stage 1, reversing #997). #997 had retired `% Discount` from the Plan surface; #1184 offers it again, so `PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS` (`api/src/domain/productBenefitActions.ts`, mirrored in `apps/admin/src/lib/productBenefitActions.ts`) is those three and `LEGACY_PLAN_BENEFIT_ACTIONS` is **empty**. Nothing else changed: the section `PUT`s validate through `benefitConfigError()` (a percentage 0..100, an action that takes no value carries none), pricing is `applyLineBenefit()`, and **`Fixed discount` and `Fixed Price` stay Promotion-only** (#896 §16 — the CHECK and the route enforce it, not the dropdown). The offered-vs-stored split (`benefitActionsFor()` / `storedBenefitActionsFor()`, `isRetiredBenefitAction()`, `keepsRetiredBenefit()`) is kept as the pattern a later retirement reuses, so a new Plan action still goes in three places (the declaration, its admin mirror and the CHECK). No migration, no backfill; the `plans:percentage-benefits` report script is gone because no line is unconfigurable any more. Do not reintroduce a Plan-side `fixed_discount`/`fixed_price`, convert a stored percentage, or add a second place that decides which treatments a context offers.
- A Membership Plan Product benefit carries its own **Mandatory Yes/No** (#1184 stage 2, migration 237): `mandatory TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` on `membership_plan_{session,oneoff,periodical}` and on the assignment-side `user_membership_{session,oneoff,periodical}`, no CHECK (`ADD CONSTRAINT` would rebuild the tables under `ALGORITHM=COPY`). It is the Plan ↔ Product **relationship's** flag — `true` (every existing row, and every new one) means the member must keep the benefit at assignment, `false` that they may decline it — and it is **not `products.mandatory`** (#832/#893, which forces an item into every Plan) nor a Promotion line's `requirement` (#959). `parsePlanBenefitMandatoryInput()` (`api/src/domain/planBenefitMandatory.ts`) is the one place a request's value is judged, with #896's/#918's three answers: not mentioned **keeps** what is stored (so a quantity-only save cannot reset an optional line), an explicit boolean sets it, anything else is a 400, never a coercion. It is part of the agreement, so `snapshotAssignedPlan()`, `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` and Plan Duplicate copy it. In the admin it is the **last data column** of the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` (`… | Final Price | Mandatory | Actions`), behind a `showPlanMandatory` opt-in only the Plans page passes. **Declining is stage 3** (#1184): `POST /user-memberships`, `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan`, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign` and `POST /me/membership-plans/:id/assign` take `declined_benefits: [{ section, product_id }]`, and `api/src/domain/declinedPlanBenefits.ts` is the one pure rule (`declinedBenefitsError()`: an entry naming a benefit the Plan does not carry, or a `mandatory = true` one, is a 400 — never dropped quietly) with `api/src/api/declined-plan-benefits.ts` the SQL half every path calls through `resolveDeclinedBenefits()`. `snapshotAssignedPlan()` takes `declinedBenefits` and simply does not copy those lines, so billing and every projection read the accepted set and the source Plan is untouched. The Admin's `AssignPlanInlineEditor` shows a checkbox per benefit (disabled where mandatory, `lib/declinedBenefits.ts`); the Members App picker and the Plans page's bulk Assign still send none (every benefit stays) until their checkboxes land. Stage 3b: `GET /me/membership-plans` reports each plan's `benefits` (`loadNamedPlanBenefitLines()`), the Members App's Add Plan card draws a checkbox per benefit (`declinedBenefitsPayload()` in `lib/memberPlanCatalogue.ts`) and the Plans page's bulk Assign sends the same `declined_benefits` through `declinedPayload()`; stage 4 (§22): a non-empty decline is audited as its own `decline_benefits` event against the **`user_membership`** (`recordDeclinedBenefitsAudit()`, `api/src/api/declined-plan-benefits.ts`, called by all four assignment paths), never against the source Membership Plan, and nothing is written when nothing was declined. Do not add a second place that decides what may be declined or a client-only enforcement. Do not add a second flag, a client-only enforcement, or a decline path that bypasses that one rule.
- A Membership Plan's **Session Benefit** carries a renewal **Frequency** beside its quantity (#918, migration 205) — `Personal Training Class | 2 | Weekly | Waive` means two included classes *every week* — and it is the **Session section's alone**: `frequency` exists on `membership_plan_session` and on the assignment-side `user_membership_session`, on no other benefit table and on no Promotion grant. It has **its own accepted set**, not the Product's: `api/src/domain/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts` declares `once`, `week`, `four_weeks`, `month`, `year`, mirrored by the `chk_<table>_frequency` CHECK beside each table and again for the browser in `apps/admin/src/lib/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts`, so a new value goes in **three** places. `week` is offered here although #821 retired it from `products.billing_frequency`, because how often an allowance *renews* and how often an item is *priced* are different questions; `per_session` is not a period and is never offered. The column is **nullable with no default and no backfill**: `NULL` is the dropdown's `—` and is exactly what every Session Benefit written before the ticket means (a one-time allowance granted on the start date), `once` says the same thing deliberately, and the two are not to be collapsed. On the way in, `parseSessionBenefitFrequencyInput()` is the only place a request's value is judged and its three answers are #896's for #896's reason: a line the request did not *mention* **keeps** the Frequency it is stored with, because the section `PUT` is replace-all and a quantity-only save — the other two sections, the Assigned Plan snapshot editor, a mandatory item re-added by `withMandatoryBenefits()`, any client written before #918 — would otherwise clear it; clearing is the explicit `frequency: null` (or `''`), and anything else is a 400, never a coercion. It is **part of the agreement**, so `snapshotAssignedPlan()` copies it onto the assignment and `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` keeps it on a line it keeps: a Plan switched from Weekly to Monthly later must not move what an existing member was agreed (#635 §13–§17), and `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation()` reads the snapshot, falling back to the live Plan only for an assignment that captured none. In the **simulation** the allowance is *summarised onto the billing events that already exist*, never multiplied into new ones: `sessionFrequency` is a **required** field of `SimulationPlanBenefit` (for the reason `benefit` and `personalFeeBenefit` are — a loader that forgot it would show a member 2 sessions where their contract entitles them to 8), and a renewing value turns the item into a stream at the **assignment's own billing cadence** (`buildSessionAllowanceStream()`, `domain/billingSimulation.ts`) whose every occurrence reports `quantity × renewalsInPeriod(...)` — the renewals of one schedule anchored at `starts_at` that fall inside that cycle. So a 4-weekly Plan with `2 | Weekly` reads 8 sessions on each billing date, a monthly one reads the 5 renewals a 31-day cycle really contains and the 4 of the next (never a fractional 4.35), a cycle containing no renewal shows **no line** rather than "0 sessions", and `once`, `—` or an assignment with no cadence keep the single start-date charge unchanged. A Promotion's session grant still grants its units **in total** rather than per cycle: `allocateSessionUnits()` allocates the line's units as intervals and takes an offset for what earlier cycles consumed, which at offset 0 is the pre-#918 arithmetic to the cent. The admin renders **one** Frequency column, not a second one — the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` cell takes a `frequencyColumn` prop (`'item'`, the read-only Product frequency, everywhere by default; `'benefit'` for the Plans page's Session section and, since #924 stage 1, for the *read-only* Session section of the Assigned Plan card, which shows the Frequency that assignment was agreed) — and the Promotion sections stay on `'item'`. An Assigned Plan's Session **editor** stays on `'item'` too, because its `PUT` takes quantity alone and keeps the stored Frequency. What the Frequency does **not** do is the rest of the ticket's thread: there is no consumable allowance ledger, nothing tracks how many renewed sessions a member has used, and booking eligibility is still `activity_type_eligible_plans` plus the class-package claim. Do not add a per-session-benefit allowance table, a renewal writer, a second place that decides how many units a cycle grants, or a frequency on the other two sections or on a Promotion grant, without deciding it in a ticket.
- A Product configured inside a **Promotion** also carries a **Requirement** — `mandatory` or `optional` (#959, migration 207) — and it is the **Promotion's alone**: `requirement` exists on `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}` and on the three `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` tables, on no Membership Plan benefit table and not on `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` (the thread excludes Plans from the ticket, and the Membership Fee is a singleton with no Product to make optional). It is **not** `products.mandatory` (#832/#893), which is the *catalogue* item's own flag and means the item is forced into every Membership Plan benefit section: this one belongs to the Promotion ↔ Product **relationship**, exactly where #896 put the `(action, value)` pair and for that reason — the same item may be mandatory in one Promotion and optional in another — so the two never share a grid (a Promotion section passes no `enforceMandatory`) and neither may be derived from the other. `api/src/domain/promotionItemRequirement.ts` declares the pair of values, mirrored by the `chk_<table>_requirement` CHECK beside each table and again for the browser in `apps/admin/src/lib/promotionItemRequirement.ts`, so a new value goes in **three** places. The column is **NOT NULL, defaulted and backfilled to `mandatory`**, which is what every row written before the ticket means — an applied Promotion grants everything it configures and nothing offers the member a choice — so there is no `—` here and no unanswered state; letting an item be declined is the deliberate act. `parsePromotionItemRequirementInput()` is the only place a request's value is judged and its three answers are #896's for #896's reason: a line the request did not *mention* **keeps** what it is stored with, because the three section `PUT`s are replace-all and a quantity-only save would otherwise reset an optional item to mandatory; an unknown value is a 400, never a coercion, because `optional` and `mandatory` are opposite promises to the member. It is part of what was agreed, so `snapshotPromotionGrants()` and `POST /promotions/:id/duplicate` copy it verbatim (#635 §16 — the snapshot is what an application reads, never the live row). **Nothing reads it yet, and that is the whole ticket**: no pricing path, no projection, no apply path and no member-facing surface consumes it, and an optional grant is granted and priced exactly as a mandatory one. The member's own enabled/disabled choice, its billing effect, a Membership Plan counterpart and a Requirement on the Membership Fee are the promotion assignment process's and need a ticket — a path that acts on the flag beyond configuring, reporting and snapshotting it is deciding that behaviour. In the admin it is **one more entry** in the shared `PRODUCT_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` (after Benefit, before the two prices) behind a `showRequirement` opt-in only the Promotions page passes, in both halves of its card, rendered with the grid's existing inline `<select>`: do not add a second column declaration, a control of its own, a badge or a colour.

- Any new code path that prices, or displays, a Promotion **already applied** to a `user_memberships` row reads that application's own snapshot — `membershipFeeBenefitsFromSnapshot(ump.snapshot)` for the Membership Fee Benefit, the `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` rows (via `loadPromotionGrantSnapshots()`) for the granted Products — never a live join against `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` or `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}` (#635 §16, stage 7). The fee is re-resolved on every read, so a live read lets a Promotion edited in between reprice every assignment that already carries it. The live tables are the fallback for one case only: an application whose `snapshot` is NULL (applied before migration 149), which has nothing else to read.
- A Promotion is **never configured or assigned on a Member** (#931). It applies to a target — a **Membership Plan** or a **Product** (`promotions.applies_to`, #926) — and a Member benefits from it because of what they are assigned or buy: the Promotions a plan is assigned *with* (#628's section of the Assign New Plan editor, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`'s auto-apply) and the applications an Assigned Plan already carries, which the Assigned Plans card shows from each application's own snapshot (#635 §16). So the Member card has no `PROMOTIONS` section and no `+ Add Promotion`, and `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/configuration` reports `{plans, services}` and no `promotions` key — do not reintroduce either, a Member-level promotions list, or a Promotion picker on a Member surface. Everything *below* that UI is unchanged and stays the Assigned Plan's: `user_membership_promotions` and its snapshots, `POST`/`DELETE /user-memberships/:id/promotions`, `applyPromotionToMembership()`, `validatePromotionSelection()` and every pricing path. `new_member_eligible` is still reported per plan on that read, because it is a property of the Member and the apply path is its enforcement point either way.
- A Member's **`New Member` status** is one rule with one window, and it is **derived on read, never stored** (#927). `NEW_MEMBER_WINDOW_MONTHS` in `api/src/domain/newMemberEligibility.ts` is **6** — "not had an active Membership Plan during the previous 6 months" — and that same number governs Promotions, per the ticket thread's *"6 months here and in promotions"*: a display window differing from the enforcement window would show `New Member ☑` beside an apply that refuses, which is the drift §5 exists to prevent, so do not add a second window, a display-only variant or a stored/`is_new_member` column. The Member-level answer passes **no** exclusion (`qualifiesAsNewMember(..., null)`), so a Member holding a live plan is not new; the four Promotion apply paths still exclude the assignment being configured. A reader uses `api/src/api/new-member-eligibility.ts` — `isNewMemberStatus()` for one Member, `newMemberStatusByMember()` for a page of them in **one** query (never one round trip per row, and never a second SQL copy of the rule). On the admin side it is a **calculated field of the Member Profile**: `calculated: true` on its `MEMBER_PROFILE_FIELDS` spec, rendered by `MemberProfileLayout`'s own `renderCalculated` callback in *both* modes, absent from `MemberEditFormValues` and from the `PUT` payload, and drawn as a value rather than a disabled checkbox — a calculated field that reached `renderField` could grow an input, which is the one way this goes wrong. Since #960 that value is a compact **Yes/No chip on the label's own row** (`MemberProfileLayout`'s inline cell for a `calculated` field, which an editable field never takes — its stacked label-above-control box is where its `<input>` sits in the other mode): the chip declares no colour or shape of its own, `newMemberChipStyle()` spreading `listNameBadgeAccentStyle` for `Yes` — the very pill the list puts beside a new member's name — and `listNameBadgeStyle` for `No`, and its `aria-label` keeps the full sentence (`new_member_yes`) so a screen reader does not hear a bare "Yes" beside a label that is not tied to it. Both keys come from `newMemberValueKey()` / `newMemberAnnounceKey()` in `memberProfile.ts`, so neither half of the card picks its own wording, and no glyph, second pill or Profile-local colour may come back. The list badge is `listNameBadgeAccentStyle` over the same `is_new_member` field (#913), never a second calculation in the page.
