- Any new code path that applies a Promotion to a `user_memberships` row must honour `promotions.only_applicable_for_new_members` via `isNewMember()` (`api/src/api/new-member-eligibility.ts`), passing the assignment being configured as the excluded one — it must never disqualify its own Member. The column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, so every Promotion carries the flag unless someone cleared it: a path that skips the check hands new-member Promotions to members who never left (#634 §3).
- A `user_membership_promotions` row is one **application** of a Promotion, and an assignment may hold several of the same Promotion over time (#635 stage 9, migration 183): only one of them may be *standing*, which `ump_one_standing_per_promotion` (the `standing_promotion_key` generated column, non-NULL while `status = 'applied'`) enforces. Revoking is therefore reversible — a code path that re-applies inserts a **new** row with its own snapshot of the Promotion as it stands now, and never resurrects the revoked one: that row's snapshot is what it was agreed with (§16) and its `[applied_at, revoked_at]` window is what tags the Billing Events ledger, so rewriting either falsifies what the member was already billed. Anything that asks "is this Promotion on this assignment?" filters `status = 'applied'`, and anything keyed per application (a React list key, a snapshot table's FK, a grants map) keys on `user_membership_promotions.id`, never on `(user_membership_id, promotion_id)`.
- A meal item's **`component_type`** (the admin's **Food Type**) has two accepted sets and they are not the same: a template meal item takes seven (`chk_nptmi_component_type`, migration 111) and an **assigned** plan's takes four (`chk_mnpmi_comp`, migration 105 — no `drink`/`dessert`/`other`, since #294 widened the template table only). Both live once in `api/src/domain/nutritionComponentTypes.ts`, are validated through `isComponentType()` and are served by `GET /component-types` on each of the three routers, so a new value goes in **two** places — the list and the CHECK beside it — and the selector can never offer one the CHECK refuses (register that route **before** `/:id`, or Express reads its name as an id). A Nutrition Library food's own type is its **`categories`** list, not a column: migration 142 (#501) replaced the scalar `nutrition_library_items.category` with the `nutrition_library_categories` catalogue plus an M2M junction, and a frontend still reading `.category` gets `undefined` for every food — which is what put `nutrition_plan_templates.tree_component_type_undefined` in the Food Type selector (#812). Relatedly: **next-intl's `t()` has no `defaultValue` option**. It takes interpolation values, and a missing key prints verbatim, so `t(`x_${v}`, { defaultValue: v })` renders the key rather than the fallback — decide the label before calling `t()`, as `foodTypeLabel()` and `resultTypeLabel()` do.
- The Nutrition Library holds **three** catalogues, and two of them are the goal ones #947 added: **Personal Goals** (what the member wants to achieve) and **Nutrition Goals** (what the plan should target nutritionally). They are separate concepts in the data model as well as the UI — `personal_goals` and `nutrition_goals` (migration 206), never one table with a `kind` column and never one merged list — and `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts` is the one place the kinds, their tables, their audit entity types and the seeded System rows are declared, which is what lets **one router factory per side** serve both (`api/src/api/goal-library.ts` mounted at `/personal-goals` and `/nutrition-goals`, `platform-goal-library.ts` at the `/platform` pair). Ownership is the Foods library's and may not be forked: `gym_id IS NULL` is a platform-owned **System** row, administered from Cordel and answering 403 on a gym's write, a set `gym_id` is that gym's own, soft delete is `status = 'deleted'`, and the actor pairs are #799's snapshot (reuse `actorSnapshot()` / `itemDetailColumnsSql()` / `normalizeDescription()` from `domain/nutritionLibrary.ts` rather than restating them). Two properties are load-bearing: uniqueness is among **live** rows only (the `live_name_key` VIRTUAL generated column carries the UNIQUE index, migration 183's device), so a name a gym deleted is re-usable and the index agrees with the routers' own duplicate check; and `slug` is a **seeded** row's label handle and only a System row's (`chk_<prefix>_slug_system_only`) — the seven slugs of each kind resolve `goal_library.<kind>_goal_<slug>` with the row's own `name` as the fallback, decided before `t()` is called, which is why these catalogues need no per-locale junction table and why **neither router ever writes a slug**. A new System goal therefore goes in **three** places: `SYSTEM_GOALS` in that module, the migration's seed list and the admin's `SYSTEM_GOAL_SLUGS` mirror (plus its locale key in en/es/ca). Since #1034 a **Personal Goal is also measurable** — `personal_goals.target_value` + `target_unit` (migration 218), carrying the two CHECKs `member_personal_goals` already had (a unit with no value is refused; a value with no unit is incomplete rather than contradictory and stays allowed, one direction only) — and `nutrition_goals` is deliberately untouched, because the two tables exist so they can diverge and a Nutrition Goal's own target values are still a later ticket's. `MEASURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` in that same module is the one place that says which kinds have the pair, asked once by each router rather than branched on per route (that table has no such columns, so projecting them would answer ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR the global handler turns into a bare 500, #966) and mirrored for the browser in `apps/admin/src/components/goalLibrary/goalProfile.ts` — so making a second kind measurable goes in **three** places: that declaration, the two columns plus their CHECKs on the kind's table, and the mirror. What a target *is* lives in `api/src/domain/goalTarget.ts`, which `domain/personalGoalAssignment.ts` **re-exports rather than re-implements**, because #1034 §1 forbids a second, incompatible unit system and a target the catalogue accepted that the assignment refused would be exactly that; the unit is deliberately **free text** (there is no measurement-unit vocabulary in this codebase to reuse — the only `*_UNITS` lists are billing periods — so declaring one here would *be* that second system). Migration 218 seeds a target onto the four System Personal Goals whose meaning carries a magnitude (Weight Loss 3 kg, Weight Gain 3 kg, Muscle Gain 2 kg, Maintenance 0 kg — a change of zero *is* the goal) and **none** onto Performance, Recovery and Energy, because a number invented for a qualitative goal is the "same generic target" §2 forbids wearing a different value and §13 requires a unit only "for measurable goals" — `—` is a legitimate target, so do not backfill one, and the seed fills a row only while it has no target yet, so a value Cordel changed later is never overwritten. `SYSTEM_PERSONAL_GOAL_TARGETS` mirrors the migration's `SEED_TARGETS` and the unit test asserts the two agree. The gym's Personal Goals page also carries **`⋮ → Assign goal to member`** (#1034 §4/§5) as an opt-in `onAssign` prop of the shared section, so the action is **absent** from Cordel's Base library, which has no gym whose members it could name, and the dialog behind it (`apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/AssignGoalToMemberModal.tsx`, over the app's own `CrudModal` and `formChrome`) is the page's rather than the section's — it preselects the goal as a *value*, pre-fills the target from the catalogue, keeps the unit editable (§5: follow the existing business rules, and `target_unit` has been staff-set since migration 212) and submits through the **same** `toAssignedPersonalGoalCreatePayload`/`assignedPersonalGoalFormError` the inline assignment form uses, so it is a narrower view of one create form and not a second one. Do not add a second measurable-kind list, a unit enum, a per-session or derived target, or a second way to assign a goal. Since #1035 stage 2 a **Personal Goal also carries an image** — `personal_goals.image_url` (migration 225) — and `IMAGE_GOAL_KINDS` in that same module is the one place that says which kinds have it, asked once by each router exactly as `MEASURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` is: `nutrition_goals` has no such column, so projecting it there would answer ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR and a bare 500 (#966), and the two image routes are **registered only for a kind that has one**, so a Nutrition Goal's `/:id/image` is a 404 rather than a control that writes nowhere. `api/src/domain/personalGoalImages.ts` is the one place that decides *where* the object goes, *what is accepted* and *what may be deleted*, and it is **one module for both roots** rather than the two exercises have, because a Personal Goal is one table holding both kinds of row: `<gym prefix>/goals/<goal_id>-<name>.png` for a gym's own and `cordel/goals/<goal_id>-<name>.png` for a System one, with `goals/` spelled only as `GOALS_FOLDER` (`api/src/infra/storage.ts`, where the gym's first-level marker is written from the same string — a second spelling would differ in case, and in R2 that is a different key). `POST`/`DELETE /personal-goals/:id/image` and the `/platform` pair are the only writers, each answering for its own rows (403 on a System goal from the gym side, 404 on a gym's goal from the platform side), and three of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The key is **deterministic**, so replacing an image overwrites its own object and the stale-object sweep runs only when the key genuinely moved (the goal was renamed, or its image predates the shape) — which is also why the preview is cache-busted on the row's own `modified_at`. Validation is the **file's**: the PNG signature and the dimensions are read from the bytes, never from the `Content-Type` header or the file name, and a rejection uploads nothing and writes nothing, so an invalid upload cannot disturb the image already there. And what counts as valid is deliberately *not* a Base Nutrition food's rule (#1035 `Q3`): **at most** 512×512 rather than an exact square, and **no alpha requirement** — an opaque PNG is a legitimate goal image, so `pngSupportsTransparency()` is not asked. A gym operation deletes only objects under its own `storage_folder_prefix` and the platform only objects under `cordel/goals/` (`isGymOwnedPersonalGoalImageUrl()` / `isPlatformOwnedPersonalGoalImageUrl()`, #719 §19's rule both ways), there is **no runtime fallback** from a gym goal to the System goal's image and none is generated or seeded (every row starts NULL, no backfill), and the admin renders **one** control for both screens (`components/goalLibrary/GoalImageField.tsx`, handed `basePath` and `requiresGymStorage` by the page, #806/#823) with the preview a *value* in the read-only half and the control Edit mode's alone (#797) — the create form offers none at all, because the key needs the row's id. Do not add a second image column, a stored object key, a per-kind key builder, a second place that decides which goal kinds have an image, or an image on a Nutrition Goal without deciding it in a ticket. Since #948 stage 2 exactly **one** of the two catalogues is read, and only in one way: a **Personal Goal** can be assigned to a Member (the bullet below). **Nutrition Goals is still unread** — assigning one to a Nutrition Plan and defining its target values (`2,000 kcal`) are a later ticket's, so `member_nutrition_plan_goals`, `nutrition_plan_template_goals` and the closed `NUTRITION_GOALS` vocabulary they validate are untouched; a path that consumes a Nutrition Goal row beyond that library's own CRUD is deciding that behaviour and needs a ticket. On the admin side every screen that shows a goal catalogue renders one declaration and one `GoalLibrarySection` (`apps/admin/src/components/goalLibrary/`), which names no endpoint and decides no permission (#806) — do not add a second goals editor or a second list of the four router roots. **Where** each catalogue is administered is #948's answer, not #947's: **Nutrition Goals** is a tab of the Nutrition Library page (`LIBRARY_TABS` is Foods · Nutrition Goals, and both libraries render that one strip), while **Personal Goals** is its own section — `/{locale}/personal-goals` and Cordel's `/{locale}/cordel/personal-goals` — because a Personal Goal does not depend on Nutrition and is a different entity (§8), which is also why its route is *not* nested under `/nutrition/` and why it has a feature flag of its own (`nutrition.personal_goals`, migration 211, seeded from `nutrition.nutrition_library`'s then-current value): gated on the Library's key, hiding Foods would 403 a section of a different domain. The nav group the two share reads **Nutrition & Goals** (#948 §1) and that is a label and nothing else — the group id, its module, its feature key and every existing Nutrition item's href are unchanged (§2). Both Personal Goals pages are thin wrappers that supply the scope, the permissions and the namespace and restate no control, so promoting a tab to a section changed where the catalogue lives and nothing about how it behaves.
- **A gym's availability state for a Personal Goal is the gym's own row, never the shared definition** (#1181, migration 236). `gym_personal_goals` is `gym_professional_services`' shape one catalogue over — `(gym_id, personal_goal_id)` UNIQUE, `status IN ('active','inactive')`, #799's actor pairs — and `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts` is the one place it is declared: `GYM_CONFIGURABLE_GOAL_KINDS` (exactly `personal`; Nutrition Goals are untouched, and the factory registers the three routes and projects `gym_status` only for a kind in that list, mirrored by `goalProfile.ts`), and `gymGoalStatusSql()`, the correlated subquery every reader binds one gym into. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **No row means active**: the catalogue list, the single-row read, `POST /member-personal-goals`, `POST /me/personal-goals` and `GET /me/personal-goals/available` all resolve `COALESCE(gpg.status, 'active')`, so a System goal Cordel adds later is available to every gym without a writer visiting every gym — the backfill and the gym-creation seed (`gyms.ts`, both paths, `INSERT IGNORE`) still write an explicit `active` row wherever one can be written, as the ticket asks. **`POST /personal-goals/:id/activate` and `/deactivate` are the only writers**: one upsert keyed on the UNIQUE (idempotent), audited against the goal as a gym-level change (`previous`/`next` carry `gym_status`), and they never touch `personal_goals` — a System goal deactivated by Gym A is still the platform's and still active for Gym B — nor `member_personal_goals`: an existing assignment is a snapshot (#1034) and stands. **Deactivation only prevents a new assignment**, enforced server-side on both assignment paths (`409 goal_inactive`) and excluded from `/available`; the catalogue still lists the goal, as *Inactive*, and the admin's pickers offer only what the gym may pick (`goalAvailability()` — the API's `gym_status`, active when absent — read in one helper, never the goal's own `status`, which is the lifecycle `active | deleted` and a different axis). And **`POST /personal-goals/:id/duplicate` copies any visible goal — a System one included — into a new gym-owned row**: `<name> - Copy` (the Professional Services convention, a second copy a 409), the definition fields only (`name`, `description`, the target pair) with no slug, no source ownership, no actors and no deleted state, `active` for the gym, in one transaction; the image is **copied as an object into the gym's own key** (`copyStorageObject`, best-effort after the row exists, a System image under `cordel/goals/` read and never written) rather than shared by reference, since the key carries the goal's id. The list reads *Name · Description (clipped, whole in the row and in Details) · Target · Created At · Created By (masked for a System row) · Status · Actions*, and the `⋮` offers Duplicate, then Deactivate (danger) / Activate, then the row's existing items, with Assign disabled for an inactive goal and saying why. Gates: `api/src/test/personal-goal-activation.test.ts`, `goal-library-domain.unit.test.ts`, the admin's `goal-library-ui.test.ts`. Do not add a second place that decides a goal's availability, an activation written on `personal_goals.status`, a `kind` column or a shared configuration table, a duplicate that shares an image object, or a platform-side read of a gym's state.
- An **Assigned Personal Goal** is a Personal Goal a Member holds (#948 §4, migration 212), and it is the one thing that reads either goal catalogue. `member_personal_goals` carries the pair plus what was agreed — a target value and its unit, a start date and a target date, a progress `status` and free-text `notes`, with #799's three actor pairs — and `api/src/domain/personalGoalAssignment.ts` is the one place that vocabulary and every field rule live (the *target* pair itself is `domain/goalTarget.ts`, shared with the catalogue since #1034 and re-exported here). Since #1034 the assignment is an **independent snapshot of the Gym Goal it was created from** (§7/§12), and the snapshot is the **server's, not the form's**: `POST /` reads the catalogue row it already validates, writes its `name` onto `member_personal_goals.goal_name` (migration 218) and inherits its target for any field the request did not mention, so any client takes one. An explicit `null` is still a clear; the unit only inherits while the effective value is non-null, since a unit qualifying nothing is what `chk_mpgoal_target_unit` refuses; and a submitted value is the per-member override §8 exists for, which is how one Gym Goal is held by three members at three targets. Reads project `COALESCE(mpg.goal_name, pg.name)` and never the live name alone — the fallback is for a row assigned **before** that migration, which has nothing else to read (#635 §16's shape), and there is deliberately **no backfill**, because the catalogue's current name is not evidence of what it was called then. A path that displays an assignment therefore reads the snapshot, never a live join onto `personal_goals.name`, `target_value` or `target_unit`, and **any new code path that inserts a `member_personal_goals` row writes `goal_name` from the catalogue row in the same INSERT** — the column has to stay nullable for ever (a legacy row has no snapshot and no `DEFAULT` can read another table), so the invariant lives in the writers rather than in the schema, exactly as `snapshotAssignedPlan()`'s does; a bulk assign or an import that omitted it would read back plausibly and only drift the first time somebody renames the goal. `goal-target.unit.test.ts` is the gate: every `INSERT INTO member_personal_goals` under `api/src` must name the column. One live read is left on purpose: `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` resolves an assignment's `entity_name` from the catalogue's current `name`, because the Audit Log answers "what is this called now" rather than what it was agreed under. Two columns answer two different questions and may not be merged: `status` is **progress** (`in_progress` | `achieved` | `abandoned`, mirrored by `chk_mpgoal_status`, so a new value goes in **three** places — that module, the CHECK, and the admin's `ASSIGNED_GOAL_STATUSES` mirror) while `deleted_at` is whether the assignment exists at all, which is `members`' own soft-delete shape rather than the catalogue's `status = 'deleted'` — folding deletion into the progress vocabulary would overwrite the one fact the row records. The goal it names is checked with the catalogue's **own** gym-facing rule, `(gym_id IS NULL OR gym_id = ?)` and not deleted, so a System goal is assignable by every gym and a retired one by nobody; `member_id` and `personal_goal_id` are **immutable** (`PUT` ignores both — re-pointing is a `DELETE` plus a `POST`, because the audit rows and the member's history are keyed by the pair), and the `PUT` is **partial**, so a field the body does not mention keeps what it is stored with. There may be **many** per member but only one **live, in-progress** row per `(member, goal)`, which `mpgoal_live_goal_key` (the VIRTUAL generated column, migration 183's device again) enforces so the index and the router's 409 agree by construction; an achieved or abandoned goal may be assigned again. Nothing prices, projects or bills off a goal — it is not a billing concept, and no pricing, booking or Nutrition path may start reading one. The admin administers it from **two** screens over one declaration and one editor (#806): `/{locale}/assigned-personal-goals` (its own section under *Nutrition & Goals*, outside `/nutrition/` for the catalogue's reason) and the Member card's **PERSONAL GOALS** section, which the thread settles explicitly — a goal *is* assigned on a Member directly, the opposite of #931's answer for Promotions — where every control belongs to Edit mode and is absent outside it (#797/#957). `apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/` holds the row shape, the payloads, the formatters, the shared form and the two sections; do not add a second assignment table, a second editor, a progress-measurement column or a Nutrition link. Since #1036 the **member** manages their own, from **My Goals** in the Members App (`/{locale}/goals`, `api/src/api/me-personal-goals.ts` mounted at `/me/personal-goals` behind `member_web.my_goals` **and** the catalogue's own `nutrition.personal_goals` — a gym that hid Personal Goals did not mean "and let members assign them anyway"), and it is the same table, the same domain module and the same rules rather than a second surface with its own: the member is **never named by a request** (every route resolves the caller through `resolveMemberId()` and constrains on `(gym_id, member_id)`, so another member's assignment is a 404 whatever the payload says and no body carries a `member_id`), a member **cannot create a Goal definition** (nothing there writes `personal_goals`; the picker is a read of the catalogue under its own gym-facing predicate), the create takes the server's snapshot exactly as the staff `POST` does, and **`status` is not theirs to set** — progress is a staff field and a member ends a goal by removing it. The read answers `{ goals, past_goals }` split on `isLiveAssignment()`, the predicate `mpgoal_live_goal_key` generates from, so **Past Goals** is everything removed, achieved or abandoned, and `/available` is the selector, excluding a retired goal and one the member already holds live — which is what makes the selector and the unique index agree rather than making the UI stricter than the server. **When an assignment ended** is `member_personal_goals.end_date` (migration 222) and `endDateTransition()` is the one place that decides it, asked by both routers: it stamps the day a row stops being live-and-in-progress, keeps the first such date, and clears when the goal is pursued again — it is never submitted by a client, because a fourth date to validate against the other three is a way to claim an assignment ended on a day it did not. That migration also widens the three `chk_mpgoal_*_by_type` CHECKs to admit **`member`**, so a new actor kind goes in **two** places (`ASSIGNMENT_ACTOR_TYPES` and the three CHECKs) and `memberActorSnapshot()` is what records it — a superadmin impersonating a member still records `superadmin`, which is `actorSnapshot()`'s own rule. On the member side the page decides nothing: `apps/member/src/lib/memberGoals.ts` owns the label (a System goal's slug through `goals.goal_<slug>`, the fallback decided *before* `t()` is called), the target formatting (`0 kg` is a target, not an absence), which list a row is in, the two payloads and every client-side rule as a locale **key**, while `components/MemberDialog.tsx` is the app's one dialog shell — since #1115 its two uses are the Add reading dialog and the removal confirmation, the two that are **not** a form over a goal's own fields, because creating and editing a goal happen inline in its card. Since #1037 stage 2 (migration 224) an assignment also accumulates **readings** — one `member_personal_goal_readings` row per measurement (`value` in the assignment's own unit, `recorded_at`, `is_initial`, a `created_by` pair admitting `member`) — and `api/src/domain/goalReadings.ts` is the one place that decides what a reading is and what an assignment's readings add up to, with `api/src/api/goal-readings.ts` the one loader both routers read and write through. Five of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Nothing derived is stored**: `initial_reading`, `latest_reading`, `progress_percent` and the two timestamps beside them are computed on **every** assignment-shaped read (the list, the single row and each mutation, one query per page as `newMemberStatusByMember()` is), so §11's "updates automatically" needs no writer and no path can forget one — do not add a stored progress, latest-reading or initial-reading column. **`is_initial` is the initial-reading period**, which is why there is no second table: §1/§29 put the initial reading *in* the reading history, so a period row with its own `value`/`effective_from` would hold that number twice and could contradict the measurement it was taken from. The initial-reading history is `is_initial = 1` by `recorded_at`, the **active** initial reading is its last row (the earliest reading when nothing is flagged), and a reading's period is `assignReadingPeriods()` — an ordering question, never a join or a per-page rule. **The direction is derived, never declared** (#1037 `Q2`): `progressPercent()` is `(initial - latest) / (initial - target) x 100` for both directions, `target == initial` is maintenance (100 while the latest reading is still the target, 0 otherwise), the result is clamped to 0..100 with the raw value never reported, and `null` means not computable — no readings or no target — which reads as `—` and never `0%`. Do not add a direction column, a goal-name special case or a second progress formula. **A measurement and a baseline are two writers**, `POST /:id/readings` and `POST /:id/initial-reading` on each surface, so a client cannot re-baseline a member's goal through a flag and the audit says which happened (`add_reading` / `set_initial_reading`, against the assignment — a reading gets no `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` entry of its own); a baseline **adds** a period and overwrites nothing (§21/§28), and an initial reading named on `POST /` is written in the assignment's own transaction. And the history is **append-only** (§34): no edit, no delete, the assignment's soft delete leaves every reading where it is, and a `recorded_at` in the future is refused while a date-only value is midnight UTC. Since #1037 stage 3 those fields are **rendered**, and the rendering decides nothing: §5's header is the five structured fields `GOAL_READING_FIELDS` declares — Goal, Initial reading, Target, Latest reading, Progress, label above value, reflowing rather than squeezing five columns onto a phone (§41) — drawn by **one component per app** (`apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/GoalReadingHeader.tsx` for the gym-wide list's expanded row and the Member card's cards, `GoalHeaderFields` in `apps/member/src/components/GoalReadings.tsx` for My Goals, the two apps sharing no frontend module), over the formatters in `goalReadings.ts` / `lib/memberGoals.ts` and **never** a figure a page computed: the three reading values come off the row, a percentage is the server's, and a value that is not computable reads `—` and never `0` or `0%`. §18's **reading history** is the same shape: one collapsible card per surface, newest first (the chart's order reversed, so the API answers one ordering), the **first** boundary marked `Initial` and every later one `New initial reading` (§20/§29) — with the earliest reading marked `Initial` when nothing is flagged, which is exactly the server's own baseline fallback, or the header would quote a figure no row explains — and it carries no `✕`, no edit and no delete, because §34 has no route for either. §3's **Add reading** is one dialog per app in the app's own shell (`CrudModal`, `MemberDialog`), taking a number and a date with the assignment's **unit shown beside the input rather than asked for**, the date defaulting to today and a future one refused; which of the two writers it calls is the **route** (`READING_ENDPOINTS`), never a field in the payload, so the client cannot re-baseline a goal through the measurement endpoint either. Where the action is *offered* is each surface's own rule and not this feature's: a `⋮` item on the gym-wide list, because expanding a row reads and the menu is the single entry point into a write (#797); a button inside the Member card's Edit mode, absent outside it (#957); and a `⋮` item on the member's own card too since #1115, which has no Edit mode because the goals are theirs and whose cards are collapsed, so a button in a card body would be a write nobody can reach without opening one. Since #1037 stage 4 the readings are also **drawn**, and the chart is a consumer of the shared charting layer rather than a chart of its own: one `GoalReadingChart` per app (`apps/admin/src/components/personalGoals/GoalReadingChart.tsx` for both staff surfaces, `apps/member/src/components/GoalReadingChart.tsx` for My Goals) over `@gymdesk/charts`' `LineChart`, with the rows turned into points by the same `goalReadings.ts` / `lib/memberGoals.ts` module the formatters live in (`readingChartPoints()`, whose two copies `api/src/test/goal-reading-chart.unit.test.ts` asserts answer the same chart for the same rows). It renders §12's order — header, chart, history — and decides nothing: the point's `group` is the server's own `period` (§38), so §23/§24's one colour per initial-reading period is `segmentPoints()`' doing and not a page's; the target is a dashed reference line and an assignment with **no** target gets neither a line nor a caption rather than one reading `Target —`; and a goal with no readings renders **no chart section at all**, because the history beside it already says there are none. Do not add a second chart for a goal, a page-local ECharts option, a per-surface chart layout, or a direction, trend or colour derived from a reading. Since #1115 the member's own goals are **cards**, and a card is created and edited **inside itself** rather than in a dialog: `apps/member/src/components/MemberGoalCard.tsx` is the one place that card's shape lives and it decides nothing (the title, the summary line and every menu label arrive already resolved and formatted, so it calls no `t()`, reads no row and spells no colour, exactly as `GoalReadings.tsx` and `NutritionItemRow` do), while the page owns the open state, the draft and the requests. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The header is **one button and the `⋮` is another**, siblings rather than nested, because §4 requires that tapping the menu neither expands nor collapses the card and a nested button is invalid HTML while a `stopPropagation()` is a rule a later edit can forget. A card is **collapsed by default** and **expanding fetches nothing** (#955's rule) — the reading histories come with the page, so opening one is presentation. **Creating and editing are inline and share one form**: `+ Add personal goal` and the empty state's CTA open a draft card at the top of the section with today in Start date (`newGoalForm()`, and the picker is applied *over* that draft so choosing a goal does not wipe what was typed), `⋮ → Edit` turns an existing card's body into the same `goalForm(mode)`, and Cancel removes the draft or leaves the edited card exactly as it was — which is why exactly two `MemberDialog`s are left on that screen. And the **unit is read-only**, because it is the Gym Goal's: a target the member clears takes its unit with it (`toGoalUpdatePayload()`), since `chk_mpgoal_target_unit` refuses a unit qualifying nothing and there is no longer a field they could fix it in — which is also why `goalFormError()` does not refuse that state. `api/src/test/member-goal-cards.unit.test.ts` is the gate (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not add a member-facing progress vocabulary, a second place that decides when an assignment ended, a Goal-definition write on the member side, a second dialog shell, a stored progress or baseline column, a reading edit or delete path, a second header or history rendering within one app, a page-local percentage, or a second place that decides a percentage, a modal for creating or editing a goal, a second card chrome or expand affordance in that app, a unit input on the member's side, or a control inside a card body that the `⋮` does not also offer.
- **A goal target is absolute or relative, and the effective target is derived** (#1229, migration 245). `target_type` (`absolute` default | `relative`) lives on `personal_goals` and on the `member_personal_goals` snapshot, declared once in `api/src/domain/goalTarget.ts` (`TARGET_TYPES`, `normalizeTargetType()`, `effectiveTarget()`), mirrored by `chk_pgoal_target_type` / `chk_mpgoal_target_type` and the admin's form types, so a new type goes in **three** places. Only a relative target may be negative (`-5 kg`); `targetPairError()` judges the value against the type the write ends up with and absolute keeps the non-negative rule. Assignment inherits the catalogue's type exactly as it inherits the target. `summarizeReadings()` reports `effective_target` (absolute: the value; relative: **the assignment's first reading** + the change, `null` without a reading) and measures progress against it; the baseline is deliberately the first reading, never the active initial reading, so a later `set_initial_reading` never moves an existing goal's target. Nothing derived is stored. Seeded System goals Weight Loss (-3), Weight Gain (+3), Muscle Gain (+2) and Maintenance (0) are relative; existing assignments stay absolute. Do not store an effective target, or let a baseline change move it.
- The Member app's **My Nutrition** shows what the member's plan carries and holds no control of any kind: its **Dietary Restrictions** and **Nutrition Goals** are both read-only (#932), and both render through the one shared `apps/member/src/components/NutritionItemRow.tsx`, which owns the thumbnail size, the 1:1 aspect ratio, the radius, the spacing, the typography and the `nutrition.no_image` fallback for both — a section with a look of its own is what the ticket's §4 forbids. A **restriction is a Nutrition Library food** (`member_nutrition_plan_restrictions.nutrition_library_item_id` is NOT NULL), so its picture is that item's own `image_url`, resolved server-side by `GET /me/nutrition-plan` along with its locale-specific name: there is no second image source, image mapping or bundled placeholder asset for this page. A **goal has no food at all** — `member_nutrition_plan_goals` carries `item_name` + `quantity`/`unit`/`frequency` and no `nutrition_library_item_id` — and its `item_name` is a **slug** from the plan routers' closed `NUTRITION_GOALS` vocabulary, never a label: `goalLabel()` / `goalDetail()` (`apps/member/src/lib/nutritionFood.ts`) are the one place that translate it and compose its `1 l · daily` line, deciding the fallback before `t()` is called, and every surface showing a goal calls them (My Nutrition and Home's Today's Nutrition Plan card), or the two screens word one goal differently. Since #932's second half (migration 233) a goal may be **illustrated by** a Nutrition Library food — `nutrition_library_item_id`, nullable with `ON DELETE SET NULL`, on both `nutrition_plan_template_goals` and `member_nutrition_plan_goals` — and `api/src/api/nutrition-goal-illustration.ts` is the one place the value is judged (`parseGoalIllustrationInput()`: absent keeps, `null` clears, an id must be a food visible to the gym and not deleted; a Base template's goal may name a System food only, since its goals are copied into every gym) and the one SQL that reads the pair back (`goalRowsSql()`, `illustration_name` / `illustration_image_url` beside every goal row). The food travels with the goal — Duplicate, clone-from-Base and a plan created from a template all copy the column — and `GET /me/nutrition-plan` carries the food's own `image_url` beside the goal, the same source the meals and the restrictions read; a goal illustrated by nothing carries `null` and shows the standard fallback. Nothing guesses a food from a slug, nothing is backfilled, and no second image source, mapping or placeholder asset exists for the page.
- Anything that removes a person's link to Gymdesk (a member, a staff login, a `gym_memberships` row) must decide the fate of their **Clerk account** through `api/src/infra/clerk-account-links.ts` (`classifyAccount` / `unlinkClerkAccount`): delete the Clerk user first when it was the last link, as `DELETE /members/:id` and staff `revokeAccess()` do. Otherwise the login is left orphaned — see "Orphaned Clerk accounts (#709)" in `docs/architecture.md`.
- Which activities a member may book is configured on the **Activity Type**, never on the Membership Plan — and since #973 stage 1 (migration 231) it is configured as **Professional Services, and there is no plan-based booking gate at all**: `activity_type_eligible_professional_services` names the services a non-`public_event` activity requires, a member qualifies by holding **sessions** for one of them from whichever Product granted them (`domain/memberProfessionalServices.ts`, the same list `GET /members/:memberId/professional-services` serves), and `api/src/domain/activityEligibility.ts` is the one place the rule is decided (`decideServiceEligibility()`), with `api/src/api/activity-eligibility.ts` the SQL around it and the hook. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **An activity that names no service is open to every member** (the thread's `Q3 open`), which is also why migration 231 maps nothing: a Membership Plan and a Professional Service are different things (§4), the old `activity_type_eligible_plans` rows are logged and dropped, and every activity starts open rather than locked. **Booking does not spend a session** (`Q2`): a session is spent by the consumption ledger below, and stage 1's hold-over (`tryClaimPackageCredit()` debiting a package at booking) is gone. And **the refusal names what would unlock it**: `403 { code: 'professional_service_required', professional_services: [{ id, name }] }`, for the Members App surface that routes to buying one (#1121). `PUT /activity-types/:id/eligible-professional-services` is replace-all, validates a *newly chosen* service through `validateProfessionalServiceId()` (visible, not deleted, active for the gym) and keeps a stored one the gym has since switched off (#986's rule), the admin offers such a value as a disabled option, and `isActivityTypeEligibleForMember()` keeps its signature so #647's weekly slot projection asks the same rule. The Plan-side copy of the old relation, `plan_allowances` ("Included Services"), was retired by migration 177 (#635 §1) — do not reintroduce a per-plan activity grant, a per-window session cap, an Activity → Membership Plan relation, a second place that decides eligibility, or a mapping from plans to services.
- **A member's Professional Service balance has four sources, and the Membership Plan's own Session Benefits are the fourth** (#1189 stage 2). `loadMemberProfessionalServiceGrants()` (`api/src/domain/memberProfessionalServices.ts`) adds `plan_session` beside the purchased package, the Promotion session grant and the assignment-level Additional Service: the sessions come from the **Assigned Plan snapshot** (`user_membership_session`, resolved to services through `product_professional_services`), never the live `membership_plan_session` catalogue, and only while the assignment is `active` and inside its dates — a Draft, Pending Payment, paused or cancelled row grants nothing. A covered member of a family plan holds the plan's sessions too (read through `user_membership_members`). The booking gate and `GET /members/:memberId/professional-services` share the one read, so they cannot disagree. A renewing Frequency (#918) adds its quantity on each renewal through #1227 stage 2's `plan_allowance_renewals` rows. Do not add a second wallet read or read plan sessions from the live catalogue.
- **A Professional Service session is spent by a ledger row, on attendance, a late cancellation or a no-show, and never by booking** (#1189 stage 3, migration 238). `professional_service_consumptions` holds one row per spend — `UNIQUE (calendar_event_booking_id)`, so a booking spends at most once and a corrected attendance roll can never double-spend — naming the grant spent by the very `(source_kind, source_reference_id)` pair `loadMemberProfessionalServiceGrants()` reports it under. `api/src/domain/serviceConsumption.ts` is the one place that decides it (`isLateCancellation()` over #1162's `CANCELLATION_NOTICE_HOURS` — the same constant, deliberately not a second configuration — `reasonForAttendance()`, `chooseSpendGrant()`, `applyConsumption()`) and `api/src/api/service-consumption.ts` the SQL half. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Plan-included sessions are spent before purchased ones** (`SPEND_ORDER`: plan, Promotion, Additional Service, then the class package). **The wallet is `grant - unreturned ledger rows`** for the kinds with no counter of their own, while a `class_package` keeps its live `sessions_remaining`, which the writer decrements in the same transaction — so that kind is never subtracted twice. **No-show is automatic, and staff choose**: `POST /bookings/:id/attendance` with `absent` spends under `no_show`, and `consume: false` is the "return class" answer (`returnSessionForBooking()` stamps `returned_at` and gives a package its credit back; the row stays as the record). **A member's own cancellation spends nothing** (`cancelBooking(..., { spendLateCancel: false })` from `/me`): it is only ever allowed outside the notice window or inside the grace period, so the late-cancel spend is the staff `DELETE /bookings/:id`'s alone, and a waiting-list place is never spent. A balance of zero spends nothing rather than going negative. Walk-in and bulk-present are attendance and spend through the same writer. Do not spend at booking, add a second window for a late cancel, a stored balance beside the ledger, or a path that marks attendance without going through `spendSessionForBooking()`. The legacy refund of a credit a pre-stage-3 booking was debited at booking (`user_class_package_id`) stays.
- **A staff correction of a Member's Professional Service balance is one more source of the existing derived balance, never a second balance** (#1227 stage 1, migration 242). `professional_service_adjustments` is an append-only ledger (signed `delta`, `balance_before`, `balance_after`, `reason`, #1182's plain-text `created_by`); staff enter the **new balance** through `POST /members/:id/professional-services/:serviceId/adjust` and the delta is stored. `domain/professionalServiceAdjustments.ts` is the one place that decides it: per service the adjustments net to one number — positive becomes a `manual_adjustment` grant (spendable, so `chk_psc_kind` admits that kind), negative is taken off the service's other grants, last in `SPEND_ORDER` first (a grant shared by several services is reduced for all of them, the overlap `memberProfessionalServices.ts` already documents). `GET …/wallet` and `GET …/:serviceId/history` read it; the Member card's *Professional Services* tab (`MemberProfessionalServices.tsx`) draws it. Stage 2 (migration 244) renews plan Session Benefit allowances: `plan_allowance_renewals` is an append-only row per renewal (`UNIQUE (line, renewal_date)`) that is both the grant (the `plan_session` grant is the snapshot line's quantity plus its renewals) and the *Plan allowance renewed* history entry; `domain/planAllowanceRenewals.ts` decides what is owed (renewals after `starts_at`, up to `ends_at`, a line's first run catching up only `FIRST_RUN_CATCH_UP_DAYS` so old assignments get no retroactive sessions) and `POST /plan-allowance-renewals/run` is a step of `billing-run.yml` sharing `BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET`, on the admin relay allowlist, with **no** run-log slot because every insert is idempotent. Do not add a stored balance column, a second balance read, or an adjustment that edits a ledger row.
- **A refused booking routes the member to the Products that unlock it** (#1189 stage 4). `POST /me/bookings` now returns the gate's `professional_services` beside `code: 'professional_service_required'`, `apiClient` carries `code` and the response body on the thrown error, and the Calendar offers a *Buy sessions* action to `/membership?professional_service_ids=…`. `GET /me/products` takes that same `professional_service_ids` list (`parseProfessionalServiceFilter()` in `domain/memberProductCatalogue.ts`; garbage is a 400) and only **narrows** the one catalogue predicate through `product_professional_services` — it never widens what a member may be shown or bought. The Members App's decisions live in `apps/member/src/lib/serviceRequired.ts`; the page reads the filter off `window.location` (no `useSearchParams` Suspense boundary) and offers *Show all products*. Do not add a second products read for this, a client-side filter, or a refusal route that bypasses the gate's code.
- The same holds one table over: **where** an activity runs is configured on the Activity Type, never on the Space. `activity_types.default_space_id` (migration 081) is the one direction, and the Space-side copy — the `ACTIVITIES` checkbox list in the Space form, `GET`/`PUT /spaces/:id/activity-types` and the `space_activity_types` join table — was retired by migration 197 (#801). Nothing else ever read that table: `class_sessions` carries its own `space_id`, and booking eligibility and billing never looked at it. Do not reintroduce a Space-side Activity relation, on `spaces` or in a side table.
- **Who may be assigned as a trainer is an active Staff record**, and `api/src/domain/trainerAssignment.ts` is the one place that decides it (#986). The condition is `staff.employment_status = 'active'` on a live row of the gym and **nothing else**: not the role on the login (`GET /trainers` filtered on `role IN ('trainer_performance','trainer_perf_nutrition')` until this ticket, so a gym staffed by Gym Managers and Front Desk saw an *empty* Default Trainer dropdown — the defect the ticket reports), not a center, not a Professional Service, not "already assigned elsewhere", and not `staff.current_status` either: `on_vacation` or `suspended` is a state of an employee who is still employed, and migration 067 keeps the two columns apart for that reason. The shared scope and the §5 ordering (first name, then last name, from the staff row's own names rather than `gym_memberships.name`, which is a snapshot of them) are `ASSIGNABLE_TRAINERS_FROM` + `assignableTrainersSql()`, and the three readers are projections of that one fragment — `GET /trainers` (the Activity Type picker, the Calendar's event lookups and the Trainer Availability page), `GET /me/trainers` (the member calendar's filter, id + name only) and `isAssignableTrainer()`, which **every** writer validates against — `POST`/`PUT /activity-types`, `validateSessionRefs()` on `POST`/`PUT /class-sessions`, `PUT /class-sessions/:id/effective-trainer` and `POST /trainer-availability`, the last three of which carried the retired role filter as their own validation, so a picker offering an active Front Desk member would have been refused by the very routes it feeds. The Activity Type was the one writer with no validation at all: the id went in raw, so another gym's membership satisfied the FK and crossed the tenant boundary, and a string `"5"` never equalled the stored `5`, which re-propagated the trainer onto every future occurrence on each save. The one structural condition left is not a second rule: every table that stores a trainer (`activity_types.default_trainer_membership_id`, `calendar_events.trainer_membership_id`, `trainer_availability`) keys to `gym_memberships.id`, so an active staff member whose login grant errored has no id to be stored under — the Staff card's App access section is where that is repaired, not here. Two of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. A **stored** trainer is never silently lost: `trainerWriteNeedsLookup()` treats a value identical to the one the row already holds as *not* a selection, so editing an activity's capacity cannot 400 because the trainer assigned to it last year has since left, and the admin offers that value as a **disabled** option (the #980 stage 1 pattern) so it still reads correctly instead of appearing to be none. And the clearing of an assignment whose trainer is *deactivated* is the database's, not a sweep's: deactivating staff revokes the login and deletes the `gym_memberships` row, and every FK above is `ON DELETE SET NULL`. Do not add a second trainer lookup, a role- or center-based filter beside it, an `is_trainer` flag on `staff`, or a client-side "is this person assignable" check.
- **A role's access is the module matrix's, and a `feature_flags` key is the one way to carve an exception out of it** (#1070). `PERMISSION_MATRIX` (`api/src/infra/permissions.ts`, mirrored in `apps/admin/src/config/permissions.ts`) answers per **module**, which is coarse on purpose — one cell for every screen of a section. `FEATURE_PERMISSION_OVERRIDES`, beside it in both files, is the exception and is declared per **feature key**: the same dot-separated keys the navigation and `feature_flags` use, so a feature's permission and its flag are named the same thing. Three of its properties are the rule rather than the implementation. It is matched on the **exact** key and never on an ancestor, so `nutrition.personal_goals` says nothing about the rest of `nutrition` — that narrowness is the whole reason to override at the feature level rather than widen a cell. It **replaces** the module's level in both directions, so a `NONE` override takes a feature away from a role the module admits. And it is read where the flag already is: `requireFeatureAccess(featureKey, module)` / `requireFeatureWrite(featureKey, module)` (`infra/tenantContext.ts`) take the same key `requireFeatureEnabled` is handed beside them, and the frontend asks `useModuleAccess(module, featureKey)` — one answer for the control a page offers and the route it calls, which is #611's reason for mirroring the matrix at all (`api/src/test/feature-permission-overrides.unit.test.ts` fails on drift, in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). A guard given a key with no override behaves exactly as the module guard did, so adding one goes in **two** places: the declaration and its mirror. The one override today is #1070's — **`nutrition.personal_goals` × `trainer_performance` = `RW`**, covering the Personal Goals catalogue (`/personal-goals`) and the assignments (`/member-personal-goals`), which are both mounted on that key (`GOAL_LIBRARY_FEATURE_KEYS` in `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts` says which goal kind rides which, so one factory still serves both and a Personal Trainer writes Personal Goals and not Nutrition Goals). Cordel → **Feature Flags** reports it: `GET /platform/feature-flags/role-access` answers `overrides` keyed by the full feature key **beside** the inherited `access` rather than folded into it, and the page draws that one cell in `formChrome`'s `alertTextColor` — the app's one red, now exported rather than spelled per surface — with a `title` and a legend, because a distinction resting on colour alone is not one. That page also names a flag after the section it gates (§1): `apps/admin/src/lib/featureFlagLabels.ts` resolves the key to the **navigation's own** `labelKey`, so `nutrition` reads *Nutrition & Goals* because #948 renamed that group and not because this page spells it, the stored key is untouched and still printed beside the label, and a key the navigation does not gate keeps its short key. Do not add a second place that resolves a feature's permission, a per-role editor on that screen (a permission is declared in code, not toggled), a stored permission table, an override matched by prefix, or a section name spelled in the Feature Flags page.
- **Center selection is the Calendar's** (#930). The admin app has one center context — `CenterContext`, `activeCenterId` (`null` = all centers, persisted per gym) — and since #930 exactly one control over it: `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/calendar/CalendarCenterFilter.tsx`, in the Calendar's own filter bar, wearing `FilterField` + `filterControlStyle`. The `CenterSelector` that sat in `TopHeader` is gone: a gym-wide header dropdown reads as a selection every section obeys, and the Members list never did obey it — it has its own Center filter. Do not reintroduce a global or top-header center control, and do not fork the context into page-local state: `apiClient` sends it as `x-center-id`, which is what `resolveCenterId()` defaults a **write**'s center from (class sessions, spaces, rooms, trainer availability) and what `ThemeProvider` paints a Center's own theme from, so a second copy costs a multi-center gym both. That header has never filtered a **read** on the admin side, so a read that wants one center passes `center_id` as a query param — `GET /calendar-events` and `GET /class-sessions` both take it, and the two answer differently on purpose: a session's `center_id` is mandatory so its filter is strict, while a manual event's is nullable (gym-wide) and such a row stays visible under every center, mirroring #478. A gym with one center gets no control at all, since `resolveCenterId()`'s sole-active-center fallback has already decided it.
- **A gym's Website Integration is administered from Cordel → Gyms → [Gym]** (#1052), not from the gym's own Configuration group, and it is one section of the expanded gym card: `apps/admin/src/components/gyms/GymWebsiteIntegrationSection.tsx`, beside Configuration and Storage, holding the endpoint and its Copy action, the key's prefix and date, Generate / Rotate / Revoke behind the two existing confirmations, the once-only plaintext key and the five connection steps. The move is a **relocation and nothing else**: the three routes (`GET /system/website-integration`, `POST`/`DELETE …/key`), their validation, their `website_api_key` audit rows, the `system.website_integration` flag, the `website_integration` copy in en/es/ca and the public `POST /public/gyms/:gymRef/registrations` are all untouched — so do not fork the copy per screen, build the endpoint URL in the browser (it is the API's, #645), or reintroduce a standalone Website Integration page or nav item. Because Cordel is superadmin-only, a gym admin no longer reaches it from the UI; that is the placement's consequence, not a permission change, and the route still answers a gym admin exactly as before. Those are **tenant-scoped** routes called from a screen where the *row*, not the gym selector, says whose configuration is on screen, which is the one thing the relocation needed: `apiFetch(path, { gymId })` (`apps/admin/src/lib/apiClient.ts`) overrides the selected gym for that one request, and it is the only way a Cordel screen does that — a page must not spell `x-gym-id` for itself, because the header is assembled in that one place (#824) and the selected gym would overwrite it a line later. The option can never widen what a caller may do: `tenantContext` grants a superadmin admin on any gym it is handed and refuses anyone else without a `gym_memberships` row for it, so the permission stays the server's. `uploadFetch` and `pdfFetch` deliberately take no gym. The section loads when the card is expanded rather than with the list, because the key state is not what the list's row is for and `GET /platform/gyms` stays one request.
- **A Theme's Center assignments are one replace-all set** (#985). `centers.theme_id` is the Center's own, explicit Theme and `NULL` means it follows `gyms.theme_id` (the Gym Default), so "assigned" is that column and un-assigning is writing NULL back — which is what the retired `DELETE /system/themes/:id/centers/:centerId` ("Restore Inheritance") did and what an unticked checkbox means now. `PUT /system/themes/:id/centers` is the one writer: it replaces the set (an empty array is a legitimate save), `api/src/domain/themeCenterAssignments.ts` decides what moves, and both UPDATEs run in **one transaction**, because a half-saved list would assign some Centers and leave others on a Theme the admin had unticked. A Center id is an **auto-increment integer** (`centers.id`, migration 043), so it is a number on the wire from one client and a string from another: both are accepted and every comparison — on the route and in the admin draft — runs through `centerKey()`, with the ids reported back the *stored* ones. A path that compared the two forms directly answers "center not found" for every real Center, which is the one way this write goes wrong. It replaced the picker's three routes (`GET /:id/unassigned-centers`, `POST /:id/assign-centers`, that `DELETE`) and no second assignment writer, add-only route or per-Center action may come back. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. A Center pointing at **another** Theme and not submitted is untouched — the set is this Theme's assignments, never every Center's. The **Active** rule guards *assigning*, not the request, so a `draft`/`inactive` Theme can still be cleared off the Centers it was left on while a non-empty set is still a 400. And an **inherited** Center is not an assigned one: `GET /:id/assignments` reports every non-deleted Center of the gym with `is_assigned` (its own column) beside `is_inherited` (none of its own, and this Theme is the Gym Default), the admin renders the second as a tag beside the name and never as a ticked box, because a box that pre-ticked itself would end that Center's inheritance on the next unrelated save. On the admin side the section is an inline checkbox list persisted by the card's own `Save changes` — `All Centers` is **derived** in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/themes/centerAssignments.ts` (`allCentersChecked`, never its own state) and `centerSelectionChanged` puts the Centers in the card's dirty state, which is also why a **Base Theme** has that Save pair now: its configuration stays the platform's and read-only, its assignments are the gym's. Do not reintroduce an Assign modal, a search field over the Centers, a second place that decides `All Centers`, or a Center assignment that persists on its own.
- **A calendar event's colour is the event's; a booking state is text** (#976 for the Members App, #975 for the Admin). In the **Members App** the event box is painted with `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)` — the colour the gym configured, identical for every member — and never with the member's own `availability_state`: the eight-colour `STATE_COLORS` palette that preceded it (yellow for "you are waitlisted", orange for a joinable waitlist, green for "you are booked") made one class two colours for two members standing next to each other. `apps/member/src/lib/calendarEventDisplay.ts` is the one place that decides it, and three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. A colour that is absent, blank or **not a usable hex** resolves to `null`, and `null` means the caller sets *no* inline `backgroundColor`/`borderColor`/`textColor` at all, which is the only way the theme's own `calendarEventBackground`/`Border`/`Text` can paint the box — FullCalendar writes those as inline styles, which beat the tokens (#559 stage 2/3's own reasoning), so a fallback hue invented here would override a gym's Theme. The **foreground** of an inline-coloured event is scored, not assumed: the theme's `calendarEventText` is chosen against the theme's *own* event background, so both candidates (`#111827`, `#ffffff` — no third) are run through the WCAG 2.1 contrast formula and the better taken, never a luminance threshold derived for pure black. And the member's status is a locale **key** plus its interpolation values, resolved by the page, so the module calls no `t()` and both halves stay assertable. What the status line says is the other half: `Booked`, `Waitlist · Position #2 · 4 waiting`, `Completed`, and **nothing at all** for an event the member never booked — a past class they skipped is an ordinary past class, so no `Not used`, no `Cancelled`, no `Past due` and no other operational state of the slot may be surfaced to them (#976 §2/§6/§7); `Cancelled` reaches only a member who holds a booking on it, and the waitlist total rides beside `Booked` only while the waitlist can still move. The chip it sits in declares no hue (`color: inherit` over a neutral alpha black), because a booking state must not come back as a colour in another guise. The **Admin** calendar is the same answer since #975 (`Q1 event colour`), reached through its own one place — `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventPaint.ts`, because the two apps share no frontend module — and the three rules above are its rules too, with `contrastRatio()` taken from the admin's existing `lib/calendarContrast.ts` rather than copied so the app has one WCAG implementation. What differs is only what the box is *not* allowed to say: there the status is `CalendarStatusBadge` (#559 stage 3, which exists precisely to stop status from painting the box) and the paint module cannot see a status, a booking count or a capacity at all — a caller that feeds it one is reintroducing #541, which painted the event by status and stage 3 overturned. An event that configures no colour still takes the theme's event tokens in both apps, which is what `null` is for. The hover and focus affordances of an inline-coloured admin event are two CSS rules behind the `gd-event-colored` class the module adds (`CalendarThemeStyles`): hover **darkens** the gym's colour rather than replacing it with `calendarEventHoverBackground`, and the focus ring is `currentColor` — the foreground just scored against that background — because the theme's `calendarEventText` is scored against the theme's own event background and cannot be trusted over an arbitrary hue. So do not add a second place that resolves an event's colour in either app, a colour the page spells itself, a booking-state hue, a status reaching either paint module, or a member-facing surface that reports a slot's own execution state. **What the box says beside the title is one place per app too** (#981): the **trainer and the space** are composed by `apps/admin/src/lib/calendarEventMeta.ts` and by `memberEventMeta()`/`memberEventMetaLine()` (`apps/member/src/lib/calendarEventDisplay.ts`), and three of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The values are the **occurrence's own** — `space_name`/`trainer_name` as the three reads project them from `ce.space_id`/`ce.trainer_membership_id`, with `effective_trainer_name ?? trainer_name` where a read has both (#193) — so neither module has a field an Activity Type default could be passed in through, and a page must not supply one. A name that is absent, blank or whitespace answers **`null`, and `null` renders no line at all**: no `N/A`, no `Unknown`, no `Not assigned`, which is also why the module returns the *lines* rather than a joined string, a dangling `·` being that same empty placeholder. And the **layout is the view's**, since a FullCalendar event cannot measure itself — `compact` (one truncated `Trainer · Space`) for the admin Day and Week views (#1245: line 1 is Time · Activity · Status · Occupancy, line 2 the trainer and space, two lines at most) and both member time-grid views; `full` (a line each) remains available but no admin view uses it — while every other view keeps what it had, the admin month cell its title/time/badge and the member month cell its trainer-alone line. It is text and only text, like the status above it: do not add a second place that composes an event box's secondary lines, a placeholder for a missing trainer or space, a per-page layout rule for them, or a hue derived from either.

- **An Admin event's execution status is derived on read, never stored** (#977). What happened to the *event* — `scheduled`, `not_used`, `completed`, `cancelled` — is one axis, and `api/src/domain/eventExecutionStatus.ts` is the one place that decides it: a `scheduled` row that has ended with no `booked` bookings is **`not_used`**, one that has ended *with* bookings stays `scheduled` until a person confirms it (§4/§13 — a booked past session is never completed automatically), and the two values a person chooses carry through from the `status` column that already held them. There is **no `execution_status` column and no sweep**: `not_used` is a function of the clock and the booking count, so a stored copy would need a nightly writer, and a night it missed would report yesterday's empty slots as still *Scheduled* — which is also why no confirmation task is created for an empty slot (§3, the whole point for a gym whose calendar is mostly open slots for private classes). An explicit decision **outranks the clock in both directions**, which is what keeps `Completed · 0 attendees` (§5 — the teacher held the session with nobody there) a different fact from a slot that passed unbooked, and a `draft` manual entry answers **`null`** rather than a fifth value, because it was never put on the calendar and so neither ran nor went unused. `withEventExecutionStatus()` wraps **every** read of both routers — list, single row and the rows the mutations return — so the field is reported and never re-derived: the badge's `full` (a scheduled session at capacity, still the UI's own derivation) is gated on it, and a surface that re-asked "has it ended with nobody in it" would be the second place deciding what an unused slot is. The status reaches the screen as **text, never a colour** (§8): `CalendarStatusBadge` carries it with `not_used` taking the palette's existing neutral tone (shared with `draft` — never the yellow §8 rules out), and the box takes the event's own `COALESCE(calendar_events.color, activity_types.color)` since #975 answered that question — which that paint module cannot see a status through, so this rule holds by construction rather than by leaving the box unpainted. It is deliberately **not** `computeCalendarEventStatus()` (`api/src/api/me.ts`, #503 stage 5), the member-facing lifecycle: `Not used` is an Admin operational state, members may not be shown that an unused slot existed (§15, and #976's bullet above for the member-side half), and that holds structurally because both routers sit behind `requireModuleAccess('CALENDAR')` where a `member` is `NONE`. Both explicit transitions record **previous → new** status through `recordAudit` (§14), which is also where "who confirmed it and when" lives — there is no `completed_by`/`completed_at` column beside it, and `POST /class-sessions/:id/complete` keeps its pre-existing guards (pending attendance, no trainer) rather than growing an "ended" one, since the *action* is what §4 gates and the UI is where it is offered. Do not add a stored execution status, a second place that decides `not_used`, a nightly transition writer, a fifth value, or a member-facing surface that reports any of it.

- **A cancellation is undone, never replaced** (#979). `POST /class-sessions/:id/reactivate` is the only way back from `cancelled`, and it writes `status = 'scheduled'` — clearing the `cancellation_reason` it undoes, since the audit row is where that reason lives from then on — and nothing else: the lifecycle is `Scheduled → Cancelled → Scheduled`, `Completed` stays reserved for an event that actually took place (§3), and anything not `cancelled` is a `400 { code: 'not_cancelled' }`, which is also what the loser of two concurrent calls gets because the `UPDATE` carries `status = 'cancelled'` in its own WHERE. **Nothing is restored, because nothing was removed**: `POST /:id/cancel` writes the event's status and has never touched `calendar_event_bookings`, so the enrolled members, the waitlist and its `waitlist_position` ordering are still exactly as they were (§4/§5) and reactivation inserts nothing at all — which is what makes a duplicate booking, a duplicate waitlist entry or a second event impossible by construction (§11), and why a booking the member cancelled themselves stays cancelled. Do not add a restore step, a re-booking path or a `cancelled_bookings` snapshot table; the route's booking read is there to *report* what came back (the alert and the audit row) and a path that re-books from it is deciding something else. Two things it does guard, and they are the rule rather than the implementation. The cancellation freed the trainer/space slot — every conflict check in this router skips `cancelled` rows — so re-occupying it goes through `assertSlotAvailable()`, the one `FOR UPDATE` shared-slot rule `POST /` and `PUT /:id` also use (#979 extracted it rather than writing a third copy), answering the same four `409` codes; a new writer that puts a session back on a slot calls that helper rather than checking for itself. And the members who held a booking are alerted with **`event_reactivated`**, never `booking_confirmed` — the member booked nothing (§6/§11) — while a **waitlisted** member is not alerted at all, because they were never told the class was cancelled either and have no booking to have restored; the member-facing copy is a locale key plus, where a type needs a second line, an entry in the Alerts page's declared `DETAIL_TYPES` (next-intl prints a missing key verbatim, so a `t()` fallback is not one). A **past** cancelled event is reactivatable on purpose, since "cancelled by mistake" is usually noticed after the class should have run, and #977's rule then classifies it — `not_used` when nobody is on it — so this route decides no execution status of its own. In the admin a cancelled session's Actions block offers **exactly one** action (completing, moving or re-cancelling it all act on a slot nobody holds), confirmed in the panel's own inline confirm card rather than a second overlay style, and the panel reloads instead of closing so §8's status, capacity, enrolled list and waiting list read as restored immediately. A **manual** calendar entry has no reactivate route: its status is edited in `EventDetailsPanel`'s own select, which is also how it is cancelled, so the explicit action pairs with the explicit cancel only sessions have — do not add a second reactivation path, a bulk one, or a reactivation that re-notifies, re-books or reprices anything.
- **An occurrence's Trainer and Space are edited on the occurrence** (#980 stage 1). They are `calendar_events.trainer_membership_id` / `space_id` (migration 082), so the event's own detail panel writes them through the `PUT /class-sessions/:id` that already accepted them — one `UPDATE`, so a failed save leaves the row exactly as it was, with `validateSessionRefs()` (an active space, of this occurrence's own center) and `assertSlotAvailable()`'s four `409` codes unchanged — and the Activity Type's `default_trainer_membership_id` / `default_space_id` are never touched: an occurrence *overrides* the defaults and does not rewrite them, so a path that writes an Activity Type column from an event surface is the thing §12 forbids. Every edit of that route records **previous → new** for the fields that moved, and `api/src/domain/calendarEventChanges.ts` is the one place that decides what moved: the diff is taken between the row before the write and the row after it, never from the request body (the route is a partial write and a staff form sends every field), an edit that changed nothing writes **no** audit row, and values are reported as stored because `FK_MAP` enriches `space_id`, `trainer_membership_id`, `activity_type_id` and `professional_service_id` into `{ id, name }`. Both write paths end at one `readSessionAndAudit()`, so a new field goes in **two** places: the route and `SESSION_AUDITED_FIELDS`. In the panel the split is the app's own (#797 — the info card reads, `Edit details` writes): no control exists outside that mode, the two fields wear `formChrome`'s own select and `primaryBtnSmall()`/`secondaryBtnSmall`, the lookups are the Calendar page's `/spaces` and `/trainers` passed down rather than a second fetch, a stored value that is no longer selectable is offered as a **disabled** option so it still reads correctly, and the save sends only what changed so an unrelated edit cannot 400 on a value nobody touched. The occurrence's `professional_service_id` stays a **value**: #980's `Q1 multi` makes it a multi-valued relation in a later stage, modelled once with #973's activity-level one — a path that edits it beyond displaying it is deciding that behaviour.
- **An occurrence's Waitlist is three states, and only one of them empties it** (#980 stage 2). `calendar_events.waitlist_mode` (migration 154) is nullable and three-valued, and `api/src/domain/waitlistMode.ts` is the one place that vocabulary and its meanings live — mirrored for the browser in `apps/admin/src/lib/waitlistModes.ts` and in SQL by migration 154's `chk_ce_waitlist_mode` / `chk_at_waitlist_mode`, so a new mode goes in **three** places and no router or page keeps a list of its own (`activity-types.ts` and the Activity Types page read the module's). `parseWaitlistModeInput()` is the only place a request's value is judged, and its answers are #896's for #896's reason: a field the request does not *mention* keeps what the occurrence is stored with (the `PUT` is a partial write), `null`/`''` is the deliberate "follow the Activity Type again" — `NULL` is a value here, not an absence, which is why `null → closed` is a real audited change — and anything else is a **400, never a coercion**, because `closed` and `disabled` are opposite promises to a member holding a place in the queue. `waitlistModeClosesQueue()` draws the load-bearing distinction: **`disabled` empties the waiting list, `closed` keeps it**, stopping new joins while every `waitlist_position` stays exactly where it is — a `closed` that emptied anything would take forty members' places away on the very setting a gym reaches for to avoid that. The write is the stage-1 `PUT /class-sessions/:id` and not a route of its own, so the mode and the queue it empties commit together (§9 — which is why that route's plain branch is a transaction too), and `emptyWaitlistQueue()` is the one writer: it cancels the `waitlisted` rows under `FOR UPDATE` using the existing `chk_ceb_status` vocabulary (there is no `removed` status and none may be added, #980 §5), promotes **nobody** (a closed queue is not a freed place, so it is deliberately not `cancelBooking()`), never touches a `booked` row and refunds no package credit. A new `member_notifications` type still goes in **two** places (the union and `chk_member_notifications_type`, current definition: migration 226), and this stage's two are **two** on purpose: `waitlist_closed` says the list itself is gone and `waitlist_removed` says this member was taken off one that is still open — never `event_cancelled`, since the class is still running and a waitlisted member never held a booking (§4). Staff add/remove alert too (`Q2`): `POST /bookings` with `waitlist: true` sends `waitlist_joined` and the staff `DELETE /bookings/:id` sends `waitlist_removed` when the row it cancelled was waitlisted, while a member cancelling their own place is told nothing, because they did it themselves. The admin draft is compared against the **effective** mode and never against the stored column — comparing against the column writes the inherited mode onto the occurrence on the first unrelated save and silently ends its inheritance — and the disable confirmation is a step of Save carrying the live waiting count, not a control of its own. Do not add a waitlist route beside the `PUT`, a second place that decides which mode empties the queue, a fourth mode, a new booking status for a removed member, a re-booking or restore path (nothing was taken from a member who only held a place), or a member-facing alert that words a closed list and a removed member the same way.
- **An occurrence's Eligible Professional Services inherit the Activity Type's unless the occurrence overrides them** (#980 stage 3, migration 232). `calendar_event_eligible_professional_services` is the occurrence-level half of #973's relation and `calendar_events.eligible_services_override` says whether it applies: `0` (every row written before the migration, and every new one) means the occurrence follows its Activity Type's list exactly as a NULL `waitlist_mode` follows the Activity Type's setting, `1` means the rows are the list — **an empty one included**, which under #973's `Q3 open` means any member may book this event even when the activity is restricted, and is why a list cannot say "inherit" by being empty and the flag exists. Nothing is copied at materialization and nothing propagates from the Activity Type, so an occurrence nobody edited keeps following an Activity whose list changes later. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The single `calendar_events.professional_service_id` is a different question** — which service *delivers* the occurrence (#647's slot matching) — and is untouched. **The gate reads the occurrence** (`AccessHook`'s `opts.calendarEventId`, through `loadRequiredServices()` in `api/src/api/activity-eligibility.ts`), so eligibility is a `(member, occurrence)` fact now and `GET /members/:id/personal-training-slots` asks it per occurrence (`eligibleEventIds`) over balances loaded once — never per Activity Type, which would show a slot the booking path refuses. **The `PUT /class-sessions/:id` field has three answers** (#896's, for #896's reason): an absent `eligible_professional_service_ids` keeps what the occurrence has, `null` is "follow the Activity Type again", an array becomes the occurrence's own list with a newly chosen id validated through `validateProfessionalServiceId()` and a stored one kept as is (#986's rule); the flag and the rows commit in the one transaction the Waitlist already uses, and the audit row carries `eligible_professional_service_names` previous → new, so `inherited → own` and `[PT] → []` both read as the change they are. And **the panel compares the draft against the effective list**, never the own rows, for the reason the Waitlist compares against the effective mode — an untouched form re-saved must not quietly end the inheritance — and picks from the Calendar page's own `/professional-services` read, with a stored switched-off service offered disabled. Do not copy the Activity Type's list onto occurrences, propagate an Activity edit onto them, add a second place that decides which list an occurrence is judged by, or a per-occurrence list without the flag beside it.
