- A **Promotion that reached its End Date is `expired`, not `inactive`** (#900, migration 202). `inactive` is an administrative decision and stays for ever; `expired` is the calendar catching up with a Promotion left switched on, and it means exactly what every apply path already enforced — `validatePromotionSelection()` / `applyPromotionToMembership()` refuse a Promotion whose `ends_at < now`, so `expired` is "no longer applicable" made visible. `api/src/domain/promotionLifecycle.ts` is the one place that decides it: `promotionExpiryWhereSql()` (only `active` expires — never `inactive`, `deleted` or an already-`expired` row, which is what makes the sweep idempotent — with `ends_at IS NOT NULL` and `ends_at < UTC_TIMESTAMP()`, compared in SQL so no DATETIME crosses a timezone conversion) and `PROMOTION_LIFECYCLE_STATUSES`, which both the `lifecycle_status` filter and the write validation read. A new Promotion lifecycle status therefore goes in **two** places: that module and the `chk_promotions_lifecycle_status` CHECK (current definition: migration 202). The only writer is `POST /promotion-lifecycle/run`, a system-wide sweep mounted beside the other internal runners and fired as the third step of `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml`, whose `BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET` it shares — do not give it a run-log claim (`claimRun()` guards *charging* runs against overlap; this is one idempotent `UPDATE`), a `deleted_at`-style column, an audit row (there is no `tenantCtx` actor) or a second place that derives the status at read time. It writes **`lifecycle_status` and nothing else**: an application prices from its own snapshot (#635 §16), so a Promotion expiring must never re-price, revoke or touch a `user_membership_promotions` row. On the admin side the **filter** offers Active/Inactive/Expired and the **editor** offers Active/Inactive only, with an already-`expired` row rendering that value as a *disabled* option — the status is the sweep's to write, and a select that silently dropped it would reactivate a Promotion on any unrelated save. The API still accepts `expired` on a write, because `PUT /promotions/:id` carries the status back unchanged.
- A Promotion's **`Applies To`** (#926, migration 204) says whether it is about a **Membership Plan** or about a **Product** bought on its own, and `api/src/domain/promotionTarget.ts` is the one place that decides what the two mean (`PROMOTION_TARGETS`, `targetsMembershipPlan()`), mirrored for the radio group in `apps/admin/src/lib/promotionTargets.ts` — so a new target goes in **three** places: that module, its mirror, and the `chk_promotions_applies_to` CHECK beside them. It is **configuration only**: nothing reads `promotions.applies_to` — not an apply path, not a pricing path, not a snapshot, not `isNewMember()` — so an existing Promotion behaves exactly as it did, which is also why the column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'membership_plan'` and why a client that never names it keeps creating Plan Promotions. What the target decides is which configuration the editor shows: the **Membership Fee Promotion** section (`promotion_membership_fee_benefits`) and **Suitable Membership Plans** (`promotion_membership_plans`) are **absent, not disabled**, while the target is `product`, and since #1135 **Billing & Duration** and the **Membership Fee Simulation** that projects it are too — those months are the span of the Membership Fee Benefit and of `promotionTimeline.ts`, and they decide nothing about a granted Product, whose coverage is its own grant's Duration (the bullet above) — while the **Billing Event Simulation** stays for both targets, because it is about the Products the Promotion grants and carries no Membership Fee line by design (#922). A Product Promotion does not even *fetch* the timeline. Both halves of the card ask that one predicate so a section cannot be visible in the read-only view and missing from the form. Switching the target **migrates and reinterprets nothing**: both tables keep their rows exactly as stored, so `PUT /promotions/:id` writes this column alone and a path that hides a section must stop *writing* it rather than write it empty — the Suitable Membership Plans `PUT` is replace-all, so sending the hidden draft would discard the gym's selection, which is the one way this flag goes wrong. `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it, because Duplicate is a copy. Everything else about the target is still unread and still out of scope — multiple Products on one Promotion, bundles, a per-item benefit for the target item, a Billing Event Simulation for a Product Promotion and any pricing change — so a path that acts on it beyond the above is deciding that behaviour and needs a ticket.
- A Promotion's **Membership Fee Benefit** is one row in `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` (#635 §5, migration 179), keyed to the Promotion alone — it has no Product and no `charge_type_id`, because the item is always the membership fee. It also has **no recurrence**: `action`, `value`, `enabled` and `duration_months` are the whole benefit — exactly what `MembershipFeeBenefit` (`api/src/domain/promotionApplication.ts`) hands `resolveMembershipFee()` — and migration 199 (#814) dropped the `quantity`/`frequency_interval`/`frequency_unit` triplet it had carried since 179 along with `chk_pmfb_positive` and `chk_pmfb_frequency_unit`. Those three came from `promotion_period_benefits` ("2 sessions every 3 months") and no pricing path ever read them: there is one membership fee per assignment and how often it charges is the assignment's own Billing frequency (`ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`), never a Promotion's. Do not give this benefit a quantity, a frequency or a second cadence, in the table, the singleton `PUT`, the snapshot or the editor — and note that an application snapshotted before #814 still carries the three keys, which is why `membershipFeeBenefitsFromSnapshot()` returns stored objects untouched rather than re-shaping them. Its `duration_months` is bounded by the Promotion's own timeline — Free Period + Paid Duration + Bonus Duration, never Pay Beforehand, which reclassifies paid periods rather than lengthening the Promotion (#625) — counted in **calendar months**, since #892 moved only *Membership Plan* durations onto a Billing frequency and left `promotionTimeline.ts` alone. The admin column that shows it is labelled **Duration** since #919/#920 (`promotions.col_duration`) — a UI rename only, because the number is a count of the Promotion's periods to the gym reading it: nothing converted a value, no `(months)` label may come back beside it, and the stored unit is still the calendar month this sentence describes. `PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit` is where that bound is enforced (a 400), and since #899 the editor's Duration is a **selector over the durations the Promotion can carry** (`membershipFeeDuration.ts` beside the page: `1..max`, plus the `—` option that is the null "whole Promotion" the API stores) rather than a number input clamped with `Math.min` — an out-of-range value is not offered, so nothing is silently truncated and a zero-period Promotion can carry no positive Duration. The one surviving clamp re-constrains an **already-picked** duration when the Promotion is shortened underneath it, which is what keeps that benefit saveable. It is the only Promotion benefit that changes what the membership fee bills, and `computeFinalPrice()` applies exactly one per applied Promotion. The `charge_types`-keyed tables it replaced (`promotion_charge_benefits`, `promotion_period_benefits`, `promotion_included_benefits`) are dropped: **Charge Benefits no longer exist anywhere** — not on a Plan (migration 176), not on a Promotion. Do not reintroduce a benefit that waives or discounts a Product; a Promotion's other benefits are the Product-keyed `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}` grants.
- An admin-facing **upload control that writes into a gym's own Cloudflare folder** is disabled, with the reason stated, whenever that folder cannot be written to, and one pure function decides it: `gymStorageBlock(gym, requiresGymStorage)` (`apps/admin/src/lib/gymStorageReadiness.ts`, #823) answers `'not_configured'` (the deployment has no `CLOUDFLARE_R2_*` — the API's 503), `'not_initialized'` (this gym has no `storage_folder_prefix`, so neither its folder tree nor the `themes/` root under it exists — the API's 409) or `null`. It is the only place `activeGym.storage_configured` and `activeGym.storage_folder_prefix` are read: a new control calls it rather than re-deriving the pair, and the two callers that must answer `null` stay that way — a gym that has not loaded yet is not "blocked", and `requiresGymStorage: false` keeps an object under `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (a Base Exercise's media, a Base Theme's slots) out of whichever gym a superadmin has selected. Disable the hidden `<input type="file">` together with the button — a disabled button alone is still reachable through `inputRef.current?.click()` — refuse the pick in the page handler too where the editor stages a file and uploads it on Save, and gate the *upload* only: the current image, `Remove`, the accepted formats and the size limits are not part of the block. Each block value needs its own locale key (`logo_upload_not_configured`, `members_image_upload_not_configured`, …) because the key is interpolated from the value and next-intl prints a missing key verbatim.
- A **Custom Theme logo** is an object in the gym's own Cloudflare folder, never bytes in the database: `POST /system/themes/:id/logo` is the only writer of `themes.logo_object_key`, and the key is always `<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/logo/logo.<ext>` from `buildThemeLogoKey()` (`api/src/domain/themeLogo.ts`) — the extension comes from the server-validated MIME type, never from the uploaded file name, and `CHECK chk_themes_logo_storage` (migration 180) forbids a row carrying both a key and a `logo_bytes` blob. Since #824 that key names the **theme**, not the gym: each theme of a gym holds its own logo, an upload deletes only the object *its own* row pointed at (best-effort, after the new one is stored) and touches no sibling — #713's gym-wide hand-over is gone with the gym-wide key it existed for, and the `Branding/` tree is obsolete, written by nothing — #826 removed it from Gym Bucket Initialization, and the markers an earlier run already wrote are a production sweep, not a code path. The theme's own folder and its `logo/` leaf are created on demand through `themeLogoFolderKeys()` + `ensureStorageFolders()`, and the gym-level `themes/` root deliberately is **not** — it belongs to Gym Bucket Initialization (#735), so a gym without it stays a reportable 409 and a disabled control (#823) rather than being papered over at upload time. A row written before #824 keeps its `Branding/Logo/` key untouched and still renders (the URL is derived from the *stored* key); replacing its logo is what moves it. Do not add a second logo store, a gym-scoped key, or a stored logo URL — the URL is derived from the key by `themeLogoUrl()` and returned as `logo_url`. Since #829 a **Base Theme logo is the same object one root over**: `POST`/`DELETE /platform/themes/:id/logo` write `cordel/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized name>/logo/logo.<ext>` off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` into the *same* columns (`logo_object_key` set, `logo_bytes` cleared), with the same validation, the same on-demand folders and the same sweep of the key they replaced — so `themes.logo_bytes` has **no writer left** and none may be added; a row holding one from before #829 still renders through `GET /themes/:id/logo`'s fallback and moves the next time its logo is replaced. The one difference from the gym route is what a constant root cannot have: no 409, and the platform side writes `cordel/` and `cordel/themes/` itself (`themeRootFolderKeys()`), because no Gym Bucket Initialization owns them. Each router answers 404 for the other's themes, and neither reaches into the other's root.
- **The Calendar's active and inactive areas are two theme settings, one new token and one reused** (#1214): `calendarActiveAreaBackground` (`--gd-calendar-active-area-bg`, painted on the time grid's `.fc-timegrid-slot-lane`) is the only new Global Style token, and the inactive area is the existing `calendarDisabledSlotBackground` relabelled *Calendar Inactive Area Background* — no duplicate token. The Members App inherits both per setting (`calendarActiveAreaColor`, `calendarInactiveAreaColor`, writing the same two variables), so they go in the usual three places. The non-business overlay paints above the lanes, so closed hours and holidays keep reading as inactive; operating-hours resolution is untouched. Do not repurpose `calendarBackground` or add a second inactive token.
- **The Members App's Primary and Secondary button colours are two ordinary Members App settings** (#1212): `primaryButtonColor` / `secondaryButtonColor` in the `group_members_buttons` section (*Buttons (Members App)*), inheriting `colors.primaryButton` / `colors.secondaryButton` and writing the `--gd-primary-btn` / `--gd-secondary-btn` variables `memberTheme` already reads, so every button spreading `primaryButtonStyle` / `secondaryButtonStyle` follows them with no per-component colour. Like every setting they go in **three** places (admin declaration, Members App mirror, API validator) plus the en/es/ca labels in both theme namespaces. Since #1283 the two button **text** colours are Members App settings too (`primaryButtonTextColor` / `secondaryButtonTextColor`, inheriting `colors.primaryButtonText` / `colors.secondaryButtonText`, writing `--gd-primary-btn-text` / `--gd-secondary-btn-text`), so a Cancel wearing `secondaryButtonStyle` follows both its fill and its text. Do not hard-code a button colour in a Members App component.
- The **Members App's own theme settings** are overrides, never copies (#833). `themes.tokens.membersApp` holds one key per *overridden* setting and nothing else: a key that is absent means the setting follows the **current** value of the Admin setting it inherits from, and `Restore inherited value` deletes the key (dropping the map when it empties) rather than writing the Admin value into it — a Theme created or cloned starts with no `membersApp` at all, because copying at creation turns inheritance into a snapshot and an Admin colour edited later would reach nothing. The mapping is declared **once**, in `apps/admin/src/lib/membersAppTokens.ts` (key, section, label, type, Admin source *and* its label, CSS variable), mirrored for resolution in `apps/member/src/lib/membersAppTokens.ts` and for validation in `api/src/domain/membersAppTokens.ts` (`validateMembersApp()`, reached through `validateTokens()`, so both Theme routers apply it); a new setting therefore goes in **three** places, and tests assert the three agree. Resolution is `override ?? adminSourceValue`, evaluated **per setting**, so editing one setting never implicitly overrides another, and an unusable stored value falls back to the inherited one for `calendarVarValue()`'s reason. One editor serves both Theme screens (`ThemeMembersAppEditor`, #806's rule) and holds no save state — it hands the page a token draft, so the Theme editor's Save/Cancel, dirty state and read-only mode cover these settings; there is **no Members App preview** in the Admin app, which is also why the admin's `applyTokens()` never writes a `--gd-members-*` variable. Reuse an existing Admin setting as a source rather than adding one (`Application Surface` is `advanced.modalBackground`, `Input Background` is `colors.inputBackgroundColor`, `Header Separator Width` is `colors.headerSeparatorHeight`); a source that genuinely does not exist is added as an `advanced` attribute beside the setting it belongs to **and** wired to a CSS variable a real surface reads (`headerTextFont` → `--gd-header-font`, `cardBorderWidth` → `--gd-card-border-width`), because an editable setting nothing consumes is the #677 defect. In the Members App, `ThemeProvider` calls `applyMembersAppTokens()` **after** `applyTokens()`, so a setting sharing a variable with its source (`--gd-app-bg`, `--gd-calendar-*`, `--gd-color-h1..h3`, `--gd-header-sep-*`) wins; the Section Cards border lives on `MembersSectionCard` (the one component every navigation card renders through) and never on a page. Do not add a second inheritance resolution, a per-Theme "customised" flag for a whole group, or a stored copy of an Admin value. **A setting the Admin theme has no counterpart for inherits from nothing, and says so** (#1152): the `Text (Members App)` section reads **Typography (Members App)** (six locale values; the `group_members_text` id is untouched, #1026/#970's rule), each Title carries a Font Family beside its Color — both halves of one typography level, so a typography source names its `field` and the font override writes into the level's own `--gd-font-h1..h3` exactly as the colour writes into `--gd-color-h1..h3` — and the Section Cards section holds the text's Color, Size, Font Family, Vertical Position and Horizontal Position. Colour and font have an honest source (`colors.textColor`, `typography.body.fontFamily`); size and the two positions have none, because the Admin app has no Section Cards and no setting a text's position could inherit from, so they declare `source: null` with the `default` that is today's rendering (13px, centred both ways), are stored only when overridden exactly like the rest, and the editor says *Default value* / *Restore default value* for them rather than naming a source they do not have. Reuse is still the first answer; a sourceless setting is the second and needs its reason spelled beside it — never a third `advanced` attribute wired to an Admin surface that does not want it. The three new value types (`font-size`, an integer 8–48; `align-v` over `top|center|bottom`; `align-h` over `left|center|right`, two types so one axis's word can never be stored on the other) are validated by the API and mapped to CSS by both resolvers (`alignmentCssValue()`: a card is a column flex box, so a vertical position is its `justify-content` and a horizontal one its `text-align`), and the variables carry the property value rather than the stored word so `memberChrome.ts` translates nothing. `sectionCardText` there is the one object that paints them, spread by `MembersSectionCard` **under** the caller's style: a navigation tile spells none of the five, so the Theme decides its label's colour, size, font and placement over artwork and fallback colour alike, while the dashboard's My Products & Services card keeps the `textAlign` its structured body spells (#1116) and takes the colour and font through its title. A heading reads its Title font beside its Title colour (`fontFamily: memberTheme.titleNFont`), which is also the first time the Admin's Typography Font Family reaches the Members App at all. The gate is `api/src/test/members-app-typography.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). **The text-input fill is a Members App setting too** (#1213): `inputBackgroundColor` in the `group_members_inputs` section (INPUTS (Members App)), inheriting `colors.inputBackgroundColor` and written into `--gd-input-bg`, the variable `memberTheme.inputBackground` already reads, so every `inputStyle` consumer follows it with no component spelling a colour. Do not add a second font catalogue, a per-tile text style, a sourceless setting with no default, a stored word in an alignment variable, or a text property spelled on a tile.
- **A hard reload of the Admin App paints the last applied theme, not the default** (#1299). The gym — and so its theme — only arrives after Clerk and `GET /gyms`, so `DEFAULT_TOKENS`' blue used to show first. `ThemeProvider` applies nothing while the gyms are loading and no gym is known, and after each `applyTokens()` it stores the variables that call wrote (`apps/admin/src/lib/themeSnapshot.ts`, `gd-theme-snapshot` in `localStorage`, keyed on the gym id); `THEME_SNAPSHOT_SCRIPT`, inlined in the locale layout's `<head>`, replays them before first paint only when the snapshot's gym is the stored `activeGymId`. `applyTokens()` stays the one place tokens become variables — do not add a second theme mechanism, snapshot from an editor's live preview, or time out a fallback. Gate: `apps/admin/src/test/theme-snapshot.test.ts`.
- **The Members App's context load blanks the UI only for a new identity** (#1300). `AppContext`'s load effect waits for Clerk (`isLoaded`) — treating the not-yet-loaded `isSignedIn` as signed-out flipped `loading` false and then true again on every cold start — and resets `member`/`centers`/`loading` only when the `user.id` + impersonated id + explicit `reload()` key changed (`loadedFor`); a re-run for the same key refreshes in place. Gate: `api/src/test/member-app-context-stability.unit.test.ts`. Do not reset the context on a dependency re-run for an unchanged identity, or mask a flicker with a delay.
- **The Members App spells a visual value in exactly one place** (#983): `apps/member/src/lib/memberChrome.ts`, which is to that app what `listChrome.ts` is to an Admin list and `formChrome.ts` is to the inside of an Admin card. It declares each **role** as the theme variable that holds it — `pageBackground`, `surface`, `text`/`textSecondary`/`textMuted`, `separator`, `title1..3`, the two Section Card border settings, the input pair, the primary/secondary/destructive buttons, the four status colours, and the Calendar page's own button, modal and modal-input surfaces — with the value `applyTokens()` writes as each `var()` fallback, so the fallback covers the frames before `ThemeProvider`'s effect has run and nothing else. A page or component **spreads** what it exports (`sectionCardStyle`, `rowDividerStyle`, `inputStyle`, `primaryButtonStyle`/`secondaryButtonStyle`/`destructiveButtonStyle`, `statusPillStyle(statusTone(status))`, `noticeStyle(tone)`) and never types a hex: a literal in a screen is a value the gym's Theme cannot move, which is how a dark header came to sit over white cards and near-black headings. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. `sectionCardStyle` is the **Section Cards** setting for the content cards too — `MembersSectionCard` covers the navigation tiles, and the cards of My Membership, My Training Plan, My Bookings, My Nutrition, Packages, Profile and the dashboard take the same border from this module, so neither restates it. A **status** pill or banner is one tone (`statusTone()`, shared by the dashboard, My Membership and My Bookings, which each held a copy of the same four-state map), tinted with `color-mix()` of the theme's own status colour over `surface` — over the surface, never `transparent`, so it stays opaque on a Section Card carrying artwork (#982). And a surface must never read a `--gd-sidebar-*` variable: that is the *Admin sidebar's* colour and no Members App setting can move it, which is what made the member Calendar's filter bar unthemable until it took the Calendar Buttons setting FullCalendar's own navigation buttons already follow (an Activity Type's own colour stays its own, #975). Exactly three files keep a literal, and the gate asserts the set rather than consulting it: `app/[locale]/layout.tsx`'s static `theme-color` meta (it tints the browser's chrome, is read from the document head before any gym resolves, and cannot be a CSS variable), and `AdminBar`/`ImpersonationBanner`, the two bars that say whose account a superadmin is looking at — deliberately outside the Theme, because a gym able to repaint them could make them vanish into the page it designed. The gate is `api/src/test/members-app-theme-consumption.unit.test.ts`, in the **API** suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only (#1009's reason). Do not add a second place that spells a Members App colour, a page-local palette, a second status-tone map, or a fourth carve-out without a reason of that kind.
- **There is one charting implementation, and it is a workspace both apps depend on** (#1037 stage 4). `shared/charts` (`@gymdesk/charts`) is Apache ECharts behind one abstraction — `echartsRuntime.ts` is the only file in the repository that imports ECharts and it imports it **dynamically** (a client component is still evaluated during SSR, and the `echarts` barrel pulls in every chart type the library has, so only what is drawn is registered), `lineChartOption.ts` is the pure option a chart type is drawn from, `chartTheme.ts` is where its colours come from and `EChartCanvas.tsx` is the one component that mounts, resizes, re-themes and disposes a canvas. Each app owns only the **adapter** that turns its own rows into `ChartPoint`s, with its own locale and its own labels: the layer resolves no locale key, formats no date, names no endpoint and knows no entity, so a point arrives with `label` and `valueLabel` already written. Four of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **A chart's colours are the Theme's**: ECharts paints a canvas and cannot read a CSS variable, so each role is declared as the `--gd-*` variable that holds it — the same names `applyTokens()` writes in both apps — and resolved off the element the chart sits in (a Center's own Theme therefore reaches it), with a blank value falling back rather than letting ECharts use its own palette, and a series beyond the palette's length **repeating** it rather than inventing a hue. **A period boundary is a colour change, not a gap**: `segmentPoints()` starts a new series per `group` and bridges it with the previous group's own last point, drawn with no symbol, so the line stays continuous and no measurement is reported twice — and where a `group` comes from is always the server's, never a frontend rule, which is what lets two apps' adapters answer the same chart. **The curve invents no data**: `smooth` with `smoothMonotone: 'x'`, because a plain spline overshoots and an overshoot is a value nobody recorded; points are drawn larger than the line, the value axis is `scale: true`, and the tooltip triggers on click as well as hover so a tap works on a phone. And **the wheel is left to the page**: zoom and pan are ECharts' own `inside` dataZoom with `zoomOnMouseWheel: false`, so a chart inside a scrolling card cannot trap the reader. The package has no build step — `main` is its TypeScript source and both apps list it in `transpilePackages`, so each app's own `tsc` type-checks it and each `Dockerfile` installs and copies that workspace beside its own. `api/src/test/charts-layer.unit.test.ts` is the gate (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only): no `echarts` import in either app, no quoted colour in a chart component, no `t()` inside the layer and the workspace wired into both apps. Adding a chart type is four steps in `shared/charts/README.md` — a pure option builder, its registration, a component over `EChartCanvas`, the export — and an **area** chart is `LineChart` with `area`. Do not add a second charting library, an inline SVG chart, an ECharts option built in a page, a chart component that spells a colour, or a second place that mounts a canvas.
- **An app's browser title is composed in one place per app, and only its role is code** (#1114). `apps/admin/src/lib/appTitle.ts` and `apps/member/src/lib/appTitle.ts` build `(<label>) <role> - <brand>` — `(Dev) Admin - Cordel.tech Fitness` on development, `Members - Cordel.tech` on production — and they are two modules only because the two apps share no frontend module. `APP_ROLE` is each one's single literal, because which front end a tab belongs to is not configuration; the **environment label** and the **brand** are deployment facts and come from `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_ENV_LABEL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_BRAND_NAME`, the same two names in both apps, so one environment sets them once (GitHub variables `APP_ENV_LABEL` / `APP_BRAND_NAME` → each deploy workflow's `build-args` → each Dockerfile's `ARG`/`ENV`) and the two titles cannot drift into naming different brands or different environments. Three of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **A missing value is never invented**: no brand answers the role alone (`Admin`, `Members`) and never a default brand — defaulting to the development one would label a production build *Cordel.tech Fitness* and defaulting to the production one would hide that a deployment forgot the variable — and no label answers no prefix, which is exactly what production is. **The composition is pure** and takes its environment as an argument (`appTitle(env)`), with `publicAppTitle()` beside it as the one `process.env` read, written as static `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_*` members because that is the expression Next.js substitutes at build time and the title is read in a client component too — the admin's signed-out sign-in card and `TopHeader`'s gym-name slot, which is why these are `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` at all (#1073's reason for the Google client ids). And **it is not the installed app's name**: a store build's identity is its own profile's (`apps/mobile/profiles/<id>.json`, #1074) and the PWA's is `public/manifest.json`'s, neither of which is a browser tab or carries an environment label. The title is English in every locale and is deliberately not a locale key, since it names an application and an environment rather than anything read in a language. The gate is `api/src/test/app-title.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only), which also fails the build if `Gymdesk` comes back as a string anywhere under either app's `src`. Do not spell a title in a layout or a page, add a second place that composes one, give either app a brand of its own, default a missing brand to a literal, or put the title in the locale files.
- A frontend **raw-bytes upload** goes through `uploadFetch()` (`apps/admin/src/lib/apiClient.ts`), never a hand-rolled `fetch('/api/proxy/…')`: the Next proxy forwards `x-gym-id` but cannot invent it, so an upload that sends only the bearer token reaches `tenantContext` with no gym and answers a bare `401 Unauthorized` — which is what every theme logo and Members background upload did until #824. A failure reported as a **toast** goes through `formatStorageErrorLine()` rather than a second formatter — `Toast` renders its message as one text node, so the block form's newlines collapse (#828). And a storage-backed save reports *which step* failed: the routes answer with `stage` (`api/src/domain/storageFailureStage.ts`) plus the `path` they were working on beside `describeStorageError()`'s `details`, and the page renders them through `formatStorageError()` (`apps/admin/src/lib/storageErrorMessage.ts`), naming the step itself only as the fallback for a failure that never reached storage (`err.body?.stage ?? fallbackStage`). A new stage goes in **two** places: that list and a `storage_stage_<value>` key in `apps/admin/locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json`, because the key is interpolated from the wire value and next-intl prints a missing key verbatim. Since #1042 the same answer also says **why** it failed and what to do about it: a `cause` from the closed set in `api/src/domain/storageFailureCause.ts` (`not_configured` · `not_initialized` · `not_found` · `access_denied` · `invalid_file` · `unreachable`), which the page turns into the block's *Cause* and *What you can do* lines and which gates the `Initialize bucket` button it offers beside the error. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. The cause is read from **what the storage layer answered** (`storageCauseFromDetails()` — the S3 error's own name or code first, its HTTP status second) and never from the step that was running, because a missing bucket and a refused credential break at the same step. The route states a cause the client could not derive — a 409 is also *a theme with this name already exists* and a 400 is also a rejected status value — while the admin's mirror (`apps/admin/src/lib/storageFailureCause.ts`) derives the unambiguous statuses and answers **`null`** for everything else, which renders the block exactly as #824 defined it rather than inventing an explanation. And `storageCauseSuggestsInitialize()` is the one place that decides which cause may offer to initialize anything: **`not_initialized` alone**, so a permission or network failure never sends an administrator to re-run a no-op while the real problem stands. A new cause therefore goes in **three** places: that declaration, its mirror, and a `storage_cause_<value>` + `storage_suggestion_<value>` pair in **both** theme namespaces of the three locale files. The **Next proxy** on the other side of that call forwards the response as `await res.arrayBuffer()`, never `res.text()` (#830, both apps): a UTF-8 decode turns every byte that is not valid UTF-8 into U+FFFD while the status and `Content-Type` stay correct, so nothing errors and the bytes are simply undecodable — which is how a Base Theme's blob-backed logo (`GET /themes/:id/logo`) rendered as a broken `logo preview`, and how `pdfFetch()`'s receipts were corrupted. Do not branch on the content type; `ArrayBuffer` is byte-exact for JSON too.
- A **Theme's assets** — its logo and its six Members App backgrounds — are saved by one shared sequence, `apps/admin/src/components/themes/themeAssetSave.ts` (#830), and both Theme screens (`[locale]/themes`, `[locale]/system/themes`) plan, run and report through it: the two copies that preceded it had drifted, and the Base Theme side removed a logo and a slot with no diagnostic at all. Two of its properties are the rule, not the implementation: **every asset is attempted** (`runThemeAssetOps()` collects one failure per operation and throws nothing, because a rejected `training` upload must not stop `nutrition` from being saved), and **a failed asset stays queued** (`pendingAfterFailures()`, so Save is the retry and a stored asset is never uploaded twice). Every failure is rendered as its own `formatStorageError()` block, a Members failure interpolates its slot into the heading, each failing control is marked beside itself, and a failure carrying nothing usable gets `storage_error_fallback` rather than an empty `Error:` line — a new message therefore needs its key in **both** namespaces (`gym_themes` and `themes`) in en/es/ca. The settings `PUT` is the one step that still aborts the Save, because the asset keys are built from the theme's persisted name. The module names **no** endpoint and makes no permission decision: the router root (`/system/themes` vs `/platform/themes`) and the two requests are parameters, which is what keeps the gym's module permissions and `requireSuperadmin` on the pages (#806). A preview `<img>` guards `onError` and says the image could not be loaded rather than showing the browser's broken-image icon.
- A **Gym Exercise's image** is a 2048×2048 master *and* a 512×512 thumbnail, both objects in the gym's own folder (`<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/exercises/images/<exercise_id>-<sanitized name>[-thumbnail].png` from `buildGymExerciseImageKey()`/`…ThumbnailKey()`, `api/src/domain/exerciseImages.ts`), referenced by `exercises.image_url` + `exercises.image_thumbnail_url` (migration 187). `POST /exercises/:id/image` is the only writer of that pair and `DELETE /exercises/:id/image` the only clearer; a base exercise (`gym_id IS NULL`) is 403 on both. The **browser** produces the thumbnail (#719 Q2 — do not add `sharp` or `ffmpeg` to the API image) and sends both files in one request, and the server re-validates **each file's bytes** — PNG signature, exact square, alpha channel — never the `Content-Type` header or the file name; if either fails, nothing is uploaded and nothing is written, so an invalid upload can never replace valid media, and a master is never stored without its thumbnail. Before deleting any object a gym operation asks `isGymOwnedImageUrl()` whether the key sits under *that gym's* prefix — a `cordel/…` System object, another gym's object and an external URL are never deleted (#719 §19) — and then whether another non-deleted exercise still references it, because `duplicate`/`clone`/`import` copy media *references* and two rows may share one object. There is **no runtime fallback** from a Gym Exercise to its Base Exercise in either direction: removal leaves the exercise with no image, and re-importing is how the System media comes back. Uploading or removing media never changes `cloned_from_id` — **System sourced** stays **System sourced**. Do not add a second image column, a stored object key, an `is_system_media` flag, or a new caller of the older `POST /storage/uploads/exercise-image` (one `<uuid>.png`, no thumbnail, none of these rules). Its nutrition sibling, `POST /storage/uploads/nutrition-image`, is **gone** (#1035) — see the Nutrition Library food bullet below.
- A **Gym Exercise's video** is the same shape one folder over: an MP4 *and* its 512×512 poster, both in the gym's own folder (`<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/exercises/videos/<exercise_id>-<sanitized name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png` from `buildGymExerciseVideoKey()`/`…PosterKey()`, `api/src/domain/exerciseVideos.ts`), referenced by `exercises.video_url` + `exercises.video_thumbnail_url` (migration 188). `POST /exercises/:id/video` is the only writer of that pair and `DELETE /exercises/:id/video` the only clearer, and every rule above holds unchanged — browser-made poster, per-file server validation, ownership-aware deletion through `isGymOwnedImageUrl()`, no runtime fallback to the Base Exercise, and `cloned_from_id` untouched. What differs is what "valid" means: the video is read as ISO base media boxes (`api/src/domain/mp4Video.ts` — `ftyp` brand, a `moov`, a video sample entry in an `stsd`), never by `Content-Type`, extension or `ffprobe`, so a QuickTime `.mov` renamed to `.mp4` and an audio-only file are refused; and the poster is a 512×512 PNG that is **not** required to carry an alpha channel, because a frame of video is opaque. Size is configuration, not a constant: `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB` (default 50, clamped at 200) bounds the buffered upload and the route's own `express.json` limit in `app.ts` derives from it — do not add a multipart parser, a presigned-upload path or `ffmpeg` without deciding that in the ticket. Anything that removes one kind of media checks **all four** references before deleting an object (`isMediaStillReferenced()`), because a duplicate/clone/import shares them.
- **Re-importing** a Base Exercise is the only way a Gym Exercise gets the System media back (#719 §12, part 3) — there is no "restore System media" action and no runtime fallback in either direction. `POST /exercises/import` therefore does two things: it copies a Base Exercise's four media *references* into a new copy, and for an id the gym already has it refreshes that copy's references from the Base Exercise's current ones (`refreshed` in the response, beside `imported` and `skipped`). `planExerciseMediaRefresh()` (`api/src/domain/exerciseMediaImport.ts`) is the only rule for what moves, and the `media_refreshable` flag on `GET /exercises/base` must keep mirroring it. Two of its properties are load-bearing: the image pair and the video pair move **independently**, and a pair the Base Exercise does not have restores **nothing** — a base row with no `image_url` leaves the gym's own uploaded image alone, because re-import exists to obtain System media and must never delete media the gym uploaded (that is `DELETE /exercises/:id/image`). A refresh is media-only: never write the Base Exercise's name, description, defaults or `cloned_from_id` onto a copy the gym may have edited, so a row matched by name alone keeps reading as **Custom**. Sweep the objects it stopped pointing at only after the transaction commits, through `isGymOwnedImageUrl()` + `isMediaStillReferenced()` exactly as a replacement does.
- A **Base Exercise's image** is the same pair, in the platform's own folder: `cordel/exercises/images/<exercise_id>-<sanitized name>[-thumbnail].png` from `buildBaseExerciseImageKey()`/`…ThumbnailKey()` (`api/src/domain/baseExerciseImages.ts`), built off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` because a base exercise is a `gym_id IS NULL` row and has no `storage_folder_prefix` to hang off (#716 §1, §15). It reuses `exercises.image_url` + `exercises.image_thumbnail_url` (migration 187) exactly as a Gym Exercise does — **do not add an `image_object_key` column** — and what counts as valid is not restated: both routers call `validateExerciseImagePair()`, because #716 §2/§3 are #719 §5 word for word. `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/image` (superadmin) are the only writer and clearer, and each exercise router answers for its own rows only: the platform route is 404 for a gym's exercise, the gym route 403 for a base one. Before deleting an object a platform operation asks `isPlatformOwnedExerciseImageUrl()` whether the key sits under `cordel/exercises/images/` — a gym's object and an external URL are never deleted — and then whether **any** other non-deleted exercise, in any gym, still references it, because `POST /exercises/import` copies media *references* and every gym that imported the exercise points at the platform's own object. **Generating the artwork is out of scope** (#716 Q1/Q2 as answered on the thread): there is no generator and no backfill script for exercises, so an existing Base Exercise's columns stay NULL until an administrator uploads a master, and nothing falls back to another image.
- A **Base Exercise's video** is the same MP4-plus-poster pair as a Gym Exercise's, in the platform's own folder: `cordel/exercises/videos/<exercise_id>-<sanitized name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png` from `buildBaseExerciseVideoKey()`/`…PosterKey()` (`api/src/domain/baseExerciseVideos.ts`), built off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` because a base exercise has no `storage_folder_prefix` to hang off (#717 §1). It reuses `exercises.video_url` + `exercises.video_thumbnail_url` (migration 188) exactly as a Gym Exercise does — **do not add a `video_object_key` column** (#717 Q5) — and what counts as valid is not restated: both routers call `validateExerciseVideoPair()` over `domain/mp4Video.ts` under the one `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB`, because #717 Q2/Q3 confirmed reusing #719 part 2 unchanged (no `ffprobe`, no second cap, no presigned path). The poster is **mandatory** here, not optional (#717 Q4): a failed capture fails the whole upload, and without a System poster `POST /exercises/import` has nothing to copy. `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/video` (superadmin) are the only writer and clearer, and each exercise router answers for its own rows only: the platform route is 404 for a gym's exercise, the gym route 403 for a base one. Before deleting any object a platform operation asks `isPlatformOwnedExerciseImageUrl()` **or** `isPlatformOwnedExerciseVideoUrl()` whether the key is the platform's, and then whether **any** other non-deleted exercise, in any gym, still references it through **any** of its four media columns — `POST /exercises/import` copies media *references*, so a gym that imported the exercise points at the platform's own object. There is no runtime fallback in either direction, and re-importing is how the System media comes back.
- The **Base Exercise catalogue is imported from the Free Exercise DB** (#964, migration 209), and the import is one operator script over one pure module: `npm run exercises:import-free-db` (`api/src/scripts/import-free-exercise-db.ts`, I/O only) over `api/src/domain/freeExerciseDb.ts`, which is where every mapping, the match precedence and *what a match does* are decided — not a migration (it would need a network round trip) and not an endpoint (a platform catalogue has no gym request and no `tenantCtx` actor). It writes **only** `gym_id IS NULL` rows: a Custom Gym Exercise, a training plan and an exercise assigned to a member are outside every statement it runs (§1, §14). **An operator script is only as good as its ability to run**, which is #964's reopen: every script under `api/src/scripts/` loads dotenv as a bare `import 'dotenv/config';` placed before any `../` import, because `infra/db.ts` builds its pool at module scope and a deferred `config()` in the body is reached only after that pool has already thrown — so the documented `npm run` invocation failed on a correct `api/.env` and the catalogue was never imported. Running one against a **deployed** database is a `workflow_dispatch` workflow that executes it from the VPS (`.github/workflows/exercises-import.yml`), for the reason migrations run there (the database is VCN-private), invoking the **compiled** `node dist/scripts/<name>.js` because `tsx` is a devDependency and `src/` is not in the runner image, against the image already deployed and with `--dry-run` as the dispatch default. `api/src/test/script-env-loading.unit.test.ts` is the gate for both halves. Do not add a second env-loading form, an endpoint or a migration that triggers an import, or a schedule on that workflow. Provenance is `exercises.source` (`FREE_EXERCISE_DB_SOURCE`) + `source_id`, and the stable `slug` is generated **once** from the name and never regenerated; matching is provenance → slug → exact case-folded name, where the two fallbacks adopt only a row carrying no provenance of its own and a name or slug already claimed by another source id is **reported as a potential duplicate, never merged** (§12). `base_source_key` deliberately includes soft-deleted rows, so an import somebody deleted is *skipped* rather than resurrected (§13), while `base_slug_key` covers live base rows only. The importer **never overwrites what an administrator can edit**: an update fills an empty name or description, keeps the source's own facts (the provenance pair, an adopted slug, the five metadata columns) in step and adds missing `exercise_muscles` links without removing any — which is what makes a second run report every row *unchanged*. **No image is ever imported** (§10): the dataset's `images` field is not carried into the parsed shape, so none of the four media columns and no raw GitHub URL can be written, and a unit test greps both files for them. Two mappings are the rule rather than the implementation. A source muscle reuses an existing key where this catalogue already has it under another name (`FREE_EXERCISE_DB_MUSCLE_ALIASES`: `quadriceps` → `quads`, `abdominals` → `core`) and otherwise becomes a key of its own — `lats`, `middle back` and `lower back` must **not** collapse into `back` — so adding a muscle goes in **two** places, `MUSCLE_KEYS` in `api/src/domain/muscles.ts` and a `muscles.<key>` label in `apps/admin/locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json`; a key outside the list is still stored on the link and reported, because §8 is explicit that a missing muscle may never fail the import. And **no exercise-level Exercise Type is written at all**: `exercises.exercise_type` was migration 071's and **migration 074 (#154) dropped it**, moving the measurement axis down to the exercise instance (`result_type_id` over `exercise_allowed_result_types`), so there is no column for a source type to go in — which is why #1009's gate (`api/src/test/migration-074-dropped-columns.unit.test.ts`) forbids the name. #964 shipped a mapping onto that dropped column and a `?exercise_type=` filter against it; both are gone, and nothing may reintroduce the column, a `domain/exerciseTypes.ts`-style vocabulary or a derived type beside the preserved source value. The dataset's own axis is `exercises.category` (`strength`, `cardio`, `stretching`), stored verbatim and read through `?category=`, exactly as `equipment` is. The equipment-shaped list #964 §7 calls "Exercise Type" (Bodyweight, Machine, Dumbbell, …) is the dataset's `equipment` axis, preserved verbatim in `exercises.equipment` beside `category`, `level`, `mechanic` and `force_type` (`force` is reserved in MySQL 8): do not invent an equipment taxonomy, a second exercise-type vocabulary or a parallel exercise catalogue (§11), and do not coerce a source value into a vocabulary it does not belong to — preserve it and report the ambiguity. Exercise names are English-only until a translation junction table exists (#967): no translation is invented (§4, §19).
- **The exercise lists are filtered server-side, from one declaration** (#969). The vocabulary the three exercise screens may be narrowed by — Base Exercises, the Import modal and a gym's own Exercises — and the SQL it becomes live once, in `api/src/domain/exerciseListFilters.ts` (`parseExerciseListFilter()`, `exerciseListFilterSql()`), so §19's "do not create three independent implementations of the same filtering UX" holds in the query as well as the markup: a router appends that fragment to its own scope clause and adds nothing of its own, and a context that has no such column says so with a parameter (`withSlug: false` for a gym's own exercises, which carry no slug because no editor writes one) rather than with a second query. There is **no pagination** and no `{ rows, total }` envelope — the thread's `Q3` answer is "do not paginate, display all" — so what §14 needs is a count, and the unfiltered total comes from the facets read beside the list. A **closed** set refuses an unknown value (`status`, `muscle_match` → 400); a **free-text** metadata column is simply applied, because #964 preserves `equipment`/`category`/`level`/`mechanic` verbatim from the Free Exercise DB and there is no accepted set to compare against. What the Equipment and Category dropdowns offer is therefore the values **present** in the rows in scope, never a declared list — that would be the taxonomy #964 §11 forbids — and a facet that comes back empty is a control the page does not render at all, which is also how a gym screen ends up with Search + Muscles + Status and nothing else. Each screen reads its **own** facets, one statement from `exerciseFacetsSql()` over its own scope and parameterised through `exerciseFacetsQuery()` (which repeats the scope's values once per column, because the union's arm count is that module's to know): `GET /platform/exercises/facets`, `GET /exercises/facets` (a gym's own rows) and `GET /exercises/base/facets` (the importable library) — the last two gym-facing for the reason `GET /exercises/base` is, and all three registered before `/:id`. The two gym-side lists are `withSlug: false` and the default respectively: a gym's own exercises carry no slug for §4's field to search, a Base Exercise does. And one vocabulary means no screen keeps a stricter validation of its own — `GET /exercises/base` answered `400` for a muscle key outside `MUSCLE_KEYS` until #969 stage 2 and now matches nothing, because #964 §8 lets the importer store such a key rather than fail the import and refusing it would make a legitimately stored muscle unfilterable. The ticket's `[ Exercise Type ▾ ]` **is** that `equipment` axis (the thread's `Q1`), and nothing may reintroduce a filter named after the column migration 074 dropped. On the admin side the state is one JSX-free declaration (`apps/admin/src/lib/exerciseFilters.ts` — the control values, the query they become, and the active-filter chips, each carrying the state that removing it produces) and the markup is one component (`apps/admin/src/components/exercises/ExerciseFilterBar.tsx`) over the app's own chrome: `FilterBar`/`FilterField`/`filterControlStyle`, the `MultiSelectFilter` popover, `StatusFilter` and `listNameBadgeStyle` for the chips. It declares no colour, no control height and no second filter-bar look, it takes a context's differences as props (`showSlug`, `showStatus`, `facets`), and it filters nothing — a `rows.filter()` in a page is the defect §16 exists to prevent. A qualifier that modifies a multi-select's values rather than being one of them (`Match: Any/All`, `Role: Any/Primary/Secondary`) goes in that popover's `footer`, never as two more controls on the row, and a free-text facet value's label is humanized from the stored value (`exerciseFacetValueLabel()`) rather than resolved through `t()`, since a value the dataset adds tomorrow has no locale key and next-intl prints a missing key verbatim. Do not add a second filter vocabulary, a second facets source, a page-local exercise filter or a client-side narrowing pass.
- A stored media URL is built by `buildStorageObjectUrl()` on **`CLOUDFLARE_R2_PUBLIC_URL`**, the bucket's public origin (r2.dev in development, a custom domain in production), never on `CLOUDFLARE_R2_ENDPOINT`: that is the S3 API the API uploads through, and a browser's GET on it answers `400 Authorization`, so the image renders broken. Do not point `CLOUDFLARE_R2_ENDPOINT` at the public origin either, because uploads would stop working. Rows written before the variable existed hold the private `endpoint + bucket` form until `npm run storage:rewrite-urls` rewrites them, so **one object can be stored under two URL strings**. Anything that asks "is this the same object?" or "does another row still reference it?" compares `storageKeyFromObjectUrl()` keys and matches every form (`storageObjectUrlForms()`; `mediaIdentity()` / `mediaReferenceClause()` in `api/src/domain/exerciseMediaReferences.ts` for the exercise media columns). A plain string comparison makes a sweep delete the object it has just re-uploaded under the same key.
- A **Nutrition Library food's image** is `nutrition/<food_id>-<sanitized name>.<ext>`, the same key one root apart: `cordel/nutrition/…` for a base food (`buildBaseNutritionImageKey()`, built off `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` because a `gym_id IS NULL` row has no `storage_folder_prefix` to hang off) and `<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/nutrition/…` for a gym's own (`buildGymNutritionImageKey()`), both in `api/src/domain/baseNutritionImages.ts` off the one `NUTRITION_FOLDER` and the one `sanitizeNutritionImageName()` — so do not restate the folder, the sanitizer or the shape in a second module. Since #1035 the leading token is the **food's own id**, not a UUID per upload: the key is deterministic, so replacing an image overwrites the object in place and a surface showing it cache-busts on the row's `modified_at` rather than on a new URL. A sweep therefore runs only when the key actually moved — a rename, a different format, or an image still under a pre-#1035 key (`cordel/Nutrition/`, `<prefix>/Nutrition/Images/<uuid>`) — and the gym side deletes only an object under *that gym's* prefix, so a food pointing at a `cordel/` System image or an external URL is left alone (#719 §19's rule, one table over). Nothing migrates an object a row still points at (#1035 §6): the old objects keep rendering from their stored keys, and replacing that food's image is what moves one. The two writers are `POST /platform/nutrition-library/:id/image` (superadmin) and `POST /nutrition-library/:id/image` (the gym's own, 403 on a System row exactly as the `PUT` is), each 404 for the other's rows; the generic `POST /storage/uploads/nutrition-image` is **gone**, because a route that never sees the row cannot put the food's id and name in the key — which is also why the Nutrition Library's **create** form offers no image control and says to upload it from Edit, as Cordel's Base library already did. It reuses `nutrition_library_items.image_url` (migration 138) to store the resulting Cloudflare URL: **do not add a second image column**, and do not give the base food a gym-scoped key. The name in the key comes from `sanitizeNutritionImageName()` (hyphens: `Salmon, Atlantic` → `Salmon-Atlantic`), not `sanitizeStorageFolderName()` (which deletes spaces), and the uploaded file's own name never reaches it. What counts as valid stays each side's own: the platform route validates the **file** — PNG signature, 512×512 from the IHDR, an alpha channel (`domain/pngImage.ts`) — never the `Content-Type` header or the extension, while the gym route keeps the four MIME types and the 5 MB ceiling the route it replaced accepted, which is why its extension comes from the validated MIME type rather than being a fixed `.png`. Either way nothing is uploaded and no row is written until validation passes, so a refused upload cannot disturb the image already there. Backfilling existing foods belongs in `api/src/scripts/backfill-base-nutrition-images.ts` (`npm run nutrition:base-images`) and never in a Knex migration, which must stay deterministic offline SQL; the generator behind it (`domain/nutritionImageArt.ts`) is pure and offline, because the no-AI-integration constraint above applies to image generation too.
- A Theme's **Members App background images** are eight fixed slots (`training`, `nutrition`, `calendar`, `bookings`, `background`, `membership`, since #1038 `personal_goals`, the My Goals artwork, and since #1158 `next_bookings`, the dashboard's My Next Bookings card), one `theme_member_images` row per configured slot (migration 181), each holding the R2 object key `<prefix>/themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized theme name>/members_app/<slot>.png` from `buildThemeMemberImageKey()` — the uploaded file's name and type never reach the key, and the `.png` in it is the slot's name, not a claim about the bytes (the object's validated `Content-Type` is). The prefix is the gym's `storage_folder_prefix` for a Custom Theme and `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (`cordel`, the sibling of the `gyms/` root) for a **Base Theme**, whose rows carry `gym_id IS NULL` (#732, migration 182) and are written only by `POST`/`DELETE /platform/themes/:id/members-images/:slot` — never by the gym-scoped routes, and each router answers 404 for the other's themes. A gym *reads* a Base Theme's slots (that is how its members see them), so the tenant-scoped loader takes `gym_id IS NULL` rows only for theme ids the caller already named: never relax that filter further, add a second platform root, or copy a platform object into a gym's folder. The gym's own folder tree is `GYM_FOLDERS` in `api/src/infra/storage.ts` and nothing else: Gym Bucket Initialization writes the gym-level `themes/` root (#735) and must never create a theme's own `themes/<theme_id>-<name>/members_app/` branch, which cannot exist before the theme does and is written by `ensureStorageFolders()` at upload time. Since #826 the gym root has exactly the first-level folders something writes into — `nutrition/`, `exercises/`, `themes/`, and since #1035 stage 2 `goals/` (a Personal Goal's image, `api/src/domain/personalGoalImages.ts`) — and `Branding/` (with its `Logo/`/`Images/` leaves) and `Members/` are gone: do not reintroduce a first-level folder nothing writes to, and add a genuinely new one by **appending** to that list — never in the middle, so existing folders keep the order they were written in — **with its writer**, which is what makes it legitimate at all and why `goals/` waited for stage 2 rather than arriving empty in stage 1. **Gym Bucket Initialization writes a gym's tree and nothing else** — it has never created `cordel/`, which is built by hand in the Cloudflare console (#1035 §2/§7/§10), while the platform's own upload-time markers (`baseNutritionFolderKeys()`, `themeRootFolderKeys()`, the base exercise ones) stay, since they are what make a branch nobody has created yet visible in the bucket browser. Every folder name is **lowercase** since #1035, and every marker is the key builder's own constant rather than a literal — `NUTRITION_STORAGE_FOLDER`, `EXERCISE_STORAGE_FOLDER` and its two leaves, `THEMES_FOLDER` and `GOALS_FOLDER`, all declared in `api/src/infra/storage.ts` and re-exported by the domain modules that build keys from them (`domain/baseNutritionImages.ts`, `domain/exerciseImages.ts`, `domain/exerciseVideos.ts`, `domain/themeFolders.ts`, `domain/personalGoalImages.ts`) — because a marker differing in case alone adds a *fourth* first-level folder beside the one uploads populate rather than renaming it. Spell `themes` only as `THEMES_FOLDER` (re-exported as `THEME_STORAGE_FOLDER`), `logo` and `members_app` only as `THEME_LOGO_FOLDER`/`THEME_MEMBERS_FOLDER`. Those three strings are #829's and are not to be re-cased or renamed again, and #1035's lowercasing is the last word on the other three: R2 has no directories, so a key already stored on a row is the only way back to its object — a rename creates a second tree and strands the first, which is why #829 rewrote no key, backfilled nothing and left the old `Themes/…/Logo/` and `Themes/…/Members/` objects rendering from their stored keys, and why #1035 did exactly the same for `Nutrition/`, `Nutrition/Images/` and `Exercises/` (the sweep is a production step, `docs/go-to-production.md`, and moving one is what replacing that asset does). **The row, not the object, is what makes a slot configured**: `DELETE /system/themes/:id/members-images/:slot` deletes the row and deliberately leaves the object in the bucket (#725), so a re-upload reuses the same key. A slot is added **only** through the two places below — #1038 added `personal_goals` and #1158 `next_bookings` exactly that way, with no backfill and no existing key touched — so do not add a per-slot column on `themes`, a stored URL (it is derived by `memberImageUrls()`), or a fallback to another theme or asset source: a `null` slot means the theme background colour and nothing else. Validate an upload by image **signature**, never by the `Content-Type` header. A new slot goes in **two** places: `MEMBER_IMAGE_SLOTS` in `api/src/domain/themeMemberImages.ts` **and** the `chk_theme_member_images_slot` CHECK (current definition: migration 235) — adding only the first uploads the object to R2 and *then* fails the insert, leaving an orphan and a 500 — with the admin's own `MEMBER_IMAGE_SLOTS` (`components/ThemeMembersImagesEditor.tsx`, whose order is the one the editor shows) and `MEMBER_BACKGROUND_SLOTS` (`apps/member/src/lib/membersBackground.ts`) mirroring it, and a `members_image_<slot>` label in both theme namespaces in en/es/ca. **A slot's label is the Members App's own name for the section it paints; its id is what the row and the object key are written under, and the two are renamed separately** (#1151). `membership` reads *My Products* / *Mis productos* / *Els meus productes* because #1116 renamed that dashboard card, and the id stayed `membership`: renaming an id would strand every object already stored under the old one, which is #1035's reason for rewriting no stored key, and a second slot for the same card would split one gym's artwork in two. So a rename of this kind is the six locale values and nothing else — no migration, no CHECK change, no new slot, no backfill — and the label keeps naming the same section the Members App does (`api/src/test/theme-membership-slot-label.unit.test.ts` is the gate, in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only); the editor resolves it from the slot id, so no component spells a label. In the admin those slots are **one subsection of the Members App section**, not a section of their own (#1038): `ThemeMembersAppEditor` takes the rendered `ThemeMembersImagesEditor` as its `images` prop and draws it with the same collapsible header its five setting groups use, so both Theme screens show `Members App → Images` and the editor still knows nothing about uploads, object keys or previews — the page owns the draft and performs both calls on Save. Do not give the images a top-level Theme section again (two sections labelled *Members App* is what that was), a second accordion look, or an Images subsection the page draws itself. On the member side those slots are painted in exactly two places — `MembersBackground` for the page and `MembersSectionCard` for a section surface (#728), both off `theme.members_images` and the helpers in `apps/member/src/lib/membersBackground.ts`. **Which slot a page takes is `slotForPathname()` and nowhere else**, and three slots are deliberately not in that table: `next_bookings`, because it paints a card of the dashboard and no page (#1158 — the My Bookings **page** keeps `bookings`); `personal_goals` because the My Goals page is #1036's and a slot is mapped when its page exists; and — since #984 — `calendar`: the Calendar page falls through to the general `background` slot like any other unnamed route, so the Calendar image is the dashboard **tile's** alone — a 512×512 tile stretched to `cover` behind a dense time grid reads as the tile leaking onto the page, and that screen is painted by the Calendar's own theme settings (header, buttons, time column, modal) instead. So the page follows the global Members App background image when one is configured and the resolved Members App background colour when none is, and changing the tile image cannot move it. A page is added to, or taken out of, that one map — never by a page reading `members_images` for itself and never with a second slot resolution. A surface that wants its section's artwork wraps itself in that card rather than writing a second background rule, and it never fetches, never resolves a key and never substitutes another slot's image for a `null` one. Since #982 a Section Card's artwork is the surface's **primary visual, at full opacity**: `cardBackgroundStyleValue()` takes no scrim argument at all, so the uploaded image's own colours, contrast and background are what the member sees — a black photograph reads black rather than washed out to grey — and no overlay, fade, `opacity` or theme blend may be reintroduced beside it (#728's `CARD_SCRIM_ALPHA` of 0.82 is gone, and nothing replaces it). `MembersBackground` keeps its own scrim, which is not an inconsistency: the general `background` slot is one surface under *every* page's text and controls at once, while a Section Card carries the one label its tile design already places. And a configured slot **replaces** the tile's default icon rather than layering over it — the home tiles ask `useSectionImageUrl()` (the one place that answers "does this slot have artwork") and render the emoji only when it answers `null`, with no placeholder, no second asset and no `<img>` in its place, so removing the image brings the default icon back by itself. Do not add a per-tile icon override, a scrim or opacity on a card, or a second place that decides whether a slot is configured. **A slot is named for the card it paints, never for a neighbouring one** (#1158): the ticket asked for `members_app/bookings.png`, and that key is the My Bookings tile's since #725 — the id is what the row and the object are written under, so a second card stored under it would share the tile's artwork, which the same ticket's §6 forbids — so the dashboard's My Next Bookings card is `next_bookings`, `next_bookings.png` beside `bookings.png`, each replaced in place under its own deterministic key. The card has **no background colour setting of its own**: with no image it is `sectionCardStyle`'s surface (`--gd-card-bg`, the Admin Card Background) like every other Section Card, and its body — a structure of its own, like the products card's — keeps the block layout and left alignment it spells over #1152's text placement. Since #1116 the dashboard's **My Products & Services** card — the renamed My Membership card, still `slot="membership"` — is a **cell of that same tile grid** rather than a full-width row underneath it, which is what puts it beside My Goals in the last row: it takes the tiles' own width, gap and (grid `stretch`) height, so it declares no width, no `minHeight`, no `gridColumn`, no second grid and no media query, and the cards stack by the grid's own responsive behaviour. The card's *title* is `home.products_services`, a key of its own: the My Membership **page** keeps `membership.title`, because sharing one key would rename a screen this ticket does not rename. Everything else about it is untouched — the `member_web.my_membership` flag, the `/membership` destination, the plan name, the dates and the Active badge in `statusPillStyle(statusTone(...))` (#983) — and since #1218 it wears the other tiles' pattern — icon and title as `NavTile` draws them (the theme's Section Cards text settings, no title style of its own) — with `{plan} ({status})` or `No plan`, then `{N} additional products` (`active_products_count` on `GET /me/membership`, the member's own `member_products` rows with `status = 'active'` (#1121 stage 2), never plan-snapshot lines) left-aligned at the bottom of the card; there is no status badge. The My Next Bookings empty state says only that there are none, since the Calendar tile is the entry point. The gate is `api/src/test/member-dashboard-products-card.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only). Do not give that card a grid, a width or a breakpoint of its own, a second place that titles it, or a dashboard section that reads `members_images` for itself.
- Any new code path that creates a Customer Theme (a `themes` row with a non-null `gym_id`) must capture the `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` actor snapshot in the same INSERT — today only `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` does (#712, migration 178). The columns are nullable and written once, so a path that skips them leaves that Theme with no creator forever, and the Custom Themes header renders `—`. Since #827 that same path also **checks the gym's bucket and initializes the Theme's own folders, in that order**: it resolves `gyms.storage_folder_prefix` first (503 with no `CLOUDFLARE_R2_*`, 409 with no prefix — a Theme is not created at all, because a Theme with nowhere to keep its assets is one whose every upload control is disabled and whose every upload route 409s), then writes `themeStorageFolderKeys()` (`api/src/domain/themeFolders.ts` — the theme's folder plus its `logo/` and `members_app/` leaves, which is also where both leaf names live now) through `ensureStorageFolders()`, and only then INSERTs. Storage before the row is what makes a partially created Theme impossible, so do not move the INSERT first, wrap the pair in a transaction that commits the row on a storage failure, or create the gym-level `themes/` root here (it is Gym Bucket Initialization's, #735). The markers are zero-byte keys ending in `/` — idempotent by construction, never a reason to fail — and their case is the one the key builders use, since `Logo/` beside `logo/` adds a folder rather than renaming one. A Base Theme (`gym_id IS NULL`) is governed by no gym's bucket: its objects are `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`'s, so neither the check nor this initialization applies to `POST /platform/themes` or its clone. Since #1041 that path has a third step between the folders and the INSERT: it **copies every asset the source Theme owns into the clone's own folder**, and `api/src/domain/themeAssetClone.ts` is the one place that decides what moves and where each copy lands. `planThemeAssetCopies()` names no file — a background is planned from the source's own `theme_member_images` row and keyed by `buildThemeMemberImageKey()`, the logo by `buildThemeLogoKey()` off the stored `logo_mime` — so a slot added to `MEMBER_IMAGE_SLOTS` later is copied with no change to it, and an asset the source does not configure produces no copy rather than an empty object. Four of its properties are the rule rather than the implementation. The copies run **before the row exists**, for the same reason the folders do: a clone whose assets could not be copied is never reported as created, and a failed copy sweeps the destination objects it had already written, answers `502` with `copy_logo`/`copy_members_image` as its `stage` and leaves no `themes` row — a partially cloned Theme is not a state this route can reach. Every destination key is built from the **clone's** id and name, so the clone's stored `logo_object_key` and `theme_member_images.object_key` are its own and never the source's URLs, which is what makes the two independent: replacing, removing or re-uploading anything of the source's touches a different object, and the source's own rows, keys and objects are never written, moved or deleted. The row and the references commit in **one transaction** (`theme_member_images` rows carry the clone's `gym_id`, even when the source is a Base Theme whose rows are the platform's), and a DB failure sweeps the copies too. And the copy is server-side — `copyStorageObject()` (`CopyObjectCommand`, the object's `Content-Type` travelling with it) rather than a download and re-upload — with exactly one exception: a legacy `themes.logo_bytes` logo (a Base Theme's from before #829) is not in the bucket at all, so its bytes are **uploaded** into the clone's own key, which gives the clone an R2 logo and still adds no writer to `logo_bytes`. Cloning a Base Theme reads `cordel/` and writes only under the gym's prefix, because §14 forbids initializing or copying into the platform root — which is also why the Base→Base `POST /platform/themes/clone/:sourceId` copies no assets. Do not add a second asset-copy planner, a per-slot special case, a clone that stores the source's keys, or a copy that runs after the INSERT. Since #828 a Theme's folders also have a **manual** writer on each side — `POST /system/themes/:id/storage/initialize` (gym admin, the gym's prefix) and `POST /platform/themes/:id/storage/initialize` (superadmin, `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`), each 404 for the other's rows — and both write `themeStorageFolderKeys()` and nothing else: no object is read, deleted or overwritten and no `themes` column is written, because Initialize bucket is not an edit of the Theme. A new path that needs a theme's folders calls one of those rather than becoming a third writer, the gym-level `themes/` root is still never created here (a gym with no prefix stays a 409, and the menu item is disabled through `gymStorageBlock()`), and the platform side gets no 409 counterpart, since a constant root has nothing to initialize first.
- A **member's enrollment status** — the status of their most recent `user_memberships` row — is derived in SQL in exactly one place: `latestEnrollmentStatusSql()` (`api/src/domain/memberEnrollment.ts`, #809). `GET /members` projects it as the `enrollment_status` column the Members list shows and filters on, and the Nutrition Dashboard counts a Nutrition Plan's **active members** with it; a second copy of the subquery is how a card reading `12 active members` comes to disagree with the `Enrollment status: Active` filter a gym owner applies next. A new reader calls it rather than re-spelling `ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1`, and "active member" also means `members.deleted_at IS NULL` — a soft-deleted member is gone from every other screen.
- The admin **sidebar has exactly one scroll container**: the `<nav>` inside `sidebar-panel` (#883). `.sidebar-wrapper` bounds the mobile drawer to the viewport below the top bar (`100dvh`, with the `100vh` form declared first as the fallback), the panel fills it (`flex: 1; min-height: 0`) and declares no viewport height of its own on mobile (on desktop, #1242 pins it `sticky` under the top bar at the viewport's height so the wheel scrolls the nav, still never on the wrapper) — a `min-height: 100vh` on the panel is what pushed the lower navigation items past the bottom of a phone screen with nothing able to scroll them back. Keep that `<nav>` a block container, not a column flex box: flex children shrink to fit rather than overflow, so an expanded group would squeeze its neighbours instead of scrolling. Do not add a second scroller, a fixed pixel height, a scroll affordance of its own, or a height or overflow rule on the desktop wrapper (the panel carries #1242's bound). Navigation itself stays config-driven in `config/navigationGroups.ts`. The sidebar's **dividers** are one declaration too, `apps/admin/src/components/navChrome.ts` (#1020): the 1px hairline a `NavItem.separatorAbove` draws inside a group and the 2px rule a `NavGroup.separatorAbove` draws above a first-level section sit beside each other there, so "thicker than the standard separator" is a relationship rather than two literals, and both draw in the one `rgba(255,255,255,0.15)` the rest of the sidebar's chrome uses — the `--gd-sidebar-*` set holds no separator token, so a divider never invents a colour of its own. Which section carries the rule is the config's to say beside its `icon`, never a branch on a group's id or label, and `Sidebar` is the one place that drops it for the **first visible** group; collapsed, the rule stays and only its inset narrows. Do not add a third divider spelling, a per-group colour, or a second place that decides which section is set apart. On **desktop** that panel has two widths (#1003) and `apps/admin/src/lib/sidebarCollapse.ts` is the one place that decides them: the pair itself (`sidebarWidth()` — 250px with labels since #1033, 64px icons-only), the breakpoint it applies at (`SIDEBAR_DESKTOP_MEDIA_QUERY`, mirroring AppShell's own 768/769 split rather than declaring a second one), the stored preference (`sidebarCollapsed` in `localStorage`, every access in a `try/catch`) and `navGroupContainsActivePath()`. That last one is load-bearing, because **the active navigation item is always a subsection**: collapsed, the only thing left to highlight is its parent section's icon, so both modes ask one rule and the highlight cannot move when the sidebar collapses. A collapsed section is still **not** an active navigation item — it wears the treatment an active item already has (`--gd-sidebar-selected-bg`/`-text` plus the 3px `--brand` rail, never a colour of its own), and clicking it navigates nowhere: it expands the sidebar and opens that group, which is what keeps every option reachable without a flyout menu. The expanded header is untouched, the group label becomes the icon's `title` and `aria-label` (the `<svg>` is `aria-hidden`), and `NavGroup` keeps rendering the one `<NavIcon>` at the one `NAV_ICON_SIZE` in both modes. The width lives on `.sidebar-panel`, so `main` reclaims the space by itself and AppShell is unchanged; the two collapse states are independent of which groups are open (`navGroupsExpanded`, `sessionStorage`), and the preference is ignored below the breakpoint, where the panel is the drawer. Do not add a second place that decides the collapsed width, a page- or header-level collapse control beside it, a flyout submenu for a collapsed section, a stored "active group" or a collapse that applies on mobile.
- A list page's **filter bar** is `FilterBar`/`FilterField` (+ `filterControlStyle`) and its **header band, surface, cell insets and row dividers** are `listChrome.ts` — the same constants `DataTable` is built from (#724). Never restate a header background, a control height or a row divider in a page, and never hand-roll a second filter-bar look — the small pill a row's name cell carries (`System`, and since #894 `Mandatory`) is `listNameBadgeStyle` there too — with `listNameBadgeAccentStyle` (#913) as its one accent voice, a spread of that style overriding its two colours and nothing else, which `Mandatory` wears so it is not read as chrome; a new badge picks one of the two rather than restating a look, and neither gets its own size, type or radius, distinct from `StatusBadge`, which carries a row's *state* in a column of its own: a card list and a table list have to stay the same screen. Header cells and row cells share one `LIST_COLUMNS`-derived grid inside one `overflow-x: auto` wrapper (#637).
