Since #1011 that module also decides what a list is **on a phone**, and a column declares it rather than a page: `Column.mobile` is `name` (the row's identity — pinned, truncated, its full value in the cell's `title`, and never hidden), `keep` (the primary status beside it), `actions` (the chevron / `⋮` / button cell) or `secondary`, which is the **default**, because a column that says nothing about a phone is a column the row has no room for — the defect was exactly a row whose cells had never been asked, where the fixed ones won, the name was squeezed to zero and the `⋮` went off the right edge. What those values mean is one global sheet (`LIST_RESPONSIVE_CSS`, mounted once by `AppShell` through `ListResponsiveStyles`), a sheet rather than a style object because every cell here is inline-styled and only `!important` beats an inline `display`, and the breakpoint is `LIST_MOBILE_MEDIA_QUERY` — **mirroring** AppShell's own 768/769 split as `lib/sidebarCollapse.ts` does (#1003), never a third number. Which of the two shapes a list takes is **derived, not configured**: a list whose rows expand hides its secondary columns, because their values are one tap below, and a flat list keeps them and scrolls the block between the two pinned cells *inside the list*, because hiding a value a phone has no other way to reach is not collapsing it (#1011 `Q2 scroll`). Nothing scrolls the page horizontally, and nothing applies above the breakpoint, so desktop and tablet keep #637's `LIST_MIN_WIDTH` and its scroll exactly as they were (`Q3 yes`). **How** a column is hidden is the other axis, and it is the row's shape rather than the column's: a `<table>` cell is simply hidden, while the CSS grid a page builds from its own `LIST_COLUMNS` declaration (#637's shape — Products, Members and Training Plans, and since #1011 stages 3 and 4 **every** card list of the app, each of which had restated its widths in two places or guessed them per cell: Promotions, Membership Plans, Activities, Exercises, Spaces, Centers, Staff, Professional Services, Training Plan Templates, Workout Templates, Gyms, Themes, Taxes, Nutrition Plans, Nutrition Plan Templates and Payment Providers — so a new list adopts that declaration rather than sizing cells by hand, and a list with no header band, of which there are three, still declares what each cell is on a phone) declares its **tracks on the row**, so a hidden cell there would leave its track behind and slide every cell after it under the wrong title — below the breakpoint such a row therefore stops being a grid at all (`LIST_GRID_ROW_CLASS`: a flex line, the identity taking the leftover width and the actions pushed to the end) and `LIST_MIN_WIDTH_CLASS` releases the track-sum minimum, because a row carrying only what a phone has room for has nothing to scroll. Such a page writes its cells by hand, so `listCellClasses(LIST_COLUMNS)` is how a cell reaches its column's declaration — the same `CELL_CLASS.<key>` on the header cell and the row cell, never a class spelled in the page — and the name cell carries the `title` the declaration cannot. A card list adopts that declaration rather than keeping a `rowStyle` flex row beside a hand-sized header band: the two restating the same widths at each other is how Activities' header came to fold the chevron and the `⋮` into one 68px cell against the row's two, and Centers' to carry a measured 44px inset standing in for a chevron. Do not add a second mobile rule, a page-local media query, a `gd-list-*` class spelled in a page, a second place that decides a grid row's mobile layout, or a fifth behaviour outside that declaration. See "Filter bar and list header" in `docs/feature-patterns.md`.
- A card's **own** chrome — its section headers and the hairline between its sections, the card a section's contents sit in, the box an `<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>` wears, the box a read-only value wears, the help/error lines, the `✕` that removes a row (`rowRemoveBtnStyle`, #1029) and a Save/Cancel pair — is `apps/admin/src/components/formChrome.ts` (#929), the same one-place rule `listChrome.ts` is for a list. A page spreads those objects rather than restating a padding, a radius, an uppercase heading or a button colour, and a borrowed object that needs one tweak is spread (`{ ...innerCardStyle, padding: '8px 12px' }`). Two of its properties are the rule: `formValueStyle` carries `formControlStyle`'s padding, type size and border *width* (transparent), because a value flush under a label whose input is inset by 10px moves every field sideways the moment `⋮ → Edit` opens; and a primary action is `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()` (#912) with `secondaryBtnStyle`/`secondaryBtnSmall` beside it — never a hex of its own. Which **row** that pair sits on is the same declaration's to say and not the form's, and since #1028 there are **three** of them, all **left-aligned at the fields' own content margin**: `formActionsRowStyle` is the card-level pair, under its own hairline; `inlineActionsRowStyle` is the section- and inline-editor pair, with no rule above it (#968 — the one Exercise editor, which both Exercise screens render, spelled a right-aligned `flex-end` row and a grey Cancel for itself, so its actions sat where no other inline editor's do); and `modalActionsRowStyle` is the dialog footer of the Modal CRUD shape (`CrudModal`) and of the hand-rolled `New <entity>` dialogs beside it, carrying the modal's own wider gap and no separator, since the modal's edge already is one. **None of the three declares a `justifyContent` at all** — flex's own default is the rule, so there is no second value a row could be set to by accident, and a form that spells one, or a padding or a colour there, is restating chrome. `formActionsRowStyle` was right-aligned until #1028 and some thirty entity forms declared a `flex-end` of their own, which is why Spaces' `[Cancel] [Save changes]` sat at the far right of a card whose fields start at the content margin while Products' pair sat at the left and Staff's read `[Save] [Cancel]`; every entity create/edit form spreads one of the three rows now, and the change moved **no** label, handler, permission gate, `disabled` state, locale key, payload or endpoint — what a primary action is painted in stays #912/#954's question. Exactly three files keep a right-aligned footer on purpose and are not drift: `ConfirmDialog` and `DependencyDialog` (a destructive confirmation's pair, not a form's) and `CrudModal`'s `hideSave` branch, which is how that component renders a read-only Details view whose single `Close` keeps the convention every standalone Details modal already uses — `apps/admin/src/test/form-actions-alignment.test.ts` asserts that set rather than consulting an allowlist, so a fourth one fails the build. Do not reintroduce a right-aligned form pair, a fourth action row, or a page-local `justifyContent` beside one of the three. Since #963 the **header row** of a section is `apps/admin/src/components/CardSectionHeader.tsx` — the title, then that section's contextual actions **immediately after it**, never pushed to the far edge with `justifyContent: 'space-between'`: the title is a child rather than a slot, so `[ ACTION ] SECTION TITLE` is unexpressible, the row and its action group both `flexWrap: 'wrap'` so a pair drops under the title at phone width instead of widening the card, and the heading itself is `cardSectionLabelStyle` rather than a fourth spelling of 11px/700/uppercase. What stays with a section is its *structure* (a ledger row, a weekly slot grid, a simulation's totals line), never its chrome. Two pieces of it are components rather than objects, and a card renders them rather than re-implementing them: `CardSection.tsx` (a section's heading, the hairline above it unless it is the card's `first`, and an `action` slot) and `CardDetailRow.tsx` (one horizontal `Label: Value` pair at the Membership Plan card's own 200px label width) — both presentational in `BillingDurationSummary`'s sense, so they resolve no locale key, name no endpoint and decide no permission, because whether a section carries an `Edit` button at all is the card's decision and not the section's (#897). The Member card is the reference adopter: both halves of the Profile render the same section header, the same card and the same layout (`MemberProfileLayout`, #882), so the mode swaps a cell's contents and nothing else.
- The **workout hierarchy**'s own chrome — Training Plan → Workout → Day → Block → Exercises — is `apps/admin/src/components/workoutChrome.ts` (#971), the same one-place rule `listChrome.ts` is for a list and `formChrome.ts` for the inside of a card, for the one structure three screens render: the Assigned Training Plans card, the Training Plan Templates card and Workout Templates, through the shared `WorkoutBlockBuilder`. It holds exactly the two controls they had each declared for themselves: `weekdayChipStyle` / `weekdaySelectStyle`, the training-day pill, **one object for both halves of the read-only/Edit split** so switching modes does not move the row, wearing the Theme's input pair (`--gd-input-border` / `--gd-input-bg`) at a pill radius because a weekday selector is a select; and `treeAddBtnStyle`, which is `formChrome`'s dashed `+ Add …` affordance at the tree's denser size, because `+ Block` and `+ Exercise` are secondary actions that stay lightweight — a filled `primaryBtnStyle()` is `+ Add Workout`, which opens the row's own editor. Nothing in the tree may spell a colour again: the lilacs it carried (`#eef0ff`, `#4b45c6`, `#b9b5ee`, `#6c63ff`, `#f0eeff`) followed no Theme setting, and neither does a bare `btnStyle()`, whose `--brand` is `sidebarSelectedItemBackground` — the sidebar's colour, not an action's. **And the tree is behind Edit mode**: every control in it keys off the `canWrite` it is handed, so the Assigned Training Plans card passes `canWrite && editing && !isCompleted` and an expanded plan shows the whole structure read-only — the weekday as the same chip, the workout name and each block as values, the exercise table with its sets, targets and media — with no `+ Add Workout`, no name input, no drag handle and no per-workout `⋮`, which is the app-wide "Expanding a list card reads; `⋮ → Edit` writes" rule below applied to a tree rather than a field list. Do not add a second weekday pill, a second add-button look, a read-only rendering of a block beside the builder's own, or a control in the tree that is not gated on that one expression. Since #1031 it holds **every** control the tree draws, not those two: `treeControlBox` and the `treeHeaderInputStyle` / `treeHeaderSelectStyle` / `treeCellInputStyle` / `treeComboTriggerStyle` built from it, the exercise picker's panel, search, list and options (`TREE_COMBO_ITEM_SELECTED_BG` is the Theme's app background, never the `#f0eeff` tint), the exercise table, the two drag handles, `treeBlockCardStyle` (the app's own `cardSurfaceStyle`, so a themed Card Border reaches a block too), the draft row's `✕` and `treeDropTargetStyle()`, the cross-template drop highlight — because #971 had swept two of the three screens and `WorkoutTemplateTree.tsx`, which is the **Workout Templates** card's tree, kept an un-themed copy of all of them, down to the lilac drop target. A geometry stays the tree's own (`4px 8px` at 13.5px, not `formControlStyle`'s `8px 10px` at 14px — a block header is not a form field); only the colours are shared, and a control that needs a width spreads the shared object (`{ ...treeHeaderInputStyle, width: 56 }`). Beyond the tree, that page's own form is `formChrome` like any other card's (`formFieldLabelStyle` / `formControlStyle` / `formErrorStyle` / `secondaryBtnSmall`, `CardSection` and `CardDetailRow` for its three sections and its read-only rows), its two filters are `filterControlStyle`, and its `+ Add` — like Base Workout Templates' `+ New Template` — is `primaryBtnStyle()` rather than a bare `btnStyle()`, whose `--brand` is the sidebar's colour. Do not give either tree a control style of its own again, and do not add a third tree.
- The **sidebar's first level is the nav group**, and since #884 each one carries an icon named in the config it is declared in: `icon: NavIconName` on `NavGroup` (`apps/admin/src/config/navigationGroups.ts`) is **required**, so a group added later cannot reach the sidebar without one, and `NavGroup.tsx` renders `<NavIcon name={group.icon} />` — never a branch on a group id, a label or an href. The drawings are one set, not nine: `apps/admin/src/components/icons/NavIcons.tsx` holds a single `IconShell` (the 24×24 grid, `NAV_ICON_STROKE`, the round joins, `NAV_ICON_SIZE`) and the `NAV_ICONS` registry, and `navIconNames.ts` beside it holds the JSX-free name list the pure config imports its type from — a new name goes in **both**, which `nav-icons.test.ts` asserts. An icon strokes in `currentColor` and fills nothing, because that is what makes it follow the navigation item's own active/hover colour rather than carrying a second visual state; it is `aria-hidden` and `focusable="false"`, because the accessible name is the label. It sits **beside** the expand/collapse chevron and never replaces it — the icon says which section, the chevron says whether it is open — and second-level items stay text-only. Do not add an icon library for it (nine icons do not pay for a dependency), a second icon set, an emoji, or a per-item icon in the JSX.
- **Expanding a list card reads; `⋮ → Edit` writes** (#797, #798, #799, #800, #816, #897, #957, #974, #1029). An expanded card shows the entity's own fields read-only and holds no `<input>`, `<select>`, `<textarea>`, checkbox, toggle, `<button>`, `onChange`, Save, Cancel or Edit affordance of any kind — the context menu is the single entry point into the form, and that item is gated like any other write action. The field set both halves render is declared **once**, in a module beside the page (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/members/memberProfile.ts`, `…/staff/staffProfile.ts`, `…/centers/centerProfile.ts`): the keys and their order, their read-only labels, their formatting and the persisted-row → form-values mapping the Edit action seeds with, with the row type `extends`ing it. Since #882 the **layout** is shared the same way: the read-only view is the Edit form with the inputs replaced by values, so one component (`…/members/MemberProfileLayout.tsx`) owns the grid, the field order, the labels, the full-width Notes and the position of a trailing relation, and neither half restates a grid template, a `gridColumn: '1 / -1'` or a label style — each half supplies only a cell's contents through `renderField`, which is what keeps the layout presentational and unable to make a read-only field editable. Since #974 the same holds for a **multi-section** card, and for a field only *some* rows may edit: the Products card declares its five sections and their fields once (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/financials/products/productProfile.ts`, which also holds the `type`/`status`/`enrollment_status` option sets its three forms share and the row → form mapping) and both halves render them through `ProductLayout.tsx` over the shared `CardSectionHeader` and `formChrome` — so a section cannot be present in one half and missing from the other, which `visibleProductSections({ isSystem, isSessionType })` is the single answer to. Its load-bearing part is that a column **this row** freezes comes back `editable: false` and is rendered as a *value in both modes*, exactly as #927's calculated field is: `PUT /products/:id` writes a System row's name/type/units only inside its `is_system` guard, so the form must not offer a control the route would ignore while the read-only card must still report them — hiding them in both halves to make the two agree would lose what the card already showed. The section-only question stays the draft's (the Professional Services section follows the Type being typed, not the saved one), and a section left with no visible field is dropped rather than rendered as an empty heading. The two halves never render at once either: the section that reads takes an `editing` prop keyed on the same `editingId === row.id` that opens the form, because both showing the same fields is the entity rendered twice on one page. A label or help sentence that is the *form's* stays the form's — `editLabelKey` keeps Name's required marker and `helpKey` explains how to fill a field in, so neither appears beside a value nobody can change. The read-only view renders **the same list row the form is seeded from**, never a second representation and never a second read of the entity — a second field list or a second read is how the two drift apart. Do not restate a read's business rule in the frontend either: it belongs on the route both callers use. A Member's centers carry the sole-active-center fallback (`soleActiveCenterId()` — an empty `member_centers` list is the gym's single active center, never "no centers"), and it lives on `GET /members/:memberId/centers`, which 404s a member outside the caller's gym before applying it; a Staff member, by contrast, is allowed **zero** centers (#440), so an empty list there says so in words and assumes no default. When the same entity is administered from **two pages** the shared declaration moves up rather than sideways — `apps/admin/src/components/nutritionLibrary/` holds the Nutrition Library's row shape, mapping, section order, formatters, chip style, read-only view and Details modal, and both libraries (a gym's and Cordel's Base one) import it instead of restating the columns (#799). Two rules come with that: **`⋮ → Details` carries the audit information** — the Created/Modified/Deleted By pairs and the View Audit Log link belong in that modal, not on the expanded card and never in the Edit form — and the modal renders the **list row** rather than a detail endpoint of its own, so a column it shows is a column the list returns. A "whole catalogue, assigned ones highlighted" section reuses the Edit form's selected colours through one exported style helper and renders **spans**, never disabled checkboxes. Showing an actor needs a column to read: snapshot `*_by_name` + `*_by_type` at write time (migrations 126, 178, 196), because a superadmin acting directly has no `gym_memberships` row to join to — and a shared catalogue's rows are not the reading gym's to attribute, so a gym-facing projection masks the platform actor's name (`itemDetailColumnsSql`'s `maskPlatformActors`). **The form the context menu opens is inline, in the row — never a modal** (#800): a page converted from a modal keeps `CrudModal` for its Details view only, and the inline form has to render the error line, the Save/Cancel pair and the disabled-while-saving state the modal used to give it, keep itself open on an API error with the user's input intact, and label its own controls. What it submits does not change with it: the `PUT` payload is built in the same shared module as the row → form mapping, so a column the modal carried through without rendering a control (a Center's `code`) stays in the payload as an `editable: false` field rather than silently disappearing from it. The **order** of that menu is presentation and a ticket may fix it per page — Professional Services does (#802: Duplicate first, the Deactivate·Activate slot second, Details always last), so do not "align" it back to another page's order. Red comes from `ContextMenu`'s own `danger: true` flag, never a colour in the page, and marks only what is destructive (`Deactivate` and `Delete`, never `Activate`); reordering never moves a handler, a `disabled: !canWrite` gate or a `title: readOnlyTitle`. **Creation is inline too** (#805): `+ Add <entity>` opens an inline card at the top of the list, not a modal, and it renders the *same* form body as the inline editor rather than a second copy — the section order, the row → form mapping and the payloads are declared once beside the form (`apps/admin/src/components/exercises/exerciseForm.ts`), and a column only one half may submit (Exercises' `video_url`, #717 Q6) is a parameter of that declaration plus its own payload builder, never a divergent form. A label for a fixed seeded catalogue (migration 073's nine `result_types`) is a `<thing>_<slug>` key in `apps/*/locales/base/*.json` resolved through a helper that falls back to the row's own `name`: next-intl prints a missing key verbatim, so `t(\`result_type_${slug}\`) ?? name` shows `exercises.result_type_repetitions` on screen and the `??` never fires. A card whose sections are separate sub-resources with **their own** section-level editors (#627) is still read-only when expanded: Edit mode is a mode of the one expanded body, not a second body — `isEditing` gates every section's `Edit` button and every section-level form, so both halves show the same sections and none of them can go missing from one of them (Membership Plans' PRICING was invisible to anyone editing until #816). A section-level `Edit` button belongs to that mode and nowhere else (#897 — a Promotion's four Benefit sections were editable straight from the read-only card): the header is handed its `onEdit` only inside Edit mode and `null` outside it, so the button is **absent** rather than disabled, and the card-level flag is what every section asks. The same holds for a section's own **add** action (#957 — the Member card's `+ Add Product`, which was a lilac text link offered in both halves): a shared section editor rendered by several cards takes the gate as a prop (`canAdd`, defaulting to offering it) rather than reading an entity's mode for itself, so a card with an Edit mode passes its flag and a card without one keeps the action — and the button is the Theme's `primaryBtnSmall()`, never a style of the section's own. The rule reaches a section whose rows persist through **their own** routes rather than the card's Save (#1029 — the Activity card's Schedule, whose per-rule `Edit`/`Delete` pair and `+ Add schedule rule` were offered straight from the read-only view, gated on `canWrite` alone): the section takes the card's mode as its one flag (`renderScheduleSection(row, editing)`, `true` from the edit body and `false` from the read-only one, so the two halves cannot disagree about which controls exist), outside it the rules are values with no `✕` and no `+ Add …` at all, and inside it a rule row *is* the affordance that opens the existing inline editor — a real `<button>` whose accessible name is the rule it describes — so there is no per-rule `Edit` button and no second editing state to enter. The `✕` is `formChrome`'s `rowRemoveBtnStyle` carrying the row's action as its `aria-label` (a glyph has no text of its own), the add control the shared `dashedAddBtnStyle`, and none of it changes a route, a validation or an existing 409 confirmation — the ticket is the affordance, not the behaviour. Leaving the mode clears that section's own editing state (`resetScheduleEditing()`, from Cancel, a successful Save, re-entering the mode and deleting the entity), because an open rule editor or a pending booked-occurrence confirmation left mounted would still be there the next time the card is expanded. Do not give such a section a per-rule `Edit`, a `Delete` button beside the `✕`, a second remove glyph, or a control that survives the mode. That button is one component for every card that has one — `apps/admin/src/components/SectionEditButton.tsx` (#901), rendered by both the Membership Plan and the Promotion card — and it takes its colours from the Theme's existing **Buttons** group, `primaryButton`/`primaryButtonText` (`--gd-primary-btn`/`--gd-primary-btn-text`): a page must not restate its look (Plans styled it as a brand-coloured text link and Promotions as a hardcoded lilac `btnSmall('#6c63ff')`, which is the divergence the ticket removed), and the same action must not get a Theme setting of its own — the literals are `var()` fallbacks for the frames before `applyTokens()` has run, never a second source of truth — and since #912 they are spelled in exactly one module, `apps/admin/src/components/ui.tsx`, as `primaryActionColors` with `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()` over `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()`'s geometry, which `sectionEditButtonStyle` and the Theme editor's own primary actions (`+ Assign Centers…`, `Save changes`, `+ Add`) all derive from. Since #954 that is *every* filled primary action in the admin app — the Save, Add, Create, New, Assign and Apply buttons of every page, inline editor and modal, `CrudModal`'s shared Save among them — so a `btnStyle('#6c63ff')` or a `btnSmall('#6c63ff')` anywhere outside a file picker is a regression, and `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` scans every source under `apps/admin/src` for one. A button carrying its own geometry rather than a helper's (the two Calendar detail panels' full-width actions) spreads `primaryActionColors` itself, where its `background`/`color` pair used to sit, so the `opacity`/`cursor` overrides after it still win. A new primary action calls one of those two rather than re-spelling the pair, and dropping `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()`'s argument is **not** the same thing: their default is `--brand`, which `applyTokens()` maps to `sidebarSelectedItemBackground` — the sidebar's colour, not an action's. Secondary and destructive buttons keep their own colours, and a text link is not a button (its token is `--gd-link`); neither is a selected filter chip or a card's accent border. A **file picker** was in that list too — a picker is not its form's primary action, its Save is — until #968 decided the two Exercise media controls the other way: `ExerciseImageField` and `ExerciseVideoField` take `primaryBtnSmall()` now, because the Base Exercise form's `Upload Image` / `Upload Video` were the only lilac left in a view whose Save already followed the Theme. The generic `ImageUploadField`, which that form does not render, still spells the lilac and `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` still names it, so moving it is a decision somebody takes rather than a line somebody forgets. A picker's `Remove` stays the neutral `btnSmall('#888')` either way. Since #963 that button, and every other *contextual* action of a subsection, lives **beside the section's title** in the shared `CardSectionHeader` — but since #1131 the **Save/Cancel pair of an open section editor** no longer shares that slot: it is rendered at the **bottom of the editor**, after all its fields and help text, left-aligned (`sectionSaveActions()` on the Plans page), so the actions close the form they commit; the `Edit` button stays in the header, and the editor's **error line** stays in the body under the fields it belongs to. A card's *own* form (Plans' GENERAL, the Promotion card's main configuration) is not a subsection, so its Save/Cancel stays at the end of the fields it commits — one such pair per card. The label stays the page's (`plans.edit_pricing` beside `plans.edit` beside the Promotion's `edit`), as do `disabled` and `title`, because the shared button decides no permission — a discriminator naming which section is open carries no `'main'` member, because the main configuration is a form for as long as the mode lasts. Cancelling one section closes that section alone and stays in the mode, with its own error line; only the card's own Cancel or its main save leaves it. A card with **no** main form — an Assigned Plan, every one of whose own fields is derived or frozen (#924 stage 5) — has nothing whose Cancel could leave the mode, so the same context-menu item toggles it (*Edit* / *Done Editing*) and that stays the single entry point: the applied Promotions' revoke checkbox and the Additional Products' Remove and `+ Add` are **absent** outside the mode exactly as a section `Edit` button is, and leaving it discards a half-typed draft. A section component shared with a card that is *not* behind an Edit mode takes that gate as a prop defaulting to editable (`AdditionalPeriodicServices`' `editing`, for the Member card's own section), never as a second copy of the section. The Assigned Plan card's section order is its own declaration beside the page, `ASSIGNED_PLAN_SECTION_ORDER` in `apps/admin/src/components/assignedPlan/assignedPlanProfile.ts`, and the sections one component renders are a *contiguous* slice of it (`ASSIGNED_PLAN_CONFIGURATION_SECTIONS`) — which is what keeps the card one level deep, since a nested heading the Membership Plan card has no counterpart for is the separate visual system #924 §1 forbids. An action that changes *other* rows belongs to Edit mode too, however read-only its surroundings look (Plans' "Apply new price to assigned plans" repriced every Assigned Plan from the read-only card), `⋮ → Edit` expands the row it opens so Cancel reveals the read-only view rather than collapsing it, and leaving Edit mode closes every section editor with it. The section **order** is part of the shared declaration (`PLAN_SECTION_ORDER` in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/plans/planProfile.ts`), never of the JSX, and so is a block that renders *inside* a section rather than beside it — Price History is PRICING's, as `PLAN_PRICING_SUBSECTIONS` (#881), and is deliberately absent from `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER`, since a key in both lists would render the history twice. A read-only section **two different cards present the same way** is likewise one component: the compact `Label: Value` Billing & Duration summary the Plan card and the Promotion card share (`apps/admin/src/components/BillingDurationSummary.tsx`, #879) owns the look and nothing else — which items exist, how each value is formatted and what an unset value reads as stay with each page, so a Promotion keeps omitting a zero month count while a Plan spells out *Not configured*. Do not restate that style literal in a page, and do not give a summary a control or a field's explanatory sentence — both belong to the editor behind `⋮ → Edit`. See "Read-Only Expanded Row, Editing Behind the Context Menu" and "⋮ menu order and the destructive style" in `docs/feature-patterns.md`.
- **The Assigned Plan card body is one component, and two cards render it** (#958). #806's rule applied to a *read* surface: `apps/admin/src/components/assignedPlan/AssignedPlanExpandedRow.tsx` — with `AssignedPlanConfiguration`, `AssignedPlanPromotions`, `AdditionalPeriodicServices`, `AssignedPlanDetailsModal`, `assignedPlanProfile.ts` and `types.ts` beside it — is the whole body of an Assigned Plan, rendered by the Assigned Plans page's own list and by the Member card's MEMBERSHIP PLANS section, which expands a plan card into exactly those sections. One section list, one set of `assigned_plans_page` locale keys, one `GET /user-memberships/:id` read, so `Membership Plan → Details` and `Member → Assigned plan → Expanded` are understood through the same shape and every later stage of #924 reaches both hosts at once; a second, simplified rendering of a frozen configuration is the thing the ticket forbade. The hosts differ in exactly one prop, and it is about the chrome around the sections rather than about any of their contents: `embedded` drops the body's own summary header and its `⋮`, and therefore its Edit mode — `⋮ → Edit` is the single entry point into one (#797), so the Member card's expansion **reads** the assignment and editing stays on the Assigned Plans page, where its own card lives. `embedded` is also why `editing` (`!embedded && isEditing`) is the one flag every writable section asks. The Member card's `⋮ → Details` opens that same modal through `AssignedPlanDetailsDialog`, which loads the assignment and decides nothing else, because the configuration read behind the Member card is one row per plan and carries no detail. **The list that expands into that body is one component too** (#1051): `apps/admin/src/components/assignedPlan/AssignedPlansTable.tsx` holds the columns, the chevron, the Status badge and the `AssignedPlanExpandedRow` it expands into, and the Assigned Plans page and the Member card's MEMBERSHIP PLANS section both render it — the Member card drew those same assignments as a stack of `CardDetailRow` metadata cards with a `▸/▾` toggle of its own until the ticket replaced that second rendering. Three of its properties are the rule rather than the implementation. The **scope** decides which column is the row's identity and nothing else: `gym` keeps the Assigned Plans page exactly as it was (the Member names the row, #1011's one `mobile: 'name'`), while `member` makes the **Plan** name it, because repeating the person a list is already scoped to identifies nothing and would leave a phone showing their name twice with the Plan hidden behind it. The **actions are the caller's** — `rowActions` is how a screen puts its own `⋮` on a row, so the Assigned Plans page keeps the menu inside its expanded body and the Member card keeps `Assign New Plan` · `Cancel Plan` · `Details` (and `Details` alone on a historical row, gated on that row's own `is_live`). And the **headers are the `assigned_plans_page` namespace** on both screens, so a Member-card copy of a column name cannot drift. Active and Past are two instances of that list (#1051 §4), split on `is_live` as the two card groups were, and nothing the cards showed is lost: the fee and the next billing date are the expanded body's PRICING section and Created by / Created at are in `⋮ → Details`, with the audit link. The Status column reads `lifecycle_status`, the date-aware projection of the stored `status` that `LIFECYCLE_STATUS_SQL` (`api/src/api/user-memberships.ts`) decides once in SQL for both the list and the Member's configuration read — a second CASE is how the same future-dated assignment comes to read `Active` on one screen and `Pending` on the other. There is still no `+ Add Membership Plan` while the Member holds a live one, because under #956 adding one *replaces* it and that operation is named on the row of the plan it replaces. The replacement confirmation stays the server's 409 either way: a Member covered by a family plan somebody else owns already holds one (#956 Q4). Who created an assignment is a **column** (migration 215): `user_memberships.created_by_name`/`created_by_type`, #799's snapshot pair, written by the three paths that insert a row and read as plain columns, because the Member's plan cards each show the actor and #511's `audit_logs` subquery was per row. A path that reasons about how an assignment came to exist counts **three** creation actions, one per insert path — `create`, `assign_new_plan` and the Plans page's `assign_plan` — which is both what migration 215's backfill reads and what `loadAuditMetadata()` excludes when it asks for the latest *modification*; a list missing one reports a bulk-assigned plan's creator as the last person to modify it. That backfill writes the name alone and leaves `created_by_type` NULL, because `audit_logs` records no actor kind (`sourceForRole()` maps a gym admin and a superadmin acting on the gym alike to `admin`) and inventing `staff` would mislabel every assignment a superadmin created. What stays derived is the *modified* pair, and that is not an inconsistency: the nightly run, the payment webhook and the dunning escalation all modify an assignment and none of them is a person with a name to snapshot. Do not add a second rendering of the card body or of the list around it, a second column declaration, status formatter or expand affordance for an Assigned Plan, a second Details modal, a per-host section list, a `modified_by` column beside the pair, or a client-side "does this Member have a plan" rule beside the server's.
- **A card too long to read becomes tabs, from one declaration** (#961). The Member card renders five parallel work areas — Profile · Products (the `products_services` tab, labelled *Products & Services* until #1185) · Nutrition · Personal Goals · Training Plan — and `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/members/memberTabs.ts` is the one place that says which tabs exist, in what order, and which `members.section_*` each one holds, exactly as `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER` is for the Membership Plan card: a section belongs to **exactly one** tab (asserted against the sections the card actually renders, so none can be shown twice or lost), Profile is the Member's own fields and their login, and every other section is **Products & Services**' — the sections #961's own table left unassigned (the thread's Q2) and, since #1051, **Membership Plans** too, first in that tab, which is where the Member's assigned plans are managed now (#961 §1 had kept it in Profile). The strip is the app's one tab component, `apps/admin/src/components/Tabs.tsx` — promoted out of the Nutrition Library's `LibraryTabs`, which is now a binding of it to `LIBRARY_TABS` — and it resolves no label (each page hands in a resolver, #901), declares no colour (the active tab follows `--brand`, #912/#954: no custom active-tab colour, no lilac literal), and scrolls rather than wraps at phone width. The card is **handed** its tab and renders only that tab's sections, each tab opening with `divider={false}` on its first one; the page owns the selected tab per expanded Member and mirrors it in the URL (`?member=&tab=`, an unusable value falling back to Profile through `memberTabFromParam()`). The inline Edit form is the **Profile's**, so it renders in that pane and `⋮ → Edit` selects the tab before opening it — a form must never open behind another tab, and its Save/Cancel stays with the fields it commits (#929) — while switching tabs discards nothing, the draft being the page's own state. It is a layout rule and nothing else: no section changed what it reads, writes or gates on, no endpoint, payload, permission or section heading moved with it. Do not add a second tab strip, a per-page tab look, a tab that renders a section another tab also renders, or a Member *route* (`/members/[id]`) — the detail is the expanded row, which is where the tabs live.
- **An entity administered from two pages has one editor** (#806). Exercises are edited from a gym's `[locale]/exercises` and the platform's `[locale]/cordel/exercises`, and both render `apps/admin/src/components/exercises/ExerciseEditor.tsx` over `useExerciseEditorState()` and `exerciseForm.ts` — there is no second Exercise form anywhere, so a field, a label, a section order or a validation rule is changed in one place. The split is deliberate: the **declaration** is pure (orderings and payloads are asserted directly), the **hook** owns the form values, the muscle roles, the selected result types, the validation, the error line and the `saving` flag, and the **component** is only the JSX. The editor names **no endpoint and makes no permission decision** — `onSave` and the media controls' `basePath` come from the page, which is what keeps `/exercises` (a gym's module permissions) and `/platform/exercises` (`requireSuperadmin`) out of the shared UI: do not merge the two routers, and do not put an ownership branch (`gym_id`, `cloned_from_id`, `isSuperadmin`) in the editor. A shared media control takes its route root and its storage gating as props (`ExerciseImageField`/`ExerciseVideoField`'s `basePath`, defaulting to `/exercises`, and `requiresGymStorage`, false for a Base Exercise, whose objects live under `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` and are gated by no gym's bucket). Where the two screens behaved differently, unification takes the **stricter** side — removing media asks for confirmation on both now, not neither. And a platform screen that needs a gym-facing catalogue gets its own read rather than borrowing one: `GET /platform/exercises/lookups` serves the muscle and result-type catalogues because `/muscles` and `/result-types` sit behind `tenantContext` + `requireModuleAccess('TRAINING')` + `requireFeatureEnabled('training.exercises')`, and a Base Exercises page must not break when the superadmin's *selected* gym has exercises switched off (register such a route **before** `/:id`, or Express reads its name as an id). Since #965 the **read-only half** is shared the same way and for the same reason: the expanded card is `ExerciseReadOnlyView.tsx`, the editor's counterpart over the same `exerciseForm.ts` declaration and the same `exerciseFieldChrome.ts` (so a field added to the form appears in both halves and neither can be restyled alone), the media is one `ExerciseMediaPreview` (the thumbnail, never the master; no `<video>` until the player is asked for; `playing`/`onPlay` the page's so one clip plays at a time), and `⋮ → Details` is one `ExerciseDetailModal` carrying **only** the technical metadata — the internal id, Created/Modified At & By and `ViewAuditLogButton` — because the configuration it used to restate is what the card already shows. It needs a column to read, so migration 208 gives `exercises` the `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` + `modified_by_name`/`modified_by_type` snapshot pairs (#799's rule: a Base Exercise's actor is a superadmin with no `gym_memberships` row); the **platform** router is the only writer and stamps them wherever it moves `modified_at`, the media routes included, while the gym-facing `SELECT` **masks** them on a `gym_id IS NULL` row — `GET /exercises/:id` serves base rows, and a shared catalogue's rows are not the reading gym's to attribute. Those aliases shadow the columns `e.*` now expands to, so they stay after `e.*` and that `SELECT` stays top-level (a derived table would raise ER_DUP_FIELDNAME). Do not add a second read-only view, a second media preview, a second Details modal, or an actor column beside the pair. See "When two pages edit the same entity" in `docs/feature-patterns.md`.
- Every Details view offers **View Audit Log** via the shared `apps/admin/src/components/ViewAuditLogButton.tsx` — never a hand-rolled deep link. Filter by the canonical entity type + id, never by display name. See "Details view → View Audit Log" in `docs/feature-patterns.md`.
- A gym-facing read of a **platform catalogue** (the `gym_id IS NULL` rows of a shared table) goes on the gym's own router behind `tenantContext` — never on `/platform/*`, which is `requireSuperadmin` and would lock out the gym admin who needs it (`GET /exercises/base`, #718). When a gym copies such a row into its own catalogue, the copy records provenance in **`cloned_from_id`**, and that column is the only source of truth for "where did this come from": `POST /exercises/import` reads it to decide what the gym already has, and the Exercises list derives its **System sourced** / **Custom** badge from it. Do not add a source/origin column or duplicate the library row's identity anywhere else. That copy is also the gym's **import state**, and importing is what makes the row visible to the gym (#804): `GET /exercises` filters `e.gym_id = ?` and never `OR e.gym_id IS NULL`, because existing in the platform library is not the same as having been imported — a base row a gym never imported has no business in that gym's Exercises page, under any filter, search or ordering, and adding one to the library must not make it appear in every gym at once. The filter belongs in the list query, not on one control, and there is no `imported` flag to add: the copy is the state. The library is read through `GET /exercises/base` (the Import modal), which keeps offering a non-imported row, and `GET /exercises/:id` still answers for a base row; the write paths already assume this rule, since `workout-templates.ts` and `training-plans.ts` validate an `exercise_id` with `gym_id = ?`.
- A **CodeQL alert** is cleared by fixing the code, by a human dismissing it in Security → Code scanning, or by an owner-authorized merge that records the assessment — **never by a suppression comment**. Neither `// codeql[rule]` nor `// lgtm[rule]` does anything in this repository (both were tried on the same alert, #767), so a suppression comment only misleads the next reader: `api/src/test/codeql-suppressions.unit.test.ts` fails the build if one is added. Put the reasoning in prose at the sink instead, and see "Code scanning (CodeQL)" in `docs/architecture.md` for the known `js/xss-through-dom` false positive on detached media elements, which every browser upload helper of that shape will raise again.
- What the API tells a client about a **failure that reached the global error handler** is decided in exactly one place: `api/src/domain/httpErrorResponse.ts` (#966). An error carrying an explicit HTTP `status` — the `throw Object.assign(new Error(m), { status: 400 })` shape `centerContext.ts` uses, body-parser's own 400/413 — is a *deliberate* answer and keeps its message; anything else is an unexpected failure, so the client gets `500 { error: 'Internal server error' }` and the real error is logged server-side and nowhere else. The handler used to answer `err?.message` verbatim, which is how `Unknown column 'b.result_type' in 'field list'` reached a gym owner's toast. A route that needs a specific message for a specific failure gives the error a status or answers in the route — never by relying on the handler to forward a driver's words — and `handleDupEntry()`'s 409 and the storage routes' own `stage`/`path`/`details` body (#828) are unaffected, because neither reaches the handler. The same ticket's defect is the rule beside it: an **exercise instance's result type is the exercise's, never its block's** — migration 074 (#154) dropped `workout_template_blocks.result_type` and `workout_blocks.result_type` and put `result_type_id` + `target_value`/`min_value`/`max_value`/`unit` on `workout_template_exercises`/`workout_exercises` — so a JSON tree projects it from the exercise with a **LEFT** JOIN onto `result_types` (an INNER JOIN drops every exercise configured with none), exactly as `workout-templates.ts` `GET /:id` does. #1009 is the ticket that decided the remaining question: a **block has no result type to snapshot**, so `workout_block_logs.result_type` is **dropped** (migration 209) rather than re-vocabularied — nothing read it (every reader is a blanket `SELECT *`/`SELECT wbl.*` with no consumer: no admin caller for `GET /members/:memberId/workout-block-logs`, none for `GET /me/workout-block-logs`, no report), so keeping it would have meant inventing what a block's result type means when its exercises disagree and backfilling the rows holding `Rounds`/`Score`, which have no member of the `result_types` slug vocabulary, with that invention. What survives is `result_value`: **one optional, untyped free-text value** the member typed for the block ("21:04", "7 rounds + 3"), so no path may give it a type column, a derived type, a coerced legacy value or a per-block "takes no result" flag — an empty input is `null`. #1232 amends this without contradicting it: a block now carries an optional **`result_unit`** (`workout_template_blocks`, `workout_blocks`, migration 241; NULL = the block records no global result, which is every existing block), declared with the block types that may use one (Circuit, EMOM, AMRAP, Tabata) and the units each allows in `api/src/domain/blockResultUnits.ts` — the one place that vocabulary and `parseBlockResultUnit()` / `parseBlockLogResult()` live, mirrored for the Result Unit select in `apps/admin/.../workout-templates/blockFieldConfig.ts` (no CHECK, so a new unit goes in the declaration, that mirror and a label key in both apps). The unit is **snapshotted onto each log** (`workout_block_logs.result_unit`, written with the value) so a result stays interpretable after the block is reconfigured; `POST`/`PUT /me/workout-block-logs` refuse a `result_value` on a block with no unit and validate the number against the unit (an empty value is always allowed — Mark done is independent of the result); every path that copies a block (Duplicate, template → plan, assign) copies the unit. The Members App takes the unit from the API (`lib/blockResult.ts`) and shows no input for a block without one. `exercise_logs` / `exercise_log_sets` are untouched. Typed, per-set results are the exercise instance's, in `exercise_logs` + `exercise_log_sets`. Three queries read one of 074's dropped columns before this was gated, found one ticket at a time — the two trees plus the Members App's My Training page, where `block.result_type` was `undefined` and `undefined.toLowerCase()` threw while rendering any block, so the page was down ahead of the 500 that was reported. `api/src/test/migration-074-dropped-columns.unit.test.ts` is now the gate: no production source under `api/src` or `apps/member/src` may name `result_type`, `exercise_type`, `distance_value` or `distance_unit`, because after migration 209 **no table has a column with any of those names** — and `duration_seconds` is deliberately outside that set, since 074 dropped it from `workout_template_exercises` only and it is still a real column of `workout_blocks`, `workout_template_blocks` and `exercise_logs`. Note that CI runs `npm test` in `api/` **only** (the admin job type-checks and builds), which is why that gate scans the Members App from the API suite rather than living in `apps/member/src/test`.
- A **receipt** ("factura simplificada") is issued for money *actually received*, and there is exactly one implementation of that rule: `isReceiptableEvent()` (`api/src/domain/billingEventStatus.ts`, #787). The event type must record a payment (`payment_recorded`, `recurring_payment`, `failed_billing`) **and** `deriveBillingEventStatus()` must say `paid` — which is what makes a `failed_billing` settled afterwards by Retry or Manual payment receipt-able without a special case, since #640's actions never append a second ledger row. A `waived_billing`, an `adjustment` and anything failed, expired or pending are refused. Do not add a second eligibility check, and do not put one on the `GET` routes: a `receipt_number` can only exist if the predicate already allowed it. The number itself comes from `issueReceiptNumber()` (`api/src/domain/receiptNumbers.ts`) and nowhere else — it reads the event `FOR UPDATE` before spending anything, so one payment can never burn two numbers out of the gapless per-gym, per-year `receipt_sequences`. The nightly run allocates one for every charge it settles, **after** the charge transaction has committed and in one of its own, with the failure logged and swallowed: that transaction records money the provider has already taken, so a new statement inside it can roll the charge record back while the charge stands (the `insertId` defect #635 stage 3 fixed). A receipt that fails to auto-issue stays issuable through `POST /payments/:id/receipt`, so it is never worth that risk, and `receipts_issued` is a reported counter, not a run-log column and never a reason to fail a run.
- A **`pending` payment request whose checkout page was opened is not stale** (#789). `payment_requests` carries two deadlines and they are not interchangeable: `page_token_expires` (creation + 10 min) bounds how long the *link* may be opened, and `GET /payment-page/token/:token` — the only writer that clears `page_token` on a row still `pending` — is what records that it was. `POST /billing/cleanup` therefore expires a request whose `page_token IS NOT NULL` with its token, and one whose `page_token IS NULL` only after `abandonedRequestHours()` (`api/src/domain/paymentRequestExpiry.ts`, `PAYMENT_REQUEST_ABANDONED_HOURS`, default 24, floored at 1 so a typo cannot reintroduce the defect). Do not reintroduce a single deadline, and do not add a second query or a shorter window that expires an opened request: the member is in the Card Input and a 3DS redirect while it stands, and an `expired` row is skipped by the webhook's own guard — which is how a member came to be charged with no completed request, no stored card and no `next_billing_date`. The other half of that rule lives in the webhook: **only a `completed` payload may reopen a terminal row**, and only an `expired` one, because the provider is the source of truth about money. A `failed`/`expired` payload never revives a row, and an already-`completed` row is always skipped — that skip is the handler's idempotency under Monei's retries, so nothing may widen it further. `expired` stays the cleanup response's total, because `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` parses that field (#778).
- **Replacing a member's stored card charges nothing** (#788, migration 195). It is a card *verification*: `createCardVerificationRequest()` (`api/src/payments/provider.ts`) deliberately takes **no amount**, and the Monei adapter sends `amount: 0` + `transactionType: 'VERIF'` + `generatePaymentToken: true` — do not give that method an amount, a void path or a second provider call, and do not raise a real payment to obtain a token (the Start payment button did, which is the defect the ticket removed). The `payment_requests` row it writes exists only to carry the hosted page's `page_token` and the webhook's `provider_order`: `source = 'card_update'`, `amount = 0.00`, `charge_type_id` **NULL** — never a charge type (a `card_verification` entry in `charge_types` would put a non-charge in the cash-payment picker and in charge-type reporting). It is therefore **not a financial row**: anything that reads `payment_requests` as money excludes that source, as `GET /members`' `payment_status`, `GET /payment-requests` and `GET /me/payment-requests` do, and the webhook's card-update branch writes **no** `billing_event`, stamps no `next_billing_date` and clears none of #785's dunning counters — replacing a card is not paying, and the rejected cycle is still owed. A verification that is rejected, or completes without a token, must leave the card already on file untouched. `domain/storedCards.ts` owns the constant and the pure rules (including `cardRemovalBlock()`: removal is refused while any assignment is `active`/`paused` with a `next_billing_date`, because the nightly run skips a card-less assignment *silently*) and `api/card-updates.ts` the provider call, the insert and the reads — a new surface (staff or member) calls those rather than restating either.
- **The hosted payment page's return URLs name no language** (#1081). `PAYMENT_OK_URL` / `PAYMENT_KO_URL` are **one deploy-time value for every member of every gym**, so the API cannot build them per member and must not configure a locale into them: a fixed `https://members…/es/payment/success` landed every member on the Spanish result page after paying. They point at the locale-less `…/payment/success` and `…/payment/error`, and the **Members App's middleware is what localizes the landing** — next-intl's locale detection redirects to the `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie's language, then `Accept-Language`, then `en`, preserving the query string that carries `purpose=card_update` (#788); a signed-in member whose stored `preferred_locale` differs is then moved onto it by `MemberLocalePreference` (#1039), which the locale layout mounts over these two routes like any other, so the stored preference has the last word. Both pages stay **outside** `isPublicRoute` on purpose, because each polls an authenticated route and there is nothing to show a visitor with no session. `api/src/test/payment-return-locale.unit.test.ts` is the gate (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only): nothing at runtime notices a locale creeping back, since the page renders perfectly well in one language. Do not put a locale segment or a query string in either variable or in `api/.env.example`, switch next-intl's locale detection off, add a second place that decides the return's language, or render both languages and hide one client-side — the redirect keeps the page server-rendered in one language with no flash.
- **Monei's webhook is relayed by the isolated payment app, and the relay decides nothing** (#1083, `docs/decisions.md` #19). `PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL` points at `https://pay.…/webhooks/payment`, and `apps/payment`'s nginx — which is already that container's runtime and already proxies `/payment-page/` and `/themes/` for the browser — hands the request to the API's own `/webhooks/payment` at its **internal** address. One `location = /webhooks/payment` block in `apps/payment/templates/default.conf.template` is the whole relay: do not add a Node runtime, a second relay path, a parser, or anything that re-serializes the body — the API verifies the HMAC over the exact bytes Monei signed, so `parseWebhook()` stays the route's first operation and `MONEI_WEBHOOK_SECRET` never leaves the API. Three of its answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **Monei is answered with the API's own status code**: `proxy_intercept_errors` stays off and that block carries no `return` and no `error_page`, because a blanket 200 loses a payment confirmation (the provider retries exactly what we report as failed) while an unreachable or slow API is a 502/504, which is a retry too. **The webhook's 60/min budget is keyed on the paying client, never on the relay** — it is the one route sitting behind one more proxy than `trust proxy` accounts for, so `paymentWebhookClientKey()` (`api/src/domain/forwardedClient.ts`, the one place `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` is read) adds `PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_RELAY_HOPS` to it; keyed on the relay, one gym's payments spend every gym's budget. That hop is declared **per route and defaults to 0** (where the key is `req.ip`, the pre-#1083 shape) and must never be replaced by a higher global `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, which would make Express trust one more caller-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` entry on every route a caller can still reach. And **both API origins the payment image talks to are configuration**, not literals — `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_PUBLIC_URL` for the browser-facing proxies, `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_INTERNAL_URL` for the relay, with no trailing slash — because one image tag serves dev and pro: the config is an `envsubst` template the official nginx image substitutes at start, and the Dockerfile's dev defaults are what keep a container started with no environment behaving as before. The container's own access log spells no query string (`log_format pay_no_query`, the PCI rule this host already carried for `?token=<page_token>`), and `api/src/test/payment-webhook-relay.unit.test.ts` is the gate, in the API suite because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only. This covers Monei alone: Clerk's webhook took the same shape one app over (#1085, the bullet below) and the nightly runs took it in #1086 (the bullet after it), so whether the API's own host is then closed off is #1087's question rather than any of theirs.
- **Clerk's webhook is relayed by the admin app, and the relay forwards rather than verifies** (#1085, `docs/decisions.md` #20). Clerk's dashboard endpoint is `https://admin.…/api/webhooks/clerk`, a Next route handler (`apps/admin/src/app/api/webhooks/clerk/route.ts`) that hands the request to the API's own `/webhooks/clerk` at `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL`. What it forwards, where, and what it answers when it cannot are `apps/admin/src/lib/clerkWebhookRelay.ts`' and **pure** — no `fetch`, no `next/server`, no `process.env` — with the route as the I/O half deciding nothing, the same two-module split WP2 and WP3 use. Four of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The relay verifies nothing**: `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` stays in the API, whose route still parses its own raw body and makes `verifyWebhook()` its first operation, so a tampered body is a 400 *from the API*, relayed as such — the relay is a transport, never a second trust boundary. **The body crosses as bytes** (`req.arrayBuffer()` in, the same buffer out) together with all three `svix-*` headers, because the signature is computed over `svix-id.svix-timestamp.<body>`: a `req.text()`, a `JSON.stringify` or a dropped header breaks an HMAC this app cannot recompute (#830's rule, one direction over). **Clerk is answered with the API's own status code** and never a blanket 200, because Clerk retries exactly what we report as failed and a swallowed `user.deleted` leaves an orphaned Clerk account linked to rows that should have been cleaned up (#709) — an unreachable API is a **502**, an unset `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL` a **500**, and both are replayable from the endpoint's *Replay → Replay missing messages*. And **`/api/proxy` is not reusable for this**: its header set is `authorization`, `x-gym-id`, `x-center-id`, `x-impersonate-as`, `x-locale` and `content-type` — a caller's own credentials, and none of the `svix-*` ones — so a webhook sent through it arrives unsigned; the relay's set is the three Svix headers plus the content type, declared once, with no cookie, Authorization or `host`. The path is exempt from `auth.protect()` **and** from the locale routing in `apps/admin/src/middleware.ts`, through the early `return NextResponse.next()` `/api/proxy` already uses: one without the other answers Clerk with a 401 or a redirect to `/en/…`. The **admin** app, not the members app — the two have identical middleware and proxy, so there is no technical difference and one endpoint is one thing to keep in step with the Clerk dashboard. The gate is `api/src/test/clerk-webhook-relay.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only), with the admin half's own in `apps/admin/src/test/clerk-webhook-relay.test.ts` and `clerk-webhook-relay-route.test.ts`. Do not add a second relay path or a members-app counterpart, send the webhook through `/api/proxy`, verify or parse the signature outside the API, read the body as text, answer Clerk a status the API did not give, or hard-code an API origin in either half.
- **The GitHub Actions nightly runs are relayed by the admin app, behind an explicit path allowlist** (#1086, `docs/decisions.md` #21). `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` and `recurring-booking-run.yml` call `$API_BASE_URL/<path>` where `API_BASE_URL` is `https://admin.…/api/internal` — the admin origin **plus the prefix**, which is why the workflows' own URLs are unchanged — and `apps/admin/src/app/api/internal/[...path]/route.ts` hands each POST to the API's own route at `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL`. Which paths may be relayed, what crosses the boundary and what the relay answers when it cannot are `apps/admin/src/lib/internalRunRelay.ts`' and **pure** (no `fetch`, no `node:http`, no `next/server`, no `process.env`), `lib/internalRunUpstream.ts` is the transport and the route is the glue — #1085's split with one extra half, for the fourth reason below. Five of their answers are the rule rather than the implementation. **The relay authenticates nothing**: `BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET` and `RECURRING_BOOKINGS_INTERNAL_SECRET` stay in the API, whose routers still compare the header themselves (`checkInternalSecret()`), so a wrong secret is a `401` *from the API* — relayed as such, and still spending #783's failed-secret budget. **Only the four internal runs may be relayed**: `INTERNAL_RUN_API_PATHS` (`/billing/run`, `/billing/cleanup`, `/promotion-lifecycle/run`, `/recurring-bookings/run`) is matched on the whole joined path, so neither a prefix nor a traversal nor a case variant resolves to one and anything else under that public prefix is a **404 from the relay** rather than a request the API has to judge. **The workflow is answered with the API's own status and body, byte for byte**, because both workflows fail the job on a non-2xx *and* parse the run's counters out of the body (#778): the three statuses that are the relay's own are that `404`, a **502**/**504** (unreachable, or outlived `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_TIMEOUT_MS`) and a **500** (no `CORDEL_FITNESS_API_URL`), and never a 200, which would be reported as an unreadable body instead of as the API being out of reach. **The upstream call is `node:http`, never `fetch`** — undici abandons a response whose headers take more than 300 s while `POST /recurring-bookings/run` is allowed 600 s, so a long night would be cut off at five minutes, and a run the API completed but the workflow saw as failed is the worst outcome available (#780's guard then refuses the retry as `already_completed_today`); the relay's wait defaults to 660 000 ms, above the longest `--max-time` in `.github/workflows/`, so `curl` is always the first to give up. And **the internal-run budget is keyed on the client, never on the relay**: `internalRunClientKey()` (`api/src/domain/forwardedClient.ts`) adds `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_HOPS` to `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` and reads the forwarded chain inwards from the socket, because only a 401 spends that budget — keyed on the relay, ten wrong guesses from anywhere answer the nightly run `429` for the rest of the window and a night nobody is charged on has to be caught up by hand. That hop is declared **per route and defaults to 0** (where the key is `req.ip`, the pre-#1086 shape) and must never be replaced by a higher global `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, for #1083's reason. `/api/proxy` is **not reusable for this** either: its set carries `authorization`, `x-gym-id`, `x-center-id`, `x-impersonate-as` and `x-locale` and not `x-internal-secret`, so a run sent through it arrives unauthenticated, and widening that set would put the internal runners behind a route the browser app also uses; the relay's set is `x-internal-secret`, `content-type` and `x-forwarded-for`, declared once, with no cookie, Authorization or `host`. The path is exempt from `auth.protect()` **and** from the locale routing in `apps/admin/src/middleware.ts`, through the same early `return NextResponse.next()`. The gate is `api/src/test/internal-run-relay.unit.test.ts` (in the API suite, because CI runs `npm test` in `api/` only — it also asserts the allowlist *is* the set the two workflows call, so a fifth run cannot be added to one and forgotten in the other), with `api/src/test/internal-run-relay.test.ts` for the limiter key and the admin half's own in `apps/admin/src/test/internal-run-relay.test.ts`, `internal-run-relay-route.test.ts` and `internal-run-upstream.test.ts`. Do not add a second relay path or a members-app counterpart, send a run through `/api/proxy`, widen the allowlist to a prefix or a pass-through, compare a secret outside the API, answer the workflow a status the API did not give, reshape its counters, use `fetch` for the upstream call, or hard-code an admin or API origin in any half. **Grafana's read of `/health/runs` is a fourth relay of the same family** (#1166): `apps/admin/src/app/api/health/runs/route.ts` over the pure `lib/healthRunsRelay.ts`, a `GET` of **one fixed path** — no catch-all segment and no path taken from the request, so this public URL cannot become a way into any other unauthenticated API route — that forwards **no request header**, relays the API's own status and bytes and answers 500/502/504 of its own, never a 200, because the freshness rules (#872) are Error → Alerting; the exact path is exempt from both middleware guards, and it keys the global limiter on the admin app (the bucket `/api/proxy` already shares) rather than declaring a hop. Gate: `api/src/test/health-runs-relay.unit.test.ts`. Do not widen it into a GET pass-through. **Gym websites' sign-ups are the fifth** (#1175): `apps/admin/src/app/api/public/gyms/[gymRef]/registrations/route.ts` over the pure `lib/publicRegistrationRelay.ts` relays `POST /public/gyms/:gymRef/registrations` and nothing else under `/api/public` — `<gymRef>` is one raw segment read from the still-encoded pathname and forwarded as received, a traversal or an encoded separator is the relay's own 404 — forwarding `x-api-key`, `content-type` and `x-forwarded-for` only; the API still compares the key and its statuses are passed through, the relay's own 500/502/504 are never a 2xx, and the route's per-IP limiter keys through `publicRegistrationClientKey()` (`PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_RELAY_HOPS`, default 0 = `req.ip`), #1086's per-route device, so one website cannot spend every gym's budget. What Website Integration tells a gym is `PUBLIC_REGISTRATION_BASE_URL` + the path and **nothing else**: deriving it from another variable (`PAYMENT_NOTIFICATION_URL`, until #1175) is how every gym came to be shown a payment-host URL that answers 405 once #1083 moved that variable. Gate: `api/src/test/public-registration-relay.unit.test.ts`. Do not relay a second `/public` route through it, verify a key outside the API, or reintroduce a fallback for that base. **Every hop count and every internal secret the API reads reaches it through `deploy.yml`** (#1177): that job deletes every `Environment=` line of the `fitness-api` unit and writes its own block, so it is the API's **only** environment writer and a value set by hand on the VPS is gone after the next deploy — which is how `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, `PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_RELAY_HOPS` and `INTERNAL_RUN_RELAY_HOPS` never took effect and how #1113's reminder run answered 401 on every pass. A hop is a GitHub **variable** and a secret a GitHub **secret**, each mapped in the step, listed in `envs:` and written as a `NAME=${NAME:-}` line of the API's env file; `api/src/test/deploy-env-forwarding.unit.test.ts` derives both lists from the code (`env.*_HOPS` in `forwardedClient.ts`, `process.env.*_INTERNAL_SECRET` anywhere under `api/src`) and fails when one is not forwarded. Never document a setting as "set it on the unit". **And no value ever goes on podman's command line** (#1192): journald records the command line of every process that logs to it in the `_CMDLINE` field, podman logs to it when it starts a container, and the hosts' Fleet-managed collector ships whole journal entries to Grafana Cloud Loki — so the inline `Environment=KEY=value` lines `deploy.yml` wrote until #1192 (which quadlet turns into `--env KEY=value`) and the migration's `-e DATABASE_URL=…` put every dev secret there. The API's environment is an owner-only file (`~/.config/fitness-api/api.env`, `700`/`600`, written to a temp file and moved into place) that the unit names with one `EnvironmentFile=` line, and a one-off container takes a value as `-e NAME` with no value, copied from the calling shell's environment (`mysql` likewise reads `MYSQL_PWD` rather than `-p<password>`). `api/src/test/podman-cmdline-secrets.unit.test.ts` fails the build if a workflow passes `-e/--env NAME=value` or a `-p` password to `podman run`, or writes an inline `Environment=` value into a unit. The same block also writes **`LogDriver=journald`** into that unit (#1186): it named no log driver, so not one line the API wrote reached Loki while corfront's three units (`LogDriver=journald`) did.
- An amount crosses the **PaymentProvider boundary in minor units** (cents for EUR): every caller of `createPaymentRequest()` and `executeRecurring()` converts through `toMinorUnits()` (`api/src/payments/money.ts`), while everything on our side — `user_memberships.membership_fee_price`, `billing_events.amount`, `payment_requests.amount`, what `resolveMembershipFee()` and `priceMembershipFeeOn()` return — stays a decimal number of euros. Do not add a caller that passes a euro amount to the provider or a provider that expects euros: the nightly run and the staff Retry did exactly that for as long as the provider was stubbed in tests, and a real renewal of a 29.99 € fee would have charged twenty-nine cents. A test of a provider call asserts the `amount` the stub received.
- All config via environment variables. No hardcoded values. Payment provider credentials in particular stay in the environment — the `payment_providers` catalogue (#636) names *which* adapter a gym uses (`provider_key`), never how to authenticate as it.
- Every new code path that inserts into `gyms` must set `payment_provider_id` (NOT NULL since migration 175). Take the catalogue's default (`is_default = 1 AND deleted_at IS NULL`) unless the caller names one — `api/src/api/gyms.ts`, `api/src/test/helpers.ts` and `api/src/infra/seed.ts` are the three existing paths.
- Translated **UI labels** live in `apps/*/locales/base/{en,es,ca}.json`. Translated **data** lives in a `(entity_id, locale)` junction table with the entity's own column as the base value and fallback — never one row per language, and never a `name_es`/`name_ca` column. Resolve it server-side from the request locale (`api/src/infra/locale.ts`) and return it in a separate field (`display_name`), leaving the base column for edit forms to submit back. Since #967 there is exactly **one** implementation of those rules: `api/src/domain/nameTranslations.ts`, configured per entity by a thin wrapper (`domain/nutritionLibrary.ts` for a Nutrition Library food, `domain/exerciseTranslations.ts` for an exercise) — a new translated column adds a wrapper and a junction table, never a second `COALESCE`, a second validator or a second search clause. Three of its rules are load-bearing. The stored map (`translations`) is projected on **every** read of the entity, list reads included, because `⋮ → Edit` seeds its form from the row the page already holds (#800) and a form seeded without it submits an empty replace-all set and clears them on the first save. A write that **omits** the field leaves the stored rows alone while an explicit `{}` clears them — the distinction every replace-all section `PUT` draws, and the only thing standing between a pre-#967 client and a gym's translations. And a `?q=` matches the base name **or any** stored translation rather than only the locale on screen (a gym searching `Press de Banca` means that exercise whatever language its screen is in), with a client-side picker filtering on the same three things (`apps/admin/src/lib/exerciseNames.ts`). A **read-only consumer** surface is the one place the localized value takes the field's own name (`item_name`, `exercise_name`), since it has no form to overwrite anything. A copy copies them (Duplicate, Clone, `POST /exercises/import`); a **re-import** does not, because it exists to restore System media and must never overwrite a translation the gym corrected. In the UI the language list is the API's (`GET /exercises/locales`, `GET /platform/exercises/lookups`, `GET /platform/nutrition-library/locales`) and never a list in a component, and `apps/admin/src/lib/localeLabels.ts` is the one place a locale gets a *label*, resolved before `t()` is called. See "Translated Catalog Content" in `docs/feature-patterns.md`.
- A **Member's default language** is `members.preferred_locale` (#1039, migration 220) and it is the one stored locale in the product: nothing else persists a language, and no second list of languages exists. Which locales *exist* stays `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` (`api/src/infra/locale.ts`, env-configured, parsed at boot), which is why the column carries **no CHECK** — a deployment adding `fr` would store a locale the application accepts and the database refuses — and why `parseMemberPreferredLocaleInput()` (`api/src/domain/memberPreferredLocale.ts`) is the only place a request's value is judged: it reuses `normalizeLocale()`, so what is stored is the allowlist's own string and `es-ES` folds onto `es`. `NULL` means **no preference** and is what every member written before the migration means — the per-request resolution (`x-locale`, then `Accept-Language`, then the base locale) applies unchanged, so there is no backfill, no seeded default and no stored copy of today's default. Its three answers are every partial write's: a field the request does not *mention* keeps the stored value, an explicit `null`/`''` **clears** it, and anything else is a 400 rather than a coercion — which is why `PATCH /me/profile` writes `preferred_locale = IF(?, ?, preferred_locale)` keyed on whether the field was supplied (a `COALESCE` on the value makes clearing unpersistable, the `products.mandatory` rule), and why both of its reads narrow the column through `toMemberPreferredLocale()` rather than reporting a locale the deployment no longer configures. In the Members App the preference is **applied in exactly one place**: `apps/member/src/components/MemberLocalePreference.tsx`, mounted once by the locale layout, over the pure `preferredLocalePath()` in `apps/member/src/lib/memberLocale.ts` — which is also the app's one language declaration (`MEMBER_LOCALES`, which `middleware.ts` hands to next-intl rather than keeping a list of its own). A page never switches the locale for itself, because the locale of a page *is* its first URL segment: next-intl resolves the messages from it, `<html lang>` and the Clerk localization follow it, and `apiClient` sends it as `x-locale`, so a second mechanism would make those four disagree. `preferredLocalePath()` answers *stay put* for a member with no preference, for a locale this app has no route segment or messages for, and for a member already on theirs — the case that would otherwise loop — and the component is skipped while a superadmin impersonates, since the preference is the member's and the staff user chose their own locale in the URL. Do not add a second stored locale, a per-gym or per-device language preference, a language list in a component, or a page that redirects on the preference itself.
- **A gym's Time & Localization settings are six columns on `gyms`, and the formatter is the one place they are applied** (#1246 stage 1, migration 240). `time_zone`, `first_day_of_week` (0=Sunday, Monday=1), `currency`, `date_format`, `time_format` and `number_format` default to what the product already assumed, so no gym changes until it edits them; there is no CHECK (`ALGORITHM=COPY`) and `api/src/domain/gymLocalization.ts` is the one place the accepted sets and `parseGymLocalizationInput()` live (absent keeps, an unknown value is a 400, never coerced; the zone is checked against the runtime's IANA list). **Currency is display-only and EUR is the only accepted value** — the payment provider boundary is untouched. `GET`/`PUT /system/localization` (SYSTEM module) are the only API, and the combined "Date & Time Format" is derived from `date_format` + `time_format`, never stored. `apps/admin/src/lib/gymFormat.ts` and its identical copy in the Members App are the one place a date, time or amount is formatted from the settings (a unit test fails if the copies drift). Stage 2 (Calendar, #1246): the admin Calendar takes `firstDay`, the 12h/24h `slotLabelFormat`/`eventTimeFormat` and its panels' dates from `useGymFormatSettings()`; the grid itself stays in the browser's zone because FullCalendar's named-zone support needs a plugin (`@fullcalendar/luxon3`) the app does not ship, so the gym time zone is applied to the panels' text only. Stage 3 (partial): audit timestamps on Spaces, Workout Templates and Activity Types go through `formatGymDateTime()` with `useGymFormatSettings()` (now one request per gym, cached), pinned by `apps/admin/src/test/gym-format-sweep.test.ts`; the remaining ad-hoc `toLocale*String`/`Intl.*Format` sites (date-only values, locale month names, the Members App) are still to convert. Do not add a second formatter, a stored combined format, or a currency that changes what is charged.
- **A Member's Access Rights are informational, and only the staff decision is stored** (#1238, migration 243). The Member header shows three independent statuses — Membership Plan Status (`enrollment_status`, #809), Payment Status (#1235) and Access Rights — and Clerk status stays in the Account section. `members.access_rights` stores `granted` (default) or `revoked` and nothing else; `to_be_reviewed` is **derived on read** by `deriveAccessRights()` (`api/src/domain/memberAccessRights.ts`) from a stored `granted` plus a `failed`/`expired` worst Payment Status across every billable concept, so it returns to `granted` by itself when the payment is settled and no settle path needs a hook. `revoked` is never changed by payments, and cancelling a plan never changes access. `POST /members/:id/access/grant|revoke` (MEMBERS write, audited) persist the state and touch no membership or payment row; **nothing is gated by it** (not booking, not login). Not done: a separate persisted Cancel Date (`closed_at` already records when a cancellation was applied; the paid-through End Date is #1196's).
